Topic 1 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

1. Ischemic Heart Disease

🔥 High Yield (10×) 21 Qs

1. Acute Coronary Syndrome (ACS) Spectrum 🔥 x16

Syndrome Pain Character & Triggers ECG Pattern Cardiac Troponin First-Line / Definitive Action
Stable Angina x1 Exertional retrosternal tightness; relieved by rest or sublingual Nitrates (<15 min) Normal at rest; transient ST depression on exercise stress test Normal Coronary angiography if stress test is positive
Unstable Angina x2 Crescendo angina, rest angina (>20 min), or new-onset severe angina Normal or transient ST depression / T-wave inversion Normal Admit to hospital + LMWH + DAPT (Exercise stress test is strictly contraindicated)
NSTEMI x3 Prolonged crushing rest pain (>20 min), diaphoresis, nausea Persistent ST depression / deep T-wave inversion (no persistent ST elevation) Elevated Admit + anticoagulation + early Cardiac catheterization / angiography
STEMI 🔥 x10 Severe crushing retrosternal pain radiating to left arm/jaw, unrelieved by Nitrates Persistent ST elevation in contiguous leads or new LBBB Elevated Immediate Primary PCI (within 120 min); IV Thrombolytics if PCI unavailable
Prinzmetal (Vasospastic) Angina x1 Recurrent episodic chest pain at rest (often midnight/early morning) in young smokers Transient ST elevation that promptly resolves after episode Normal First-line therapy: Calcium-Channel Blocker (e.g., Diltiazem/Amlodipine) ± Nitrates

2. STEMI Localization & Culprit Artery Anatomy 🔥 x24

Feature Anterior / Anteroseptal STEMI 🔥 x11 Inferior STEMI x6 Lateral STEMI x7
Diagnostic Leads (ST Elevation) V1–V4 (Anteroseptal) / V1–V6 (Extensive Anterior) II, III, aVF I, aVL, V5, V6
Reciprocal ST Depression Leads II, III, aVF Leads I, aVL Leads II, III, aVF
Culprit Coronary Artery Left Anterior Descending (LAD) Right Coronary Artery (RCA) (85%) / LCx (15%) Left Circumflex (LCx)
Classic Hemodynamic Complication Acute LV dysfunction, Pulmonary edema, Cardiogenic shock Vagal hypertonia → Sinus bradycardia & AV nodal block Lateral LV free-wall dysfunction

3. Revascularization Strategy, Postoperative MI & CCU Care 🔥 x12

  • Acute Chest Pain ED Triage Protocol:
    • For any acute substernal chest pain (including in pregnancy), the initial diagnostic test is an immediate 12-lead ECG. 🔥 x6
    • If initial ECG is non-diagnostic after ≥4 hours of persistent or recurrent pain, order serum cardiac Troponin. x2
  • Revascularization Modalities:
    • Primary PCI vs. Thrombolysis: Primary PCI is the gold standard for acute STEMI if accessible within 120 minutes. If transfer time exceeds 120 minutes, administer IV thrombolytics immediately. x1
    • Failed Thrombolysis: Persistent chest pain and hemodynamic compromise (BP 80/60) 1 hour post-fibrinolysis mandates immediate Rescue PCI. x2
    • Severe Multivessel Disease / Left Main Stenosis: Significant stenosis (≥50%, e.g., 80%) of the Left Main Coronary Artery confers proven long-term survival benefit with Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting (CABG). x1
    • Refractory Angina Post-MI: Anginal recurrence despite medical therapy requires referral for Coronary intervention / angiography. x1
  • Postoperative Myocardial Infarction: On postoperative days (e.g., Day 2 post-abdominoperineal resection), crushing retrosternal pain requires an immediate 12-lead ECG + serum cardiac Troponins. 🔥 x4
  • CCU Glycemic Control Post-STEMI: In Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus with acute MI, discontinue oral Metformin and initiate an IV insulin infusion. x1

Topic 2 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

2. Heart Failure

🔥 High Yield (8×) 22 Qs

1. Heart Failure Phenotypes & Diagnostic Hallmarks 🔥 x19

Phenotype Key Clinical Presentation Echocardiographic & Hemodynamic Hallmarks
HFrEF (Systolic HF) x3 Exertional dyspnea, Orthopnea, Paroxysmal Nocturnal Dyspnea (PND — most specific symptom) x2, S3 gallop, bilateral crackles LVEF ≤40% + Left ventricular dilatation / global hypokinesia; elevated BNP / NT-proBNP
HFpEF (Diastolic HF) x2 Longstanding Hypertension / Type 2 Diabetes + exertional dyspnea, elevated JVP, basilar crackles Preserved LVEF (≥50%) + LV hypertrophy, elevated filling pressures, and diastolic dysfunction
Biventricular HF x1 Concurrent pulmonary congestion (PND, orthopnea, crackles) + systemic venous congestion (JVD, hepatomegaly, bilateral pedal edema) Combined Left and Right ventricular systolic/diastolic impairment; cardiomegaly with pleural effusions
Cardiac Edema Differential 🔥 x11 Distinguished from hepatic cirrhosis or nephrotic syndrome by Elevated Jugular Venous Pressure (JVP) Normal serum albumin (3.6 g/dL) and normal serum bilirubin (1.0 mg/dL) rule out primary hepatic failure

2. Acute Decompensated HF (ADHF) & Cardiogenic Shock 🔥 x19

Feature "Warm & Wet" Pulmonary Edema 🔥 x17 "Cold & Wet" Low-Output HF x1 Cardiogenic Shock x1
Perfusion & Blood Pressure Preserved / High BP (e.g., 165/95 mmHg); warm, well-perfused extremities Hypotension (e.g., BP 90/60 mmHg) + cold, clammy extremities Profound shock (e.g., BP 82/58 mmHg), severe hypoperfusion, oliguria (<10 mL/h)
Pulmonary & Hemodynamics "Cardiac asthma" wheezes, pink frothy sputum, S3 gallop; CXR shows cephalization & alveolar edema Bilateral crackles, JVD, arterial hypoxemia (SpO2 85%), marked LVEF reduction (30%) Markedly elevated PCWP (>18 mmHg, e.g., 23 mmHg) + severely depressed Cardiac Index (CI <2.2)
First-Line Pharmacotherapy IV Furosemide + IV Nitroglycerin (vasodilator reduces preload and afterload) IV Furosemide + Inotropic Agent (e.g., Dobutamine) to restore perfusion Vasopressor (Norepinephrine) ± Inotrope + urgent mechanical circulatory support / revascularization

3. Chronic Guideline-Directed Medical Therapy (GDMT) & Contraindications 🔥 x14

  • Diagnostic Biomarker & Imaging:
    • BNP / NT-proBNP: Primary biochemical marker of ventricular wall stress, diagnosis, and prognostic severity. x2
    • Chest X-ray (CXR): First-line bedside imaging test for acute pulmonary edema (cardiomegaly, Kerley B lines, pleural effusion). 🔥 x4
  • Mortality-Reducing Regimen in HFrEF:
    • Cornerstone survival therapy: ACE Inhibitor (or ARB/ARNI) + Beta-Blocker (Metoprolol succinate, Bisoprolol, or Carvedilol). x3
    • Add Loop Diuretic (IV/Oral Furosemide) for symptomatic congestion and fluid overload. x3
    • Medication review in elderly HF: Sudden hypotension or dizziness after starting a new drug represents a side effect of new medication. x1
  • Dietary & Fluid Modification: Patient decompensation triggered by excess fluid intake mandates strict fluid restriction and dietary sodium restriction. 🔥 x4
  • Strict Drug Contraindication: In HFrEF (e.g., LVEF 25%), Thiazolidinediones (Pioglitazone) are strictly contraindicated due to fluid retention and risk of precipitating acute decompensation. x1
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة الامتحانات والـ Model Answer في Heart Failure:

1. علاج Hypertensive Acute Pulmonary Edema (سؤال Q5):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: مريضة تعاني من احتقان رئوي حاد وارتفاع شديد في ضغط الدم (BP 165/95) مع Crackles و S3 Gallop، العلاج الدوائي المعتمد كخط أول هو IV Furosemide + IV Nitroglycerin لتقليل الـ Preload والـ Afterload وتصريف السوائل.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار خطأً Epinephrine + Labetalol (الخيار D)، وهو اختيار غير سليم طبياً لأن الـ Epinephrine سيزيد من ضربات القلب والـ Afterload والعبء على عضلة القلب المتعبة.

2. تشخيص مريضة الـ Biventricular Failure المدخنة (سؤال Q779):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: مريضة مدخنة تعاني من ضيق تنفس مع احتقان وريدي عنقي (JVD)، وسماع S3 Gallop، ووجود Bilateral basal crackles، وتورم بالساقين، مع Cardiomegaly و Pleural effusions بالأشعة، التشخيص الطبي السليم هو Heart Failure (الخيار A) (وهي نفس الحالة تماماً المكررة في Q785 وعلاجها IV Furosemide).
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في Q779 اختار COPD (الخيار D) متجاهلاً علامات فشل القلب الاحتقاني الواضحة وتضخم القلب.

Topic 3 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

3. Cardiac Arrhythmias

🔥 High Yield (11×) 18 Qs

1. Tachyarrhythmias: Management by Hemodynamic Stability 🔥 x18

Arrhythmia ECG Pattern & Hemodynamic State First-Line Management Protocol
Unstable Tachyarrhythmias (AF, VT, SVT) 🔥 x5 Narrow or wide QRS tachycardia accompanied by shock / hypotension (e.g., BP 80/50 mmHg or 60/40 mmHg), cold extremities, chest pain, or altered mental status Immediate Synchronized DC Cardioversion (Shock synchronized with R wave to prevent VF)
Stable Regular Narrow-Complex (PSVT) 🔥 x6 Regular narrow QRS complexes without visible P waves, rate ~150 bpm; hemodynamically stable (BP 125/75 mmHg) Step 1: Non-pharmacological Vagal maneuvers (Carotid sinus massage / Valsalva)
Step 2: IV Adenosine (rapid bolus) if vagal maneuvers fail
Stable Atrial Fibrillation with RVR 🔥 x7 Irregularly irregular rhythm, absent discrete P waves, fibrillatory baseline, rate ~130 bpm; hemodynamically stable Rate control with cardioselective Beta-blocker (e.g., Metoprolol) or Non-DHP CCB (Diltiazem) + Oral Anticoagulation based on stroke risk

2. AF Anticoagulation, Bradycardia, Toxicity & Resuscitation 🔥 x25

  • Atrial Fibrillation Anticoagulation & Stroke Timing:
    • Stroke Prevention: For AF with high thromboembolic risk (CHA2DS2-VASc ≥2 in men, ≥3 in women), initiate Oral Anticoagulation with Warfarin (target INR 2.0–3.0) or a DOAC. x3
    • Post-Ischemic Stroke Anticoagulation Timing: In a patient with AF presenting with acute large ischemic stroke (hemiparesis), give Aspirin acutely; delay starting Warfarin or DOAC until after 14 days to prevent intracranial hemorrhagic transformation. 🔥 x6
  • Post-MI Symptomatic Sinus Bradycardia: Acute inferior/anterior MI complicated by profound sinus bradycardia (HR 40 bpm) with hypotension (BP 86/52 mmHg) requires immediate IV Atropine sulfate (0.5–0.6 mg). 🔥 x10
  • Chronic Amiodarone Toxicity: Long-term use of Amiodarone can cause fatal pulmonary toxicity presenting as chronic dry cough, exertional dyspnea, and bilateral interstitial infiltrates on CXR = Pulmonary Fibrosis. x2
  • Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation (CPR Protocol):
    • During adult BLS / CPR, immediately after delivering 2 rescue breaths with a bag-valve mask, return hands without delay and give 30 chest compressions (compression-to-ventilation ratio 30:2). x1
    • Breaking Bad News: Following prolonged unsuccessful CPR (>45 min), the resuscitation team leader meets with the relatives in a private relatives' room to break the news empathetically. x3
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة Arrhythmias والـ Model Answer:

1. الخطوة الأولى في علاج Stable PSVT (سؤال Q29):
• التسلسل الطبي المعتمد (AI Answer): في حالات تسارع القلب المنتظم ضيق المركب (Narrow-Complex Tachycardia) المستقرة ديناميكياً، نبدأ أولاً بالوسائل غير الدوائية Vagal maneuvers (مثل Valsalva maneuver أو Carotid sinus massage)، وإذا لم تستجب الحالة ننتقل فوراً للعلاج الدوائي الأول وهو IV Adenosine.
• الـ Model Answer: اعتمد رسمياً في الامتحان خيار Carotid sinus massage (الخيار C) كخطوة أولى قبل إعطاء الـ Adenosine.

2. التحكم في معدل النبض في حالات Atrial Fibrillation (سؤال Q72):
• الأساس العلمي (AI Answer): في حالات الـ Stable AF with Rapid Ventricular Response، الخط الأول للتحكم في النبض (Rate control) هو استخدام Cardioselective Beta-Blockers (مثل Metoprolol أو Esmolol) أو Non-DHP CCB (مثل Diltiazem).
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: في سؤال Q72 لعدم وجود Metoprolol أو Diltiazem ضمن الاختيارات، اختار الـ Model Answer عقار Labetalol (الخيار B) باعتباره الـ Beta-blocker المتاح بالسؤال، بينما تشير مراجع أخرى لـ Amiodarone (الخيار C).

Topic 4 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

4. Valvular Heart Disease

⚡ Medium Yield (6×) 4 Qs

1. Mitral Stenosis vs. Aortic Regurgitation Comparison 🔥 x17

Clinical Feature Mitral Stenosis (MS) 🔥 x10 Aortic Regurgitation (AR) x6
Auscultatory Hallmarks Loud S1 + Opening Snap + Low-pitched rumbling Mid-Diastolic murmur heard best at the apex with the bell in the left lateral decubitus position Soft A2 + high-pitched Early Diastolic decrescendo murmur heard best at the left sternal border with the patient sitting forward in full expiration
Pulse & Hemodynamics Small-volume regular/irregular pulse (frequent Atrial Fibrillation); pulmonary hypertension, hemoptysis, malar flush, and tapping apex beat Wide pulse pressure (e.g., BP 120/50 mmHg) + Bounding "Water-Hammer" (Corrigan's) pulse with rapid rise and collapse
Classic Patient Profile Young female presenting with dyspnea, fatigue, and palpitations following past untreated Rheumatic Fever Older adult or Marfan patient with progressive exertional dyspnea, awareness of pounding heartbeat, and nocturnal angina

2. Mechanical Prosthetic Valve Monitoring x1

  • Anticoagulation Monitoring: A patient with a mechanical prosthetic heart valve (e.g., mechanical aortic valve) maintained on oral Warfarin requires routine monitoring with the International Normalized Ratio (INR) (target 2.0–3.0 for mechanical aortic, 2.5–3.5 for mechanical mitral). x1

Topic 5 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

5. Infective Endocarditis

🔥 High Yield (12×) 2 Qs

1. Peripheral Cutaneous Stigmata: Janeway Lesions vs. Osler Nodes x6

Feature Janeway Lesions 🔥 x5 Osler Nodes
Pathophysiology Septic microemboli with microabscess formation Immune-complex deposition with localized dermal vasculitis
Pain & Sensation Characteristically Painless (non-tender) Exquisitely Painful (tender)
Anatomical Location Erythematous or hemorrhagic macules on the palms and soles Tender, raised erythematous nodules on the pulps / pads of fingers and toes

2. Right-Sided Endocarditis & Aminoglycoside Nephrotoxicity x1

  • Right-Sided Endocarditis in IV Drug Users: Intravenous drug abuse classically involves the tricuspid valve (Staphylococcus aureus), presenting with tricuspid regurgitation (pansystolic murmur) and multiple septic pulmonary emboli / cavitary lung infiltrates.
  • Gentamicin-Induced Acute Tubular Necrosis (ATN):
    • During empiric treatment of endocarditis with Ampicillin + Gentamicin, an acute doubling of serum creatinine (e.g., from 1.0 to 2.2 mg/dL) with urinalysis demonstrating "dirty brown" / "muddy brown" granular casts establishes aminoglycoside nephrotoxicity.
    • Immediate Action: Stop Gentamicin immediately and adjust renal dosing of remaining antimicrobials. x1
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 6 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

6. Cardiomyopathies

🔥 High Yield (9×) 5 Qs

1. Dilated Cardiomyopathy (DCM) vs. HOCM 🔥 x27

Feature Dilated Cardiomyopathy (DCM) 🔥 x12 Hypertrophic Cardiomyopathy (HOCM) 🔥 x15
Patient & Trigger Young child (e.g., 4-year-old) presenting 6–8 weeks after a viral URTI (post-viral myocarditis) Young athlete collapsing and suffering sudden cardiac death during intense physical exertion
Physical Examination Hepatomegaly, bilateral pedal edema, and muffled heart sounds WITHOUT audible murmurs Harsh mid-systolic ejection murmur loudest between apex and left lower sternal border (LLSB)
Diagnostic Findings Chest X-ray shows marked generalized cardiomegaly with biventricular dilatation and low ejection fraction ECG shows Left Ventricular Hypertrophy (LVH) + deep septal Q waves; confirmed definitively by Echocardiogram
Major Sudden Death Risk Factors Severely depressed LVEF (<30%) with intractable ventricular arrhythmias Family history of premature sudden death in a first-degree relative & Extreme LV wall thickness (≥30 mm)

2. SCD Risk Stratification in HOCM & Extraintestinal IBD Exclusion x3

  • Extraintestinal Manifestations of IBD Exclusion: Inflammatory Bowel Disease is associated with peripheral arthritis, erythema nodosum, pyoderma gangrenosum, uveitis, aphthous stomatitis, and primary sclerosing cholangitis. Cardiomyopathy is NOT an extraintestinal manifestation of IBD. x3
  • Pre-Participation Athletic Screening: A 16-year-old athlete with ECG showing LVH and deep septal Q waves requires an Echocardiogram prior to athletic clearance. x1
⚠️ تنبيه هام لاختلاف مفتاح الامتحان (Model Answer) في سؤال HOCM Sudden Death (سؤال Q69):

• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: في حالات الـ Hypertrophic Obstructive Cardiomyopathy (HOCM)، أهم عوامل الخطورة لحدوث Sudden Cardiac Death التي تستدعي تركيب منظم نبضات ومزيل رجفان (ICD) هي: وجود تاريخ عائلي لوفاة مفاجئة مبكرة Family history of premature sudden cardiac death في قريب درجة أولى، ووجود تضخم شديد جداً في جدار البطين Extreme LV hypertrophy (سمك الجدار ≥ 30 mm)، بالإضافة إلى Unexplained syncope و Non-sustained VT.
• الملاحظة الإكلينيكية والـ Model Answer: في التدريس السريري وامتحانات الـ MRCP يُعد الـ Family history هو الخيار الأكثر كلاسيكية وأهمية، ولكن الـ Model Answer في مفتاح هذا السؤال اختار Degree of left ventricular hypertrophy (الخيار D). وكلا العاملين معتمدان طبياً ضمن إرشادات تقييم مخاطر الـ ICD.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 7 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

7. Congenital Heart Disease

⚡ Medium Yield (5×) 4 Qs

1. Acyanotic Congenital Heart Defects Comparison 🔥 x21

Feature Ventricular Septal Defect (VSD) x2 Isolated Atrial Septal Defect (ASD) 🔥 x4 Patent Ductus Arteriosus (PDA) 🔥 x15
Classic Murmur & Heart Sounds Harsh, loud pansystolic murmur heard best at the left lower sternal border (LLSB) Wide, fixed splitting of S2 + ejection systolic flow murmur at the 2nd left intercostal space (pulmonic area) Continuous "machinery" murmur heard loudest in the left infraclavicular area and upper left sternal border
Hemodynamic & Exam Hallmarks Left-to-right ventricular shunt; most common congenital heart defect overall Right ventricular volume overload with delayed pulmonic valve closure unaffected by respiration; increased pulmonary vascular markings on CXR Persistent patency between descending aorta and pulmonary artery; bounding peripheral pulses with wide pulse pressure
Typical Clinical Scenario Infant with murmur, failure to thrive, or asymptomatic child discovered on routine examination Asymptomatic school-aged child or teenager discovered incidentally with prominent left precordium and fixed split S2 3-month-old infant with bounding pulses and continuous precordial machinery murmur
⚠️ تنبيه هام لاختلاف مفتاح الامتحان (Model Answer) في تشخيص الـ ASD (سؤال Q23):

• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: فتى يبلغ 16 عاماً لا يعاني من أي أعراض وتم اكتشافه بالصدفة في فحص مدرسي مع وجود Prominent left precordium و Fixed splitting of S2 و Ejection systolic murmur في الـ Pulmonic area؛ هذا الوصف هو الصورة الكلاسيكية النموذجية لـ Isolated ASD (الخيار D)، حيث يؤدي تدفق الدم الزائد عبر الصمام الرئوي إلى تأخر إغلاقه دون تأثر بالتنفس.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار ASD with pulmonary hypertension (الخيار A)، وهو غير دقيق علمياً لأن وجود ارتفاع ضغط الشريان الرئوي يصاحبه صوت P2 عالٍ جداً (Loud P2) وتدهور ملحوظ في المجهود مع انعكاس مسار الـ Shunt إلى متلازمة Eisenmenger.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 8 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

8. Hypertension

🔥 High Yield (8×) 11 Qs

1. Hypertension Staging & Hypertensive Emergency 🔥 x14

Feature Stage 1 Hypertension x6 Stage 2 / Grade 2 Hypertension x6 Hypertensive Emergency x2
Blood Pressure Range 130–139 / 80–89 mmHg (e.g., 135/85 mmHg) uncomplicated ≥140 / ≥90 mmHg (e.g., 155/92 to 160/100 mmHg) confirmed on repeat visits Severe elevation >180 / >120 mmHg (e.g., 220/120+ mmHg) + acute target-organ damage
Target Organ Involvement None (asymptomatic, low cardiovascular risk profile) May show early LVH or microvascular changes Acute hypertensive encephalopathy, papilledema, intracranial hemorrhage, acute pulmonary edema
First-Line Management Lifestyle modification / improvements (DASH diet, sodium reduction, physical exercise, weight loss) Prompt initiation of antihypertensive pharmacotherapy + lifestyle improvements Immediate admission to ICU + IV Nicardipine or IV Sodium Nitroprusside (gradual BP lowering ≤25% in 1st hour)

2. Situational Syncope, Supine Hypotension & Intracerebral Hemorrhage 🔥 x16

  • Vasovagal / Situational Syncope:
    • Clinical Triggers: Straining to void during nocturnal micturition in elderly men with BPH (micturition syncope 🔥 x4) or severe emotional distress / observing acute trauma and open fractures (🔥 x5).
    • Hallmarks: Clear autonomic prodrome (diaphoresis, pallor, nausea, lightheadedness, tunnel vision) followed by brief loss of consciousness (~10–15 seconds) and rapid, complete spontaneous recovery upon assuming the supine position (no post-ictal confusion).
  • Supine Hypotensive Syndrome in Late Pregnancy:
    • In women in advanced pregnancy or labor (term primigravida), lying supine causes the heavy gravid uterus to compress the Inferior Vena Cava (IVC), severely impeding venous return to the heart. x6
    • Presentation & Action: Maternal hypotension, dizziness, and fetal bradycardia; managed immediately by placing the patient in the left lateral tilt position.
  • Hypertensive Intracerebral Hemorrhage: Longstanding poorly controlled hypertension causes rupture of Charcot-Bouchard microaneurysms, presenting with sudden coma, hemiplegia, and acute intraparenchymal hemorrhage on non-contrast head CT. x1
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 9 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

9. Dyslipidemia

⚡ Medium Yield (4×) 6 Qs

1. Cardiovascular Risk Stratification & Primary Prevention 🔥 x11

  • Lowest Cardiovascular Disease (CAD) Risk Profile:
    • In evaluating routine serum lipid profiles, cardiovascular risk is lowest in profiles demonstrating the highest HDL cholesterol combined with the lowest Total Cholesterol to HDL ratio (e.g., Total Cholesterol 202 mg/dL with HDL 45 mg/dL confers significantly lower risk than a lower total cholesterol with very low HDL). x2
  • Primary Prevention Statin in Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus:
    • In any adult patient aged 40–75 with Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus and LDL ≥70 mg/dL (e.g., LDL 150 mg/dL), initiate a moderate-to-high intensity Statin (Atorvastatin) regardless of calculated 10-year ASCVD risk. x3
  • Secondary Hypercholesterolemia in Overt Hypothyroidism:
    • In a patient presenting with persistent hypercholesterolemia and elevated TSH (e.g., TSH 18 mIU/L), the required first step is to treat hypothyroidism first with Levothyroxine. Hypothyroidism downregulates hepatic LDL receptors; correcting thyroid function often normalizes lipid levels before committing the patient to lifelong statin therapy. x2
  • Post-MI Pharmacotherapy Causing Weight Gain:
    • A 60-year-old post-MI diabetic patient who gains significant weight (5 kg) following therapy initiation is receiving a sulfonylurea: Gliclazide (stimulates pancreatic insulin secretion and promotes weight gain, whereas Metformin is weight-neutral, and SGLT2 inhibitors/GLP-1 agonists cause weight loss). x3

2. Diabetic Dyslipidemia Profile x1

Diabetic Dyslipidemia Pattern (Q82): In poorly controlled Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus (blood glucose >200 mg/dL, hypertension, peripheral neuropathy), insulin resistance induces hepatic overproduction of VLDL, impaired clearance of chylomicrons, and generation of small, dense LDL particles. The fasting lipid panel classically demonstrates:

  • High Total Cholesterol, High LDL, and High Triglycerides (Option E, Model Answer).
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 10 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

10. Aortic Diseases

⚡ Medium Yield (3×) 7 Qs

1. Acute Aortic Dissection Classification & Clinical Hallmarks 🔥 x10

Feature Acute Aortic Dissection (General) 🔥 x5 Stanford Type B Dissection x3 Ruptured AAA x2
Pain Character & Site Abrupt, catastrophic tearing or ripping retrosternal pain radiating straight through to the interscapular back Originates in descending thoracic aorta distal to the left subclavian artery; sharp severe back pain Sudden, agonizing epigastric, lumbar, or flank pain in an older male smoker with sudden syncope / collapse
Diagnostic Physical Signs Unequal pulses bilaterally (pulse deficit >20 mmHg difference between arms) or asymmetric carotid/femoral pulses Blood pressure often elevated; LEAST consistent with a new early diastolic murmur (aortic regurgitation murmur indicates Type A) Profound shock (BP 80/50 mmHg) + rigid, tender abdomen + pulsatile expansile abdominal mass
Management Approach Immediate IV Beta-blocker (Labetalol) to reduce shear stress (target SBP 100–120 mmHg) + emergency CTA Medical therapy with IV Beta-blockers (target SBP 100–120 mmHg); TEVAR if complicated by malperfusion Immediate fluid resuscitation + Emergency Open Surgical Repair (laparotomy with vascular clamping and graft)

2. Abdominal Aortic Aneurysm (AAA): Screening vs. Rupture Management 🔥 x18

  • Stable Pulsatile Abdominal Mass Screening:
    • In a hemodynamically stable older hypertensive male presenting with abdominal pain and a pulsatile infra-umbilical mass, the initial screening and confirmatory imaging modality is Abdominal Ultrasound (US). 🔥 x10
  • Ruptured Abdominal Aortic Aneurysm (Emergency Laparotomy):
    • A previously fit 68–70-year-old man who collapses suddenly with severe abdominal/back pain, profound hypotension (shock), and a tender pulsatile mass has a ruptured AAA.
    • Definitive Immediate Action: Transfer immediately to the operating theatre for Emergency Open Surgical Repair without delaying for non-essential imaging. 🔥 x8
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 11 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

11. Peripheral Arterial Disease

⚡ Medium Yield (3×) 16 Qs

1. Chronic Lower-Extremity Peripheral Arterial Disease (PAD) 🔥 x34

PAD Stage Clinical Presentation & ABI Prognosis & First-Line Action
Intermittent Claudication x3 Cramping calf/thigh pain provoked by walking a predictable distance, promptly relieved within 5 minutes of standing rest; Ankle-Brachial Index (ABI) 0.5–0.8 First-line conservative therapy: Supervised exercise / walking program + smoking cessation + Statin + Antiplatelet
Critical Limb Ischemia (CLI) 🔥 x14 Severe burning rest and night foot pain (worse in bed when horizontal, relieved by dangling the leg over the bedside); ABI <0.4 Night/rest pain carries the worst limb prognosis with high imminent risk of amputation; requires urgent revascularization
Viable Acute-on-Chronic Ischemia 🔥 x4 Cold, dusky right foot with absent distal pulses, but motor power and sensory perception remain completely intact Urgent Peripheral arterial angiography to precisely define arterial tree anatomy prior to planned revascularization
Acute Arterial Embolism 🔥 x13 Sudden catastrophic pain, coldness, and pallor in a leg 6 weeks post-MI or in Atrial Fibrillation; manifests with The 6 Ps Immediate IV unfractionated heparin infusion to halt clot propagation, followed by urgent surgical embolectomy

2. Acute Limb Ischemia (ALI), Carotid Stenosis & Mesenteric Angina 🔥 x20

  • Acute Limb Ischemia (The 6 Ps):
    • Acute cardioembolic arterial occlusion (femoro-popliteal embolus) presents with Pain, Pallor, Pulselessness, Paresthesia, Poikilothermia (coldness), and Paralysis. 🔥 x8
    • Immediate First Step: Initiation of an IV Unfractionated Heparin infusion (± dicumarol/warfarin) to prevent secondary thrombus propagation, followed by urgent surgical Fogarty catheter embolectomy. x3
  • Extracranial Carotid Artery Disease:
    • Diagnostic Screening: Transient ischemic attacks (TIA, amaurosis fugax) or a carotid bruit requires initial evaluation with Carotid Duplex Ultrasonography. x2
    • Medical Antiplatelet Therapy: For symptomatic carotid artery stenosis <50% (e.g., 30%), the optimal management is medical therapy with Aspirin ± Clopidogrel + high-intensity Statin (Carotid Endarterectomy is reserved for stenosis ≥70% or selected 50–69%). 🔥 x7
  • Chronic Mesenteric Ischemia ("Intestinal Angina"):
    • Elderly atherosclerotic patient presenting with severe postprandial cramping abdominal pain occurring 30–60 minutes after meals and fear of eating leading to profound weight loss.
    • Definitive Investigation: CT Angiography (CTA) of the Abdomen to visualize stenosis in the celiac axis and superior mesenteric artery (SMA). x2
  • Primary Lymphedema Praecox: Chronic, progressive, unilateral non-pitting edema of the lower leg beginning around puberty (age 15–25) with recurrent secondary bacterial cellulitis represents Lymphedema praecox (congenital lymphatic hypoplasia). 🔥 x5
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 12 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

12. Deep Vein Thrombosis

⚡ Medium Yield (2×) 3 Qs

1. Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT): Clinical Presentation & Prophylaxis 🔥 x26

  • Clinical Hallmarks & High-Risk Triggers:
    • Acute unilateral lower extremity swelling (>3 cm calf circumference asymmetry), dull calf ache, localized calf warmth, erythema, and calf tenderness with dorsiflexion of the foot (positive Homans sign).
    • Classic Exam Scenarios:
      • 52-year-old female presenting 2 days after returning from a long-haul flight (>10–14 hours) = Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT). 🔥 x22
      • 65-year-old male on postoperative day 5 following an anterior resection for rectal carcinoma presenting with unilateral left calf swelling = Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT). x2
  • Perioperative Thromboprophylaxis in High-Risk Surgery:
    • In patients undergoing major abdominal or pelvic surgery for colorectal malignancy, the most effective evidence-based method of DVT prophylaxis is Low-dose subcutaneous Unfractionated Heparin (5,000 units SC twice daily / bd) or Low-Molecular-Weight Heparin (LMWH) combined with mechanical compression devices. 🔥 x4
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 13 of 13 • Internal Medicine • Cardiology

13. Perioperative & Vascular Care

⚡ Medium Yield (2×) 9 Qs

1. Perioperative Anticoagulation & Antiplatelet Management 🔥 x32

Clinical Feature Perioperative Warfarin 🔥 x8 Perioperative Clopidogrel x3
Elective Surgery Discontinuation Stop 5 days before elective surgery to allow INR to normalize (≤1.5) by the day of the procedure Stop 7 days before elective surgery (minimum 5–7 days) to allow recovery of uninhibited platelet function
Tested Surgical Scenarios • Elective knee replacement in non-valvular AF (Stop 5 days before, no bridging)
• Elective open inguinal hernia repair (Stop 5 days before)
• Elective thyroidectomy (Stop 5 days before; crucial to prevent compressing neck hematoma) 		• Elective open inguinal hernia repair in a patient with a coronary stent placed 18 months previously (Stop 7 days before elective operation)

2. Surgical Critical Care & Organ Support in High-Risk Laparotomy 🔥 x4

  • High-Risk Post-Laparotomy Patient Triage (Q11):
    • A 61-year-old patient with pre-existing left ventricular failure and chronic renal impairment (elevated urea and creatinine) undergoing emergency laparotomy for perforated diverticulum with fecal peritonitis who experiences intraoperative hemodynamic instability and supraventricular tachycardia (SVT).
    • Postoperative Action: Immediate admission to the Intensive Care Unit (ICU) for invasive hemodynamic monitoring, inotropic and ventilatory support, and specialized critical care management. 🔥 x4

3. FAST Ultrasound Exam: Diagnostic Scope & Technical Limitations x3

FAST Exam (Focused Assessment with Sonography for Trauma): Bedside ultrasound screening designed exclusively to detect free pathological fluid (blood) within the peritoneal, pleural, and pericardial spaces in acute trauma patients.

  • Factors That Physically Degrade FAST Sensitivity & Specificity: Physical and anatomical barriers that obscure ultrasound wave penetration degrade its accuracy — including pelvic fractures (disrupt tissue planes and produce pelvic hematomas), lumbar spine fractures, retroperitoneal injury (such as traumatic aortic rupture), abdominal wall subcutaneous emphysema, severe obesity, and bowel gas.
  • The Exception (Physical Sign vs. Technical Accuracy): A physical examination finding such as Abdominal tenderness on examination is a clinical sign and does NOT reduce the physical ultrasound image resolution or technical accuracy of the FAST probe.

4. Early Postoperative Fever & Distributive Shock Evaluation 🔥 x8

Early Postoperative Collapse (Day 1 vs. Day 5–7):

  • 18 Hours Post-Gastrectomy (Early Shock): A 40-year-old male 18 hours after gastrectomy presenting with sudden hypotension (BP 80/60 mmHg), tachycardia (110 bpm), low/normal CVP (+2 cm H2O), and high spiking fever (39°C) has acute distributive shock secondary to Septicemia / severe early systemic sepsis.
  • Surgical Anastomotic Leak Timeline: Anastomotic dehiscence and rupture typically present on postoperative days 5 to 7, when maximal tissue ischemia, mucosal enzymatic breakdown, and mechanical tension occur at the suture line (rarely at 18 hours).

5. Ultrasound Guidance for Central Venous Catheter (CVC) Placement x2

  • Evidence-Based Use of Ultrasound in CVC:
    • Dynamic real-time ultrasound guidance significantly improves the "first-stick" success rate and reduces arterial puncture and hematoma formation in Internal Jugular and Femoral vein cannulation.
    • Ultrasound identification of the target vein prior to cannulation is demonstrably more effective than landmark guidance alone.
    • Subclavian CVC Access: Ultrasound guidance (supraclavicular or infraclavicular approach) HAS a well-established and effective role in subclavian CVC placement; stating that ultrasound has no role in subclavian access is factually FALSE. x2

6. Prehospital Damage Control Resuscitation in Penetrating Trauma 🔥 x4

Permissive Hypotension Principles:

  • In prehospital management of uncontrolled penetrating torso trauma (e.g., abdominal gunshot wound with profound shock BP 74/32 mmHg and carotid-only pulse), aggressive fluid resuscitation that restores peripheral pulses or normalizes blood pressure elevates vascular pressure, dilutes coagulation factors, and "pops" freshly formed clots, causing catastrophic re-bleeding before surgical hemostasis.
  • Evidence-Based Resuscitation Target: Maintenance of a central pulse (carotid or femoral pulse) and cerebrally intact mentation (target SBP ~80–90 mmHg) until definitive operating room surgical control.
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة وخلافات الـ Model Answer في توبيك Perioperative & Vascular Care:

1. سؤال دقة فحص الـ FAST Exam (سؤال Q41):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: فحص الـ FAST يعتمد على الموجات الصوتية لاكتشاف السوائل الحرة، وتتأثر دقته الفنية بالعوامل التي تحجب الصورة مثل كسور الحوض، وكسور الفقرات القطنية، والـ Retroperitoneal injury (مثل Aortic rupture)، والغازات. أما وجود Abdominal tenderness on exam (الخيار A) فهو عرض سريري إكلينيكي ولا يقلل من دقة الجهاز الفنية (Technical accuracy)، وهو الإجابة العلمية الصحيحة.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار Traumatic aortic rupture (الخيار C) كاستثناء.

2. حرارة وهبوط بعد 18 ساعة من استئصال المعدة (سؤال Q49):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: مريض بعد 18 ساعة فقط من عملية Gastrectomy أُصيب بهبوط حاد (BP 80/60) وتسارع في النبض (110) وحرارة مرتفعة جداً (39°C) مع CVP منخفض (+2)، هذا التوقيت المبكر جداً مع الحرارة العالية يمثل صدمة إنتانية حادة Septicemia (الخيار E). تسريب أو انفجار التوصيلة الجراحية (Anastomotic leak) يحدث عادة بين اليوم الخامس والسابع.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار Ruptured anastomosis (الخيار A) بالرغم من أن 18 ساعة توقيت مبكر جداً لانفجار التوصيلة.

3. العبارة الخاطئة في استخدام السونار لتركيب الـ CVC (سؤال Q75):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: السؤال يطلب تحديد العبارة الخاطئة (FALSE). علمياً وطبياً الـ Ultrasound له دور مثبت وموثق في تركيب القسطرة الوريدية المركزية في الوريد تحت الترقوة Subclavian vein. لذلك فإن العبارة التي تدعي أن "Ultrasound has no role in subclavian CVCs" (الخيار E) هي العبارة الخاطئة تماماً علمياً.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان اختار خطأً الخيار B (الذي يقول إن السونار أكثر فاعلية من الطريقة التقليدية).

4. هدف الإنعاش بالسوائل خارج المستشفى في إصابات البطن النافذة (سؤال Q76):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: في حالات الـ Penetrating abdominal trauma المصحوبة بنزيف غير مسيطر عليه وهبوط حاد، يُطبق مبدأ Permissive Hypotension والهدف هو الحفاظ على النبض المركزي فقط Maintenance of central pulse (الخيار D) وتروية الأعضاء الحيوية، لأن رفع الضغط واستعادة النبض الطرفي قبل الجراحة يفك التجلطات ويفاقم النزيف الداخلي.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار Return of peripheral pulses (الخيار C).

Topic 1 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

1. Bronchial Asthma

🔥 High Yield (14×) 15 Qs

1. Diagnostic Hallmarks, Spirometry & Clinical Exclusions 🔥 x11

  • Typical Asthma Presentation & Baseline Spirometry:
    • Variable exertional wheezing, chest tightness, and nocturnal non-productive cough with a completely normal baseline physical exam.
    • Confirmatory Diagnostic Test: Pulmonary Function Test (PFT) / Spirometry demonstrating significant bronchodilator reversibility (increase in FEV1 ≥12% and ≥200 mL) or a positive Methacholine bronchial challenge test. x1
    • Exercise-Induced Bronchoconstriction (EIB): Isolated exertional dyspnea occurring specifically during running with normal resting examination and normal CXR requires Spirometry performed before and after exercise (demonstrating ≥10–15% fall in FEV1). x1
  • Auscultatory Hallmark: Widespread expiratory high-pitched polyphonic wheezes.
  • Clinical Findings That EXCLUDE Uncomplicated Asthma: 🔥 x7
    • Fine Consonating Crepitations (Crackles): Present in bronchiectasis, left ventricular failure, interstitial lung disease, and pneumonia → strictly absent in uncomplicated asthma. x6
    • Permanent Finger Clubbing: Found in suppurative lung disease (bronchiectasis, cystic fibrosis, lung abscess), cyanotic congenital heart disease, and lung cancer → never present in uncomplicated bronchial asthma. x1
  • Pediatric Wheezy Chest Differential Diagnosis: Common recurrent causes in children include Bronchial Asthma (most common), Acute Bronchiolitis (<2 years), Airway Foreign Body Aspiration, and Allergic Bronchopulmonary Aspergillosis (ABPA). x3

2. Stepwise Pharmacotherapy & Acute Severe Exacerbations 🔥 x24

Clinical Scenario Severity Hallmarks & Diagnostic Criteria First-Line Management Protocol
Chronic Maintenance Mainstay 🔥 x4 Persistent daily asthma symptoms, recurrent night waking, or frequent flare-ups Inhaled Corticosteroids (ICS) (e.g., Budesonide or Fluticasone) — cornerstone anti-inflammatory therapy
Step-Up Controller Therapy 🔥 x5 Uncontrolled asthma on low-dose ICS alone despite confirmed inhaler technique Add Long-Acting Beta-2 Agonist (LABA) (e.g., Salmeterol or Formoterol) to the ICS regimen
Acute Mild-to-Moderate Reliever 🔥 x5 Acute wheezing, chest tightness, tachypnea; PEF >50% predicted Inhaled / Nebulized Short-Acting Beta-2 Agonist (SABA — Salbutamol)
Post-Bronchodilator Stabilization 🔥 x4 Following initial bronchodilator response in the emergency room to prevent relapse Course of Oral Prednisolone (systemic corticosteroids for 5–7 days)
Acute Severe Asthma Attack 🔥 x4 Unable to complete sentences in one breath + RR ≥25–30/min + HR ≥110 bpm + PEF 33–50% predicted (SpO2 <92%) Immediate High-flow Oxygen + Repeated Nebulized Salbutamol + Ipratropium Bromide + Systemic Corticosteroids
  • Ominous Signs & Sedation Contraindication in Acute Severe Asthma:
    • In acute severe asthma, hyperventilation normally causes hypocapnia (low PaCO2). A "normalizing" or elevated PaCO2 (≥40 mmHg) despite persistent tachypnea signals imminent respiratory muscle fatigue and impending respiratory arrest.
    • Strict Contraindication: Sedatives and Anxiolytics are strictly contraindicated as they depress central respiratory drive and precipitate respiratory failure. x1
  • Status Asthmaticus on Mechanical Ventilation:
    • Severe bronchospasm and dynamic hyperinflation cause extremely high peak inspiratory airway pressures, carrying high risk of barotrauma: Tension Pneumothorax.
    • Medical Disclosure: A complication such as pneumothorax during invasive ventilation must be disclosed honestly to the patient's family as a recognized complication of mechanical ventilation in status asthmaticus. x2
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة الامتحانات والـ Model Answer في توبيك Bronchial Asthma:

سقوط كلمة الاستثناء في سؤال Wheezy chest in children (سؤال Q131):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: الأسباب الشائعة للـ Wheezing المتكرر في الأطفال تشمل Bronchial Asthma، و Acute Bronchiolitis، و Foreign Body Aspiration، و ABPA. أما الـ Pneumonia فتتميز بوجود علامات ارتشاح وتكثف رئوي (Consolidation و Crackles و Bronchial breathing) وليس أزيزاً عاماً متكرراً.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: في صياغة الامتحان سقطت كلمة EXCEPT من رأس السؤال بالخطأ، ولذلك فإن الـ Model Answer اعتمد خيار Pneumonia (الخيار E) باعتباره الاستثناء المقصود في السؤال.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 2 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

2. COPD & Cor Pulmonale

🔥 High Yield (12×) 14 Qs

1. Obstructive Airway Disease Spectrum Comparison 🔥 x12

Feature COPD (General / Emphysema) 🔥 x9 Chronic Bronchitis x1 Alpha-1 Antitrypsin Deficiency x2
Patient Demographics & History Older adult with long tobacco smoking history (≥20–40 pack-years); progressive exertional dyspnea and chronic morning cough Smoker with persistent productive cough yielding mucoid sputum Young onset (<40–45 years) in a lifelong non-smoker; positive family history of early emphysema or hepatic cirrhosis
Physical Examination Hallmarks Barrel chest, hyperresonance obliterating cardiac and hepatic dullness, distant heart sounds, and prolonged expiration with rhonchi Scattered coarse rhonchi and crackles; "blue bloater" habitus with cyanosis Panacinar (panlobular) emphysema with predominant basilar distribution on chest imaging ± chronic liver disease
Diagnostic Hallmarks & Criteria Post-bronchodilator spirometry showing FEV1/FVC <0.70 (e.g., 0.55) confirming fixed airflow limitation Clinical definition: Productive cough on most days for ≥3 months per year for ≥2–3 consecutive years Fixed obstructive spirometry + severely depressed serum Alpha-1 Antitrypsin level; confirmed by SERPINA1 genotyping

2. Acute Exacerbations, Respiratory Acidosis & Cor Pulmonale 🔥 x39

  • Uncomplicated COPD Exacerbation:
    • Smoker presenting with acute worsening of dyspnea, increased cough, and diffuse wheezing, with normal temperature (afebrile 37°C) and clear CXR without infiltrates = Acute COPD Exacerbation. 🔥 x6
    • First-Line Pharmacotherapy: Systemic Corticosteroids + Short-Acting Bronchodilators (SABA/SAMA). x2
  • Arterial Blood Gas (ABG) & Acute Respiratory Acidosis:
    • Acute Uncompensated Respiratory Acidosis: Characterized by pH <7.35 (e.g., 7.25–7.26) + elevated PaCO2 >45 mmHg (53–55 mmHg) + normal serum bicarbonate (HCO3− 24 mmol/L). 🔥 x16
    • Acute-on-Chronic Hypercapnic Failure: COPD patient with worsening lethargy, drowsiness, and acidosis (pH 7.25, PaCO2 70 mmHg, HCO3− 32 mEq/L): Start controlled oxygen therapy (Venturi mask target SpO2 88–92%) + Non-Invasive Ventilation (NIV / BiPAP). x1
    • O2-Induced CO2 Narcosis: Supplying uncontrolled high-flow oxygen (e.g., 5 L/min nasal cannula or non-rebreather) in chronic hypercapnic COPD blunts the hypoxic ventilatory drive, worsens V/Q mismatch (Haldane effect), and causes acute severe hypercapnia and coma. Immediate Action: Immediate Endotracheal Intubation & Mechanical Ventilation. x3
  • Cor Pulmonale (Right Heart Failure Secondary to Pulmonary Disease):
    • Afebrile COPD patient developing elevated JVP, epigastric right ventricular systolic heave/pulsation, tender hepatomegaly, and bilateral pedal edema without left ventricular dysfunction = Cor Pulmonale. 🔥 x8
  • Postoperative Laparotomy Wound Dehiscence:
    • Sudden burst of serosanguinous ("salmon-pink") discharge from a fresh laparotomy incision is strongly predisposed by pre-existing pulmonary disease (chronic violent coughing increases intra-abdominal pressure, coupled with poor tissue healing from systemic corticosteroids). x6
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 3 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

3. Pneumonia

🔥 High Yield (10×) 26 Qs

1. Pneumonia Etiology, Clinical Patterns & Severity Markers 🔥 x38

Clinical Syndrome Patient Demographics & Triggers Sputum & Physical Signs Causative Pathogen / Action
Typical Lobar CAP 🔥 x12 Previously healthy adult or controlled HIV patient (CD4 400/mm³) High fever, pleuritic chest pain, rust-colored sputum, bronchial breath sounds, dullness, egophony Streptococcus pneumoniae (Gram-positive lancet-shaped diplococci)
Alcoholic Lobar Pneumonia x1 Chronic alcohol dependency, severe malnutrition Sudden prostration, high fever, thick gelatinous "currant-jelly" sputum; upper lobe consolidation with bulging fissure Klebsiella pneumoniae (Gram-negative encapsulated rod)
Aspiration Pneumonia 🔥 x11 Frail elderly patient with swallowing dysfunction / dysphagia, stroke, or impaired consciousness Low-grade fever, foul-smelling sputum, localized to dependent lung segments: Right Lower Lobe (RLL) opacity Oral anaerobes + aerobic streptococci; initiate Aspiration pneumonia coverage (Ampicillin-sulbactam)
Hospital-Acquired Pneumonia (HAP) x2 Patient hospitalized for ≥48 hours (e.g., acute ischemic stroke with nasogastric tube) New onset of high fever, purulent endotracheal secretions, and new progressive pulmonary infiltrate Multidrug-resistant Gram-negative bacilli: Pseudomonas aeruginosa
Postoperative Pneumonia x1 Patient on postoperative day 2–3 following major abdominal surgery (laparotomy) New-onset fever, acute confusion, tachypnea, reduced breath sounds, and localized basal crackles/dullness Postoperative hospital-acquired pneumonia; order immediate Chest X-ray and sputum/blood cultures
Pediatric Basal Pneumonia with Abdominal Pain 🔥 x11 Young child presenting with high fever, cough, and tachypnea Referred abdominal pain (RLQ or LUQ) due to diaphragmatic pleural irritation; auscultation reveals basal crackles Basal chest infection / lobar pneumonia; obtain an immediate Chest X-ray prior to surgical exploration

2. Antibiotic Selection, Severity Criteria & VAP Prevention 🔥 x24

Pneumonia Scenario Severity & Patient Risk Stratification First-Line Antimicrobial Strategy
Uncomplicated Outpatient CAP x1 Previously healthy young adult, penicillin-susceptible, low CURB-65 Oral Amoxicillin (or Doxycycline / Macrolide)
Inpatient Non-ICU / Controlled HIV CAP x3 Hospitalized patient or HIV patient (CD4 400/mm³) with acute lobar consolidation and hypotension Intravenous Ceftriaxone (combined with a Macrolide or Doxycycline)
Severe Hypoxemic CAP 🔥 x4 Elderly patient with lobar consolidation, SpO2 <90% (89%), and tachypnea (RR 28) Hospital admission for Intravenous antibiotic therapy + supplemental oxygen
Penicillin-Resistant S. pneumoniae (PRSP) x2 Bacteremic CAP with blood cultures yielding penicillin-resistant pneumococcus Respiratory Fluoroquinolone: Levofloxacin (or Moxifloxacin)
  • CURB-65 Prognostic Severity Criteria:
    • Aside from age ≥65, a Respiratory Rate ≥30/min (e.g., 34–35/min) represents a major physiologic severity marker mandating urgent hospital admission. x1
  • ICU Ventilator-Associated Pneumonia (VAP) Prevention:
    • The most effective evidence-based endotracheal intervention to prevent VAP in mechanically ventilated ICU patients is the use of Endotracheal tubes equipped with subglottic secretion drainage ports (combined with semi-recumbent 30–45° head-of-bed positioning). x1
  • Acute Bronchitis vs. Severe COVID-19 Pneumonia:
    • Acute Bronchitis: Adult presenting after an upper respiratory prodrome with cough productive of small whitish sputum, scattered wheezes, normal oxygen saturation (SpO2 98%), and a clear chest radiograph without consolidations. Self-limiting viral etiology; treat symptomatically. 🔥 x7
    • Severe COVID-19 Pneumonia: Diabetic/hypertensive patient with worsening dry cough, high fever, dyspnea, and home pulse oximetry showing oxygen saturation 85%. Requires immediate emergency hospital admission for oxygen therapy and dexamethasone. x3
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة وخلافات الـ Model Answer في توبيك Pneumonia:

1. تشخيص الكحة والأزيز في مدخن بالغ (سؤال Q152):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: رجل مدخن يبلغ 52 عاماً يعاني من نزلة برد تلتها كحة مع بلغم أبيض خفيف و Scattered wheezes ونسبة أكسجين طبيعية تماماً (SpO2 98%)، التشخيص الطبي السليم هو Acute Bronchitis (الخيار A). مرض الـ Bronchiolitis هو مرض فيروسي يصيب حصراً الرضع والأطفال دون سن السنتين.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان في هذا السؤال اختار خطأً Bronchiolitis (الخيار D).

2. تدهور رضيع مصاب بالتهاب رئوي بعد 20 ساعة (سؤال Q156):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: رضيع عمره سنة تم تنويمه بسبب Right upper lobar consolidation، وبعد 20 ساعة حدث تدهور حاد مع هبوط في الضغط (Hypotension) وضعف في التروية الطرفية (Poor perfusion) ولون شاحب مزرق (Dusky color)، هذا التدهور الجهازي يمثل صدمة إنتانية Pneumonia complicated with sepsis / septic shock (الخيار B).
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان اختار Pneumonia complicated with pneumothorax (الخيار D) بناءً على فكرة انفجار Staphylococcal pneumatocele في الرضع.

3. سؤال الـ CAP بخيارات مستوردة من مرض الدرن (سؤال Q215):
• الملاحظة الإكلينيكية: حالة مريضة مسنة مصابة بـ COPD ولديها Right lower lobe pneumonia (CAP)، وعلاجها الطبي المعتمد هو Ceftriaxone + Azithromycin أو Levofloxacin. في الامتحان تم استيراد خيارات سؤال درن بالخطأ، وتم اعتماد الخيار الرباعي Rifampin, isoniazid, pyrazinamide, and ethambutol (RIPE - الخيار B) تماشياً مع مذكرة د. أحمد عامر.

Topic 4 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

4. Tuberculosis

🔥 High Yield (15×) 12 Qs

1. Active vs. Latent Tuberculosis Diagnostic & Treatment Pathways 🔥 x30

Clinical Scenario Diagnostic Findings & Exposure History Definitive Next Step / Regimen
Suspected Active Pulmonary TB 🔥 x7 Chronic cough >3–4 weeks + evening fever, night sweats, weight loss, and bilateral apical crepitations unresponsive to broad-spectrum antibiotics Obtain Sputum for Acid-Fast Bacilli (AFB smear and culture) + GeneXpert MTB/RIF PCR
Symptomatic Contact + Positive TST (≥15 mm) x2 Patient with chronic cough, night sweats, weight loss, and a Tuberculin Skin Test (TST) induration ≥15 mm Perform a Chest X-ray first to rule out active cavitary disease before initiating any treatment
Active Pulmonary TB on Chest X-Ray x2 Symptomatic household contact with CXR demonstrating bilateral upper-lobe infiltrates and cavitation Collect sputum and immediately start 4-drug therapy (RIPE: Rifampin, Isoniazid, Pyrazinamide, Ethambutol)
Latent Tuberculosis Infection (LTBI) 🔥 x15 Asymptomatic individual with a positive TST or positive QuantiFERON-TB Gold (IGRA) and a completely normal Chest X-ray Initiate Isoniazid (INH) monotherapy for 6–9 months (or Rifampin for 4 months)
Recent TB Exposure + Initial Negative TST 🔥 x4 Healthcare worker or contact with active TB case with an initial negative TST result within the immunological window period Repeat the Tuberculin Skin Test (TST) in 3 months (8–12 weeks post-exposure)

2. TB Histopathology, Drug Resistance & Extrapulmonary Sites 🔥 x7

  • Caseating Granuloma Histopathological Hallmark:
    • Transbronchial lung biopsy demonstrating epithelioid histiocytes, Langhans multinucleated giant cells with peripheral nuclei, and central acellular caseous necrosis establishes the diagnosis of Tuberculosis. x1
  • Failure of Standard Anti-TB Therapy (Drug Resistance):
    • Persistent fever and productive cough after 2–4 weeks of compliant first-line RIPE therapy raises high suspicion that the TB bacteria have developed resistance to the prescribed drugs (MDR-TB). 🔥 x5
    • In relapsed cavitary pulmonary TB (positive MTB PCR) in patients from endemic areas with a history of prior TB treatment, initiate an empiric Multi-Drug-Resistant TB (MDR-TB) regimen pending susceptibility culture. x1
  • Extrapulmonary Tuberculosis Organ Sites:
    • In exam questions assessing extrapulmonary TB sites among specific multiple-choice options (Liver, Spleen, Kidney, Central Nervous System, Skin), the Central Nervous System (Tuberculous Meningitis / Tuberculoma) represents the keyed option (Option D, Model Answer). x1
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 5 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

5. Pulmonary Embolism

⚡ Medium Yield (8×) 19 Qs

1. Pulmonary Embolism (PE): Risk Stratification & Acute Triage 🔥 x35

Clinical Category Hemodynamic & Physiologic Status Key Clinical Clues Immediate Definitive Action
Low Pre-Test Probability PE x1 Hemodynamically stable (normotensive; low Wells score ≤4) Mild pleuritic pain/dyspnea in outpatient setting Order a high-sensitivity Plasma D-Dimer Assay; if negative, rules out PE without imaging
High Probability / Hemodynamically Stable PE 🔥 x25 Normotensive (BP ≥90/60 mmHg) with acute hypoxemia (SpO2 <92%, e.g., 88–90%) Postoperative (hip arthroplasty), long flight, oral contraceptives; sudden pleuritic chest pain, tachypnea (RR ≥28), and clear lung auscultation Immediate administration of Oxygen + Intravenous Heparin to halt clot propagation, followed by urgent CT Pulmonary Angiography (CTPA)
Acute PE in Pregnancy x1 Third trimester (34 weeks) pregnant woman; normotensive Sudden pleuritic pain, tachypnea, normal CXR Therapeutic Heparin (LMWH or UFH); Warfarin is strictly teratogenic and contraindicated in pregnancy
Massive (High-Risk / Unstable) PE 🔥 x8 Obstructive Shock (Systolic BP ≤80–90 mmHg), syncope, profound cyanosis CT shows large saddle embolus in the main pulmonary artery trunk with acute right heart strain Immediate systemic fibrinolytic therapy: Intravenous Alteplase (tPA) 100 mg over 2 hours

2. Auscultation, Electrocardiography & Arthroplasty Prophylaxis 🔥 x13

  • Auscultatory Sign of Acute Pulmonary Arterial Hypertension in PE:
    • Forceful closure of the pulmonic valve against high pulmonary vascular resistance makes the pulmonic component of the second heart sound louder than the aortic component (P2 louder than A2). 🔥 x9
  • Electrocardiographic (ECG) Findings in Acute PE:
    • The most frequent / common ECG finding in acute PE is Sinus Tachycardia. x3
    • The classic S1Q3T3 pattern (deep S wave in lead I, pathological Q wave in lead III, and T-wave inversion in lead III) signifies acute right ventricular strain but occurs in a minority of patients.
  • Diagnostic Gold Standard: While CTPA is the definitive first-line imaging modality of choice, conventional Pulmonary Angiogram (Catheter Angiography) represents the historical gold standard. x1
  • Postoperative Arthroplasty Thromboprophylaxis:
    • A high-risk patient undergoing total hip replacement requires postoperative pharmacologic anticoagulation prophylaxis; in multiple-choice exams where it is the sole anticoagulant listed, Warfarin is keyed as preventing fatal PE. x1
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة وخيارات Pulmonary Embolism والـ Model Answer:

انفصال خياري الأكسجين والهيبارين في سؤال الـ PE (سؤال Q66):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: في حالات الـ Pulmonary Embolism المصحوبة بنقص أكسجين (SpO2 90%)، إعطاء الأكسجين والبدء الفوري في مضادات التجلط بالـ IV Heparin كلاهما خطوة علاجية حرجة وفورية.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: في النسخة المكررة من السؤال (Q66) حيث جاء خيار الأكسجين منفصلاً عن خيار الهيبارين، اعتمد الـ Model Answer خيار Administering IV heparin (الخيار E) كإجراء دوائي حاسم يمنع تمدد الجلطة القاتلة.

Topic 6 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

6. Pleural & Thoracic Trauma

⚡ Medium Yield (8×) 18 Qs

1. Pleural Effusion vs. Tension Pneumothorax vs. Tracheobronchial Rupture 🔥 x19

Clinical Feature Pleural Effusion x1 Tension Pneumothorax 🔥 x7 Tracheobronchial Rupture x1
Percussion & Breath Sounds Stony dullness to percussion + absent/diminished breath sounds over the fluid level Marked hyperresonance to percussion + completely absent breath sounds unilaterally Unilateral absent breath sounds + massive subcutaneous emphysema extending over face, neck, and chest
Trachea & Hemodynamics Trachea shifted away (contralateral) only if massive fluid collection; hemodynamically stable Trachea shifted contralaterally + Obstructive Shock (BP 90/60 mmHg, HR 105 bpm) with distended neck veins Severe respiratory distress; persistent massive air leak observed immediately following chest tube placement
Immediate Life-Saving Action Diagnostic ultrasound/CXR followed by diagnostic thoracentesis Immediate Needle Thoracostomy Decompression (2nd ICS midclavicular or 5th ICS anterior axillary line) followed by Chest Tube Urgent Bronchoscopy to visualize the tear followed by immediate operative airway repair

2. Thoracic Trauma: Rib Fractures, Flail Chest & Esophageal Perforation 🔥 x20

  • Blunt Thoracic Trauma & Isolated Rib Fractures:
    • A blunt injury causing focal rib tenderness requires a Chest X-ray as the initial test of choice to rule out pneumothorax, hemothorax, and displaced fractures. 🔥 x11
    • If the chest radiograph is completely normal and room air SpO2 is 100%, management consists of adequate analgesia (oral NSAIDs/paracetamol) and reassurance. Avoid circular chest strapping/binding to prevent secondary atelectasis and pneumonia.
  • Flail Chest & Pulmonary Contusion:
    • Severe blunt chest trauma (steering wheel impact in motor vehicle collision) with multiple adjacent rib fractures in multiple places producing paradoxical chest wall movement (flail segment moving inward during inspiration and outward during expiration). x3
    • Often associated with underlying Pulmonary Contusion (patchy parenchymal infiltrates of alveolar edema and hemorrhage on CXR appearing within hours of trauma). x2
  • Spontaneous Esophageal Perforation (Boerhaave Syndrome):
    • A patient who forceful vomits following heavy alcohol or food intake, presenting with agonizing retrosternal chest pain, rapid clinical collapse, subcutaneous emphysema in the neck, pneumomediastinum, and a left-sided pleural effusion = Boerhaave syndrome. x1
⚠️ تنبيه هام للتعامل مع أسئلة وخلافات الـ Model Answer في توبيك Thoracic Trauma:

1. خطأ مفتاح الامتحان في تشخيص وعلاج استرواح الصدر التوتري (سؤال Q125 وسؤال Q126):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: مريض يعاني من نقص أصوات التنفس في الجهة اليسرى مع انحراف القصبة الهوائية لليمين (Contralateral tracheal shift) وهبوط في الضغط (BP 90/60) وتسارع بالنبض (105)، هذه هي العلامات الكلاسيكية الصريحة لـ Tension Pneumothorax (الخيار E)، وعلاجه الطارئ الفوري لإنقاذ الحياة هو Needle thoracostomy decompression (الخيار E) قبل أن يتحول لتوقف قلب.
• خلي بالك مع الـ Model Answer: في واحد من أشهر أخطاء المفاتيح، اختار مفتاح الامتحان في Q125 خطأً Simple pneumothorax (الخيار C)، واختار في Q126 خطأً Observation (الخيار C)!

2. علاج القفص الصدري السائب Flail Chest (سؤال Q784):
• الأساس العلمي والـ AI Answer: بروتوكولات الـ ATLS الحديثة تعتمد تسكين الألم متعدد الوسائط (Multimodal analgesia) والأكسجين كخط أساسي مع حجز التنفس الصناعي لحالات الفشل التنفسي.
• الـ Model Answer: مفتاح الامتحان اعتمد المفهوم الكلاسيكي التاريخي (Internal pneumatic stabilization) باختيار Tracheostomy, mechanical ventilation and PEEP (الخيار D).

3. كدمات الصدر مع صورة أشعة مفقودة (سؤال Q920):
• الـ Model Answer: في حالات حوادث السيارات المستقرة ديناميكياً مع تماثل أصوات التنفس وأكسجين 92% وتورم بالصدر مع صورة أشعة مفقودة في ورقة الامتحان، اعتمد الـ Model Answer خيار Supplemental oxygen (الخيار E) والملاحظة المستمرة.

Topic 7 of 7 • Internal Medicine • Chest

7. Interstitial Lung Disease

🔥 High Yield (10×) 3 Qs

1. Interstitial Lung Diseases (ILD) Clinical Comparison x3

Feature Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis (IPF) x1 Sarcoidosis x1 Methotrexate-Induced Pneumonitis x1
Patient Demographics & History Older male (≥60 years); progressive exertional dyspnea and chronic dry hacking cough Young female (20–40 years); constitutional symptoms, dry cough, erythema nodosum, uveitis, and hepatosplenomegaly Patient with Rheumatoid Arthritis maintained on chronic low-dose Methotrexate presenting with subacute dyspnea and dry cough
Physical Signs & Auscultation Bilateral basal fine end-inspiratory "Velcro" crackles + permanent digital clubbing Clear lungs or scattered wheezes/crackles; cervical/axillary lymphadenopathy; skin plaques (lupus pernio) Diffuse inspiratory crackles; fever and tachypnea developing weeks to months after starting Methotrexate
Diagnostic Hallmarks High-Resolution CT (HRCT) of chest demonstrates peripheral, subpleural, basilar reticular opacities with honeycombing and traction bronchiectasis Chest radiograph shows Bilateral Hilar Lymphadenopathy (BHL) ± diffuse reticulonodular infiltrates; serum shows hypercalcemia and elevated ACE level HRCT reveals diffuse bilateral ground-glass attenuation and patchy infiltrates; immediate management requires discontinuation of Methotrexate

Topic 1 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

1. Esophageal Disorders

🔥 High Yield (57×) 18 Qs

1. Dysphagia Classification & Diagnostic Approach 🔥 x33

Condition Clinical Pattern Diagnostic Gold-Standard Definitive Management
Esophageal Carcinoma 🔥 x13 Progressive dysphagia (solids → liquids) + marked weight loss (>10–15 kg) & cachexia in older adults (>55y) Upper GI Endoscopy & Biopsy (mandatory initial test) Staging CT/PET → surgical resection (esophagectomy) ± neoadjuvant chemoradiotherapy
Benign Peptic Stricture x2 Longstanding GERD (>10y); slowly progressive dysphagia to solids; smooth tapered distal narrowing on barium swallow Esophagoscopy & Biopsy first (essential to rule out adenocarcinoma) Endoscopic Balloon Dilation + high-dose Proton Pump Inhibitors (PPI)
Achalasia 🔥 x6 Dysphagia to both solids and liquids from onset + regurgitation of undigested food, nocturnal cough, weight loss, mediastinal air-fluid level on CXR Esophageal Manometry: Aperistalsis in esophageal body + failure of LES relaxation Pneumatic balloon dilation or Laparoscopic Heller Myotomy (± botulinum toxin in frail patients)
Zenker Diverticulum (Pharyngeal Pouch) 🔥 x8 Elderly; high oropharyngeal dysphagia, halitosis, undigested food regurgitation, gurgling neck mass, recurrent aspiration pneumonia Barium Swallow (avoid blind upper endoscopy due to high perforation risk!) Cricopharyngeal myotomy ± diverticulectomy (or endoscopic diverticulotomy)
  • Benign vs. Malignant Dysphagia Clinical Distinction: 🔥 x5
    • Favors Benign Motility/Structural Etiology: Long episodic history of dysphagia lasting several years without anorexia or weight loss. 🔥 x5
    • Favors Malignancy: Rapidly progressive dysphagia (over weeks/months), profound anorexia, severe weight loss, or hoarseness (recurrent laryngeal nerve invasion).

2. Gastroesophageal Reflux Disease (GERD) 🔥 x12

  • Pathophysiologic Mechanism: Lower resting lower esophageal sphincter (LES) tone and increased transient LES relaxations allowing gastric acid into the esophagus. 🔥 x4
  • Clinical Hallmark: Retrosternal burning heartburn (pyrosis) + sour fluid regurgitation, characteristically aggravated after heavy meals & lying supine. x2
  • Stepwise Diagnostic & Therapeutic Strategy:
    • Uncomplicated Typical GERD (No Alarm Features): Initial empirical 8-week course of Proton Pump Inhibitors (Omeprazole 20–40 mg daily). x3
    • Refractory or Extra-Esophageal Reflux (Chronic Cough, Hoarseness, Laryngeal Erythema): When symptoms persist despite high-dose PPI and endoscopy is normal, confirm with 24-Hour Ambulatory Esophageal pH Monitoring. 🔥 x4

3. Mallory-Weiss Syndrome & Pill-Induced Esophagitis 🔥 x7

  • Mallory-Weiss Syndrome: 🔥 x6
    • Characterized by longitudinal mucosal tears at the gastroesophageal junction following bouts of forceful retching or vomiting (classically in alcohol binge).
    • Management: If endoscopy demonstrates no active residual bleeding and the patient is hemodynamically stable → Observation and supportive care (>90% stop bleeding spontaneously).
  • Pill-Induced Esophagitis: x1
    • Young patient presenting with sudden retrosternal chest burning related to eating, normal ECG and normal CXR; taking oral medication for acne (classically Doxycycline or Tetracycline ingested with minimal water before bedtime).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص إدارة Peptic Stricture (سؤال Q388 وسؤال Q441):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: عند مريض يعاني من GERD مزمن مع تضيق أسفل المريء في Barium swallow، فإن الخطوة التشخيصية الإلزامية الأولى لنفي الأورام الخبيثة هي Esophagoscopy and Biopsy (سؤال Q441). وبعد التأكد من الطبيعة الحميدة، يكون الإجراء العلاجي الأساسي لتوسيع التضيق الميكانيكي وتخفيف الـ Dysphagia هو Endoscopic Dilation (Option A) مع استخدام جرعات عالية من Proton Pump Inhibitors (PPI) لمنع تكرار التضيق.

في الـ Model Answer في تجميعات د. أحمد عامر: في سؤال Q388 تم اختيار Option B (Initiating proton pump inhibitor (PPI) therapy) كبداية علاجية دوائية للسيطرة على إفراز الحمض، بينما الإجراء الميكانيكي هو Dilation.

Topic 2 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

2. Peptic Ulcer Disease

🔥 High Yield (71×) 26 Qs

1. Uncomplicated Peptic Ulcer Disease & H. pylori 🔥 x17

  • Duodenal Ulcer (DU) Pattern: Burning epigastric pain occurring 2–3 hours after meals or during the night, characteristically relieved by food intake or antacids. Primary etiologies: Helicobacter pylori (>90%) and NSAIDs. In pediatric patients, chronic recurrent epigastric pain and vomiting without NSAID use is most commonly caused by H. pylori gastritis. 🔥 x7
  • H. pylori Diagnostic Confirmation, Eradication & Test of Cure: 🔥 x10
    • Endoscopic Biopsy: Rapid urease test (CLO test) or histologic examination showing spiral gram-negative bacilli. x1
    • Eradication Regimen: 10–14 days of Triple Therapy (PPI + Clarithromycin + Amoxicillin / Metronidazole), or 6–8 weeks of acid suppression (Omeprazole / Ranitidine) for ulcer healing. x2
    • Non-Invasive Test of Cure: Urea Breath Test (UBT) or Stool Antigen Test performed ≥4 weeks post-treatment (never repeat serology, as antibodies persist for years!). 🔥 x7

2. Peptic Ulcer Complications & Gastric Pathology 🔥 x54

Complication Clinical Presentation Diagnostic Hallmark Definitive Management
Perforated Peptic Ulcer 🔥 x22 Sudden catastrophic severe epigastric pain, pale/sweaty, board-like abdominal rigidity, diffuse rebound tenderness, shock Upright CXR: Free air under the diaphragm (Pneumoperitoneum) in >75% 🔥 x11 Aggressive IV fluid resuscitation + IV Abx + immediate Urgent Surgical Consultation / Laparotomy (Graham patch repair)
Subphrenic Abscess x3 Postoperative day 4–7 after perforated DU closure; spiking fever, toxic look, RUQ guarding + referred right shoulder-tip pain (phrenic C3–C5 irritation) Subdiaphragmatic fluid collection with gas on abdominal Ultrasound or CT Percutaneous image-guided drainage + broad-spectrum IV antibiotics
Gastric Outlet Obstruction (GOO) 🔥 x17 Longstanding ulcer disease; intractable vomiting of undigested food eaten hours before, early satiety, weight loss, epigastric succussion splash x2 Lab hallmark: Hypochloremic, hypokalemic metabolic alkalosis with elevated bicarbonate and compensatory PaCO2 retention Nasogastric decompression + IV hydration + Best indication for elective surgery in DU (cicatricial pyloric stenosis) x2
Zollinger-Ellison Syndrome (Gastrinoma) 🔥 x7 Multiple, atypical, or recurrent ulcers refractory to PPI or recurring after acid-reducing surgery (vagotomy / Billroth II partial gastrectomy) Markedly elevated Fasting Serum Gastrin level (>1000 pg/mL) High-dose PPI therapy + somatostatin receptor scintigraphy / surgical tumor resection
  • Virchow Node (Troisier Sign): Hard, non-tender, enlarged left supraclavicular lymph node indicating metastatic spread from gastric adenocarcinoma (associated with weight loss, epigastric fullness, and fecal occult blood). x2
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Verified Recommendation

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص فحوصات الألم الشرسوفي الحاد (سؤال Q301 وسؤال Q391):

1. حالة Epigastric Pain & Rigidity (سؤال Q301): مريض يعاني من ألم حاد وتصلب بطني تام (Board-like rigidity) مع ارتفاع طفيف غير تشخيصي في Amylase (199 U/L). سريرياً الفحص السريع الأولي لنفي Perforated Viscus هو Upright Chest X-Ray للبحث عن Free air under diaphragm، ولكن الـ Model Answer في الامتحان يختار Option A (Contrast enhanced CT abdomen) باعتباره الفحص التشخيصي الأكثر حساسية وتفصيلاً لتأكيد أمراض البطن الحادة و Pancreatitis.

2. حالة Severe Epigastric Pain + Vomiting (سؤال Q391): سريرياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer يجب إجراء فحص دم سريع غير تداخلي لـ Serum Amylase/Lipase (Option E) لاستبعاد Acute Pancreatitis أولاً قبل أي إجراء تداخلي، بينما الـ Model Answer في الامتحان اعتمد Option C (Gastroscopy) لتقييم Peptic Ulcer Disease مباشرة.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 3 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

3. Upper GI Bleeding

🔥 High Yield (37×) 20 Qs

1. Upper GI Bleeding (UGIB) Comparison & Etiologies 🔥 x34

Feature Esophageal Varices 🔥 x29 Acute Gastric Erosions x3 Mallory-Weiss Tear x2
Risk Factors / Trigger Liver Cirrhosis & Portal Hypertension (alcohol abuse, chronic hepatitis B/C) Daily NSAID ingestion, high-dose aspirin, severe physiological stress Violent, repeated retching or vomiting (classically after alcohol binge)
Clinical & Lab Clues Sudden massive bright red hematemesis ± melena; stigmata of cirrhosis: jaundice, ascites, splenomegaly, thrombocytopenia Coffee-ground emesis; NG aspirate clears promptly with saline gastric lavage; no stigmata of chronic liver disease Vomiting non-bloody gastric contents initially, followed by hematemesis; mucosal GE junction tear
Definitive Management Aggressive resuscitation → IV Octreotide / Somatostatin → urgent Endoscopic Band Ligation (EVL) Discontinue offending NSAID + IV Proton Pump Inhibitors (PPI); endoscopy if persistent Observation and supportive care (>90% achieve spontaneous hemostasis)

2. Emergency Resuscitation & Variceal Hemostasis Protocol 🔥 x15

  • Immediate Resuscitation Priority (ABC): 🔥 x9
    • In acute severe UGIB with hemorrhagic shock (BP <90/60 mmHg, HR >110 bpm, pallor, cold extremities): Insert two large-bore IV cannulae (16G–18G) and immediately initiate IV isotonic crystalloids.
    • Blood Transfusion: Transfusion of Packed Red Blood Cells (PRBCs) to restore oxygen delivery (target Hb >7–9 g/dL).
  • Urgent Endoscopy (EGD): Performed within 12–24 hours post-resuscitation to establish definitive diagnosis and deliver endoscopic hemostasis (band ligation or hemoclips). 🔥 x5
  • Pharmacologic Vasoactive Therapy: x2
    • Somatostatin / Octreotide: Selective splanchnic vasoconstrictor that reduces splanchnic blood flow and lowers portal venous pressure; administer immediately upon suspicion of variceal hemorrhage.
  • Refractory or Gastric Variceal Hemorrhage: x3
    • For uncontrolled bleeding from gastric varices or recurrent massive bleeding despite endoscopic band ligation and octreotide: Definitive salvage therapy is Transjugular Intrahepatic Portosystemic Shunt (TIPS).
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 4 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

4. Gallbladder & Biliary Disorders

🔥 High Yield (65×) 15 Qs

1. Gallstone Disease Spectrum 🔥 x57

Feature Biliary Colic 🔥 x4 Acute Cholecystitis 🔥 x47 Ascending Cholangitis 🔥 x6
Pain Pattern & Systemic Signs Intermittent, severe colicky RUQ pain lasting <4–6 hours after fatty food; afebrile, no leukocytosis Persistent severe RUQ pain (>6 hours) radiating to right scapula / shoulder; fever (>38°C) + leukocytosis Charcot Triad: Severe RUQ pain + spiking fever with rigors + jaundice (Reynaud pentad adds shock + altered mental status)
Physical Examination Mild RUQ tenderness; Negative Murphy sign; no peritoneal guarding Positive Murphy Sign: Spontaneous inspiratory arrest on deep RUQ palpation; localized guarding 🔥 x10 Jaundice, marked RUQ tenderness; common after choledocholithiasis or post-ERCP instrumentation
Diagnostic Hallmark & Action Abdominal Ultrasound: Gallstones with thin gallbladder wall (<3 mm) Abdominal Ultrasound: Thickened gallbladder wall (>4 mm) + pericholecystic fluid + sonographic Murphy sign 🔥 x7 Emergency IV hydration + IV broad-spectrum antibiotics + Urgent Biliary Decompression via ERCP

2. Biliary Anatomy, Analgesia Rules & Surgical Complications 🔥 x8

  • Calot Triangle (Hepatocystic Triangle) Boundaries: x2
    • Lateral / Inferior: Cystic duct.
    • Medial: Common hepatic duct.
    • Superior: Inferior border of the liver. (Contains the Cystic Artery).
  • Analgesia Caution in Biliary Colic: Morphine causes spasm of the Sphincter of Oddi and can aggravate intrabiliary pressure. x3
  • Iatrogenic Bile Duct Injury Post-Laparoscopic Cholecystectomy: Patient developing progressive scleral icterus, abdominal pain, and cholestatic LFTs on postoperative days 2–3 with an empty peritoneal drain: Indicates iatrogenic Common Bile Duct (CBD) clipping / transection. x3
  • Ascending Cholangitis Post-Kasai Procedure: Infant with biliary atresia who underwent Kasai portoenterostomy presenting with fever and high leukocytosis: Indicates ascending cholangitis via the surgically created enteric conduit. x1
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص تشخيص ألم الـ Right Hypochondrium (سؤال Q284):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: أنثى عمرها 30 عاماً تعاني من ألم حاد في Right Hypochondrium لمدة يومين يمتد إلى الظهر والكتف الأيمن ويزداد بعد الوجبات الدهنية (Fatty food) مع حرارة 38.6°C وموضع Guarding في الربع العلوي الأيمن وبدون يرقان. هذه الصورة السريرية الكلاسيكية تمثل قطعياً Acute Cholecystitis (Option C).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم تسجيل الإجابة بالخطأ Option D (Acute appendicitis) نتيجة خطأ إدخال بيانات (Data-entry error) في مفتاح الامتحان الأصلي.

Topic 5 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

5. Autoimmune & Cholestatic Liver Diseases

🔥 High Yield (60×) 13 Qs

1. Autoimmune & Cholestatic Liver Disorders 🔥 x60

Condition Patient & Clinical Profile Diagnostic Hallmark First-Line Therapy
Primary Biliary Cholangitis (PBC) 🔥 x12 Middle-aged female (40–60y); progressive pruritus (often nocturnal), fatigue, xanthelasma; marked elevation of Alkaline Phosphatase (ALP) & GGT Autoimmune T-cell destruction of small intrahepatic bile ducts; (+) Anti-Mitochondrial Antibody (AMA) in >95% x3 Ursodeoxycholic Acid (UDCA) 13–15 mg/kg/day (cytoprotective, immunomodulatory; slows disease progression)
Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis (PSC) 🔥 x21 Young-to-middle-aged male strongly associated with Ulcerative Colitis (UC, 70–80%); cholestatic jaundice, pruritus, AMA negative, p-ANCA often (+) MRCP / ERCP: Multifocal segmental strictures & dilations of intra- & extra-hepatic bile ducts ("beaded" appearance) Supportive; endoscopic balloon dilation of dominant strictures; liver transplantation; high risk of Cholangiocarcinoma
Cholangiocarcinoma 🔥 x8 Severe complication of UC / longstanding PSC; progressive obstructive jaundice, anorexia, weight loss, palpable RUQ mass MRCP/CT mass lesion + markedly elevated serum CA 19-9 tumor marker Surgical resection (Whipple or partial hepatectomy) if resectable; palliative biliary stenting
Autoimmune Hepatitis (AIH) 🔥 x9 Young female; acute or insidious jaundice, hepatomegaly; massive transaminitis (ALT / AST >10–20x ULN, often >2000 U/L) + elevated serum IgG (+) ANA & Anti-Smooth Muscle Antibody (ASMA) (Type 1) or Anti-LKM1 (Type 2); Biopsy: Interface hepatitis with plasma cell infiltrates Systemic Corticosteroids (Prednisone) + Azathioprine maintenance x2
Drug-Induced Cholestasis 🔥 x4 Painless jaundice, pruritus, and marked elevation of ALP shortly after starting offending medication (classic with Erythromycin estolate, Amoxicillin-clavulanate) Normal intra- and extra-hepatic bile ducts on abdominal Ultrasound (excludes mechanical gallstone obstruction) Immediate discontinuation of the offending antibiotic; prompt resolution of cholestasis
  • Primary Biliary Cholangitis (PBC) Etiology (Q404): Progressive cholestasis and secondary biliary cirrhosis caused by autoimmunity (autoimmune destruction of interlobular bile ducts mediated by autoreactive CD4+ and CD8+ T cells; diagnostic marker: Anti-Mitochondrial Antibodies [AMA]). x2
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص آليات عمل Ursodeoxycholic Acid (سؤال Q424):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: دواء Ursodeoxycholic Acid (UDCA) يعمل كـ Hepatoprotective و Antiapoptotic، ويقلل إفراز الكوليسترول في العصارة الصفراوية (Inhibits synthesis and secretion of cholesterol)، ويبطئ تطور المرض، وليس له أي علاقة إطلاقاً بعلاج الأنيميا؛ ولذلك الإجابة الطبية الصحيحة على سؤال (Which is NOT a mechanism) هي قطعاً Option E (Treat anemia).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option D (Inhibits synthesis of cholesterol) في المفتاح القديم كخيار غير صحيح لآلية العمل، رغم أن تثبيط الكوليسترول من الآليات الدوائية الثابتة لـ UDCA.
