Topic 6 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

6. Pancreatic Disorders

⚡ Medium Yield (33×) 11 Qs

1. Acute Pancreatitis & Pancreatic Cysts 🔥 x17

  • Diagnostic Triad of Acute Pancreatitis (Requires ≥2 of 3): 🔥 x13
    • Pain: Severe, acute, continuous epigastric pain radiating straight through to the back, typically relieved by leaning forward, associated with nausea and vomiting.
    • Enzyme Biomarkers: Serum amylase or lipase ≥3x upper limit of normal (e.g., >1100 U/L; Lipase is more sensitive and specific, remaining elevated longer).
    • Characteristic Imaging: Contrast-enhanced abdominal CT or MRI demonstrating pancreatic enlargement, peripancreatic fat stranding, or fluid collections.
    • Leading Etiologies: Gallstones (biliary pancreatitis) and alcohol abuse account for >80% of cases. Hypertriglyceridemia (serum TG >1000 mg/dL) is a well-established etiologic cause.
  • Modified Glasgow / Imrie Severity Score (PANCREAS Criteria): 🔥 x6
    • Assessed at 48 hours: PaO2 <60 mmHg, Age >55y, Neutrophils (WBC >15×109/L), Calcium <2 mmol/L, Renal urea >16 mmol/L, Enzymes (LDH >600 U/L), Albumin <32 g/L, Sugar (Glucose >10 mmol/L). (Score ≥3 indicates severe pancreatitis). Note: Excludes HR, BP, temperature, and serum amylase.
  • Infected Pancreatic Necrosis: Spiking fever, leukocytosis, and gas bubbles within pancreatic necrosis on contrast CT → Manage with CT-guided percutaneous catheter drainage + broad-spectrum IV antibiotics (Carbapenems). x1
  • True Pancreatic Cyst vs. Pseudocyst: x3
    • True Pancreatic Cyst: Lined by a true epithelial cellular lining (congenital, retention, or mucinous cystadenoma).
    • Pancreatic Pseudocyst: Follows acute pancreatitis by 4–6 weeks; encapsulated fluid collection surrounded by fibrous and granulation tissue devoid of an epithelial lining.

2. Chronic Pancreatitis & Exocrine Insufficiency 🔥 x16

  • Clinical Triad of Chronic Pancreatitis: Intractable epigastric pain + Steatorrhea (bulky, pale, foul-smelling greasy stools difficult to flush) + Secondary Diabetes Mellitus (Hyperglycemia) due to islet destruction. 🔥 x8
  • Steatorrhea Differential by Organ of Origin: 🔥 x8
    • Pancreatic insufficiency (lipase deficiency), Biliary obstruction / liver failure (bile salt deficiency), and Small-intestinal mucosal disease (Celiac/Crohn) cause steatorrhea. The Stomach is the least likely organ to cause steatorrhea.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص مظاهر Pancreatic Insufficiency (سؤال Q423):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: قصور البنكرياس المزمن يؤدي إلى نقص الإنزيمات الهاضمة مما يسبب Steatorrhea و Diarrhea و Weight loss، ومع تدمير جزر لانجرهانس يحدث Pancreatogenic Diabetes مع Hyperglycemia. أما Hypertriglyceridemia (Option A) فهي سبب معروف (Etiologic Cause) لحدوث التهاب البنكرياس الحاد وليست أثراً ناتجاً عن القصور المزمن، ولذلك هي الإجابة الطبية الصحيحة لسؤال (Which is NOT a manifestation).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option D (Normal blood glucose level) بافتراض أن مستوى السكر لا يكون طبيعياً في القصور البنكرياسي المتقدم بل يرتفع نتيجة تدمير خلايا بيتا المفرزة للإنسولين.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 7 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

7. Viral Hepatitis

⚡ Medium Yield (33×) 21 Qs

1. Viral Hepatitis Comparison (HAV vs. HBV vs. HCV) 🔥 x25

Feature Hepatitis A (HAV) 🔥 x12 Hepatitis B (HBV) 🔥 x8 Hepatitis C (HCV) 🔥 x5
Transmission Route Fecal-oral route (contaminated food, untreated water, shellfish) x2 Parenteral (needlestick injuries in surgeons/nurses 🔥 x4), sexual, vertical (perinatal) Blood transfusion / IV drug abuse; rarely sexual x3
Chronicity Risk 0% (Never becomes chronic); self-limiting acute hepatitis 5–10% in immunocompetent adults; up to 90% in infected neonates x1 High chronicity (~80%); >20% progress to cirrhosis & HCC x3
Diagnostic Hallmark Anti-HAV IgM (acute phase marker; massive transaminitis ALT >1000) 🔥 x5 Initial screen: Hepatitis B Surface Antigen (HBsAg) (ALT/AST >> ALP) Screen: Anti-HCV Antibody; confirm active viremia via HCV RNA PCR x1

2. Hepatitis B Serologic Profiles & Healthcare Worker Protection 🔥 x8

Serologic Marker HBeAg Positive x3 Isolated Anti-HBs (+) x1 Anti-HBs (+) & Anti-HBc IgG (+) 🔥 x4
Clinical Interpretation Active viral replication & HIGH infectivity; marker of high transmission risk and increased chronicity Vaccine-induced protective immunity (HBsAg negative, Anti-HBc negative) Immunity from resolved past natural HBV infection (HBsAg negative)
  • Infected Healthcare Worker / Surgeon Protocol (Q328): A surgeon found to be HBsAg positive, HBeAg positive, and Anti-HBc positive associated with post-operative acute HBV transmissions in surgical patients must receive a full course of antiviral therapy (Interferon or nucleos(t)ide analogues) and be restricted from performing exposure-prone surgical procedures until viral load is fully suppressed. x1
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص فحص Acute Viral Hepatitis الأولي (سؤال Q243):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: عند تقييم مريض شاب (28 عاماً) يعاني من يرقان حاد وألم بالربع العلوي الأيمن مع ارتفاع كبير في إنزيمات الكبد (ALT 670 و AST 480)، فإن البانيل المصلي المبدئي القياسي لالتهاب الكبد الحاد يشمل فحص كل من Anti-HAV IgM (Option B) و HBsAg (Option C) معاً لاستبعاد HAV و HBV.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اعتماد Option B (Anti HAV IgM) كإجابة نموذجية لفحص اليرقان الفيروسي الحاد الأولي.

Topic 8 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

8. Liver Cirrhosis & Portal Hypertension

🔥 High Yield (72×) 17 Qs

1. Liver Cirrhosis, Ascites & Coagulopathy 🔥 x34

  • Most Common Complication of Cirrhosis: Ascites (>80% of decompensated patients), accompanied by physical stigmata of portal hypertension: palmar erythema, spider nevi, caput medusae, gynecomastia, and splenomegaly. 🔥 x8
  • Ascitic Fluid Analysis & SAAG Gradient: 🔥 x12
    • Portal Hypertension Ascites: Serum-Ascites Albumin Gradient (SAAG) ≥1.1 g/dL (Cirrhosis, Budd-Chiari, Congestive heart failure) x3 → Initial management: dietary sodium restriction (<2 g/day) + Oral Diuretics (Spironolactone ± Furosemide) x2.
    • Non-Portal Hypertension Ascites: SAAG <1.1 g/dL (Peritoneal carcinomatosis, Tuberculous peritonitis, Nephrotic syndrome, Pancreatic ascites).
    • Spontaneous Bacterial Peritonitis (SBP): Cirrhotic patient with ascites presenting with fever, abdominal pain, or worsening encephalopathy; diagnostic criterion: Ascitic fluid absolute polymorphonuclear (PMN) count ≥250 cells/mm³ → Immediate empiric IV 3rd-generation cephalosporin (Cefotaxime 2g IV q8h). x1
  • Preoperative Coagulopathy Correction (Prolonged PT / INR): Cirrhosis impairs hepatic synthesis of vitamin K-dependent and independent clotting factors. Before emergency or elective surgery, correct prolonged PT/INR immediately with Fresh Frozen Plasma (FFP) transfusion. 🔥 x21

2. Specific Parenchymal & Focal Liver Pathologies 🔥 x38

Condition Clinical Context & Key Presentation Diagnostic Hallmark & Action
Hepatocellular Carcinoma (HCC) 🔥 x17 New focal liver mass, rapid decompensation, or bloody ascites in a patient with cirrhosis (HBV 🔥 x9, HCV, alcoholic 🔥 x4) Markedly elevated serum Alpha-Fetoprotein (AFP) + dynamic multiphasic CT/MRI showing arterial enhancement and venous washout
Acetaminophen Hepatotoxicity 🔥 x6 Toxic ingestion (>10–15g); centrilobular hepatic necrosis mediated by toxic NAPQI metabolite depletion of glutathione Leading cause of Acute Liver Failure; Antidote: prompt administration of N-Acetylcysteine (NAC)
Alcoholic Hepatitis x2 Heavy chronic ethanol intake presenting with acute jaundice, fever, tender hepatomegaly, anorexia, and leukocytosis AST:ALT ratio ≥2:1 (AST typically <300–500 U/L); Mallory-Denk bodies on liver biopsy
Hereditary Hemochromatosis ("Bronze Diabetes") 🔥 x6 Autosomal recessive HFE gene mutation; iron overload triad: Cirrhosis + Bronze skin hyperpigmentation + Diabetes mellitus (± pseudogout, testicular atrophy) Initial screening: Transferrin Saturation (>45–50%) + Serum Ferritin (>500–600 ng/mL); Treatment: therapeutic phlebotomy
Budd-Chiari Syndrome x2 Hepatic venous outflow obstruction, classically triggered by myeloproliferative neoplasms (Polycythemia Vera, JAK2 mutation) Clinical triad: Severe RUQ pain + tender hepatomegaly + intractable ascites; confirm via Doppler ultrasound of hepatic veins
Amoebic vs. Pyogenic Liver Abscess x2 Pyogenic abscess (biliary origin) requires image-guided drainage + IV antibiotics; Amoebic Abscess (E. histolytica, "anchovy paste" fluid) Responds definitively to Oral Metronidazole alone (routine percutaneous drainage is NOT required unless imminent rupture)
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص استقصاء الاستسقاء النضحي والالتصاقات (سؤال Q337):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: عند شابة تبلغ 25 عاماً تعاني من نقص وزن واستسقاء متزايد مع التصاقات بريتونية على السونار (شبهة Tuberculous Peritonitis أو Peritoneal Carcinomatosis)، فإن الفحص التشخيصي الأعلى حساسية ودقة قطعية (>95%) هو Diagnostic Laparoscopy with peritoneal biopsy (Option B).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option D (Ascitic fluid cytology) باعتبار البزل التشخيصي (Diagnostic Paracentesis) هو الخطوة التمهيدية الأولى الأقل تداخلاً (Least invasive initial bedside test).

Topic 9 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

9. Jaundice & Hyperbilirubinemia

⚡ Moderate (10×) 4 Qs

1. Unconjugated Hyperbilirubinemia Comparison 🔥 x10

Feature Gilbert Syndrome x3 G6PD Deficiency Hemolysis 🔥 x5 Post-Viral Hemolysis x2
Mechanism & Clinical Trigger Mild hereditary reduction in UGT1A1 enzyme activity (~30% of normal); episodic mild scleral icterus provoked by fasting, dehydration, viral illness, or physical exertion X-linked recessive enzyme deficiency; acute hemolytic crisis triggered by oxidative stress: fava beans, TMP-SMX (sulfa drugs), primaquine, or infections Pure indirect hyperbilirubinemia following acute viral infection in the absence of intrinsic liver disease (Total Bilirubin 4.0, Direct 0.3 mg/dL)
Hemoglobin & Liver Function Tests Normal Hemoglobin, normal reticulocyte count; completely normal ALT, AST, ALP, and albumin; zero evidence of hemolysis Anemia (Hb <10 g/dL), elevated reticulocyte count, elevated LDH, decreased haptoglobin; normal AST/ALT/ALP; Heinz bodies on peripheral smear Transient unconjugated hyperbilirubinemia; select Hemolysis as the underlying pathophysiologic etiology
Management & Prognosis Reassurance only; completely benign genetic variant requiring no pharmacologic intervention or activity restriction Discontinue offending oxidative drug; supportive hydration; blood transfusion if severe anemia with hemodynamic compromise Supportive monitoring; spontaneous resolution following recovery from the inciting viral infection

Topic 10 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

10. Inflammatory Bowel Disease

🔥 High Yield (86×) 20 Qs

1. Crohn Disease vs. Ulcerative Colitis Comparison 🔥 x59

Feature Crohn Disease (CD) 🔥 x35 Ulcerative Colitis (UC) 🔥 x19
Anatomical Distribution Any part of GI tract from mouth to anus (terminal ileum most common); characteristically exhibits discontinuous "skip lesions" with rectal sparing 🔥 x6 Confined to the colon and rectum; starts in the rectum and extends proximally in a continuous, unbroken pattern
Depth & Histology Transmural inflammation; non-caseating granulomas, cobblestone mucosal appearance, creeping mesenteric fat Mucosal and submucosal inflammation only; Crypt Abscesses and architectural distortion on mucosal biopsy 🔥 x10
Clinical Presentation Crampy RLQ pain, chronic non-bloody or semi-formed diarrhea, weight loss, oral aphthous ulcers, tender palpable RLQ mass 🔥 x17 Recurrent episodes of bloody diarrhea with mucus, tenesmus, and lower abdominal cramping → confirm via Colonoscopy & mucosal biopsy 🔥 x19
Barium Radiology Sign "String Sign of Kantor": Marked luminal narrowing of the terminal ileum due to spasm and fibrotic stenosis 🔥 x17 "Lead-Pipe" Colon: Loss of colonic haustrations with diffuse mucosal granularity and luminal rigidity

2. Intestinal & Cutaneous Complications of IBD 🔥 x27

  • Crohn Disease Transmural Complications: 🔥 x19
    • Fibrotic Strictures: Recurrent episodes of small-bowel obstruction (colic, distension, vomiting). 🔥 x5
    • Fistulas & Abscesses: Enterocutaneous, enterovesical, and perianal fistulas; chronic inflammatory RLQ mass. 🔥 x4
    • TPN-Associated Tetany in Crohn Fistula: Patient on total parenteral nutrition developing tetany with normal calcium, potassium, and pH → Caused by Hypomagnesemia (serum Mg 1.2 mg/dL). 🔥 x9
  • Ulcerative Colitis — Toxic Megacolon: x2
    • Fulminant complication: Sudden cessation of diarrhea, massive abdominal distension, high fever, tachycardia, shock + transverse colon diameter >6 cm on plain abdominal radiograph → Medical emergency: NPO, IV steroids, broad-spectrum Abx; urgent colectomy if non-responsive.
  • Extraintestinal Cutaneous Manifestations: 🔥 x6
    • Erythema Nodosum: Tender, erythematous, non-ulcerating subcutaneous nodules over the anterior shins (parallels bowel disease activity). 🔥 x5
    • Pyoderma Gangrenosum: Painful, necrolytic pustules rapidly progressing to deep violaceous ulcers with undermined borders on lower extremities → Treat with Systemic Corticosteroids (never surgically debride, as trauma induces pathergy!). x1
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص الكتلة والناسور بعد جراحة سابقة (سؤال Q253):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: ظهور كتلة مزمنة متصلبة في الحفرة الحرقفية اليمنى (Right Iliac Fossa) مع ناسور يفرز صديداً (Draining sinus with pus / sulfur granules) على أثر ندبة جراحية سابقة في البطن يعتبر العرض الجراحي الكلاسيكي لـ Actinomycosis (Option D) التي تنشأ بعد جراحات الأمعاء والزائدة.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option C (Crohn's disease) باعتبارها انتكاسة للمرض المعوي الأصلي وتكوّن ناسور معوي جلدي (Enterocutaneous Fistula).

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 11 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

11. Irritable Bowel Syndrome & Malabsorption

⚡ Medium Yield (35×) 14 Qs

1. Irritable Bowel Syndrome (IBS) 🔥 x17

  • Rome Criteria for Diagnosis: Recurrent abdominal pain or discomfort present on average at least 1 day per week in the last 3 months, associated with ≥2 of the following criteria: 🔥 x14
    • Related to defecation: Pain improved or altered with bowel movements. 🔥 x6
    • Associated with a change in stool frequency: Alteration in number of bowel movements per day/week.
    • Associated with a change in stool form / appearance: Alternating diarrhea (Bristol 6–7) and constipation (Bristol 1–2).
  • Absence of Organic Alarm Features (Red Flags): Absence of unintentional weight loss, rectal bleeding, anemia, fever, family history of colorectal cancer/IBD, and strictly NO nocturnal awakening or diarrhea. x2
  • First-Line Management: Patient education, reassurance, dietary modification (soluble fiber supplementation, low-FODMAP diet), and regular physical exercise. x1

2. Malabsorption, Osmotic Diarrhea & Zinc Deficiency 🔥 x18

Condition Clinical Trigger & Presentation Diagnostic Hallmark & Action
Small-Bowel Mucosal Malabsorption (Celiac / Whipple) 🔥 x4 Chronic fatty diarrhea (steatorrhea), abdominal distension, severe weight loss, and fat-soluble vitamin deficiencies (A, D, E, K) Definitive test: Upper Endoscopy with Small-Intestinal Mucosal Biopsy (villous blunting/atrophy in Celiac; PAS-positive macrophages in Whipple)
Lactose Intolerance x3 Abdominal bloating, audible borborygmi, cramping, and explosive watery osmotic diarrhea occurring shortly after consuming dairy products Definitive confirmation & primary management: Empirical trial of a strict lactose-free diet
Sorbitol / Osmotic Diarrhea x2 Painless watery diarrhea, bloating, and excess flatus in patients chewing sugar-free gum or consuming diet diabetic candies Pathophysiology: Ingestion of non-absorbable carbohydrates creating an osmotic gradient that draws water into the intestinal lumen
Blind-Loop Syndrome (SIBO) x2 Chronic watery diarrhea and 7-kg weight loss developing months after antrectomy with Billroth II reconstruction Bacterial stasis and overgrowth within the stagnant afferent jejunal loop leading to bile salt deconjugation and malabsorption
Zinc Deficiency (Acrodermatitis Enteropathica) 🔥 x7 Patient on prolonged Total Parenteral Nutrition (TPN) lacking trace elements presenting with clinical triad: Chronic diarrhea + Alopecia + Periorificial and acral dermatitis Diagnosis: Low plasma zinc level; Treatment: Parenteral or oral Zinc replacement therapy
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Verified Recommendation

تنبيهات هامة للامتحان بخصوص متلازمة القولون العصبي (IBS):

1. معايير روما لتشخيص IBS (سؤال Q281 و Q282): المعايير الدولية الحديثة (Rome Criteria) تنص على وجود ألم بطني متكرر مرتبط بالتبرز (Pain related to defecation). في تجميعات د. أحمد عامر وبنك الأسئلة تم اعتماد Option D (Pain with defecation)، بينما الـ AI Recommended Answer يوضح أن المعيار الأصلي يربط بين عتبة الألم الأسبوعي والتغير في التبرز.

2. التشخيص الأقل احتمالاً للإسهال المزمن لمدة 5 سنوات (سؤال Q334): مريضة تعاني من إسهال وغازات لمدة 5 سنوات دون أي علامات خطورة. سريرياً Viral Gastroenteritis (Option E) هو الأقل احتمالاً على الإطلاق لأنه مرض حاد ينتهي خلال أيام، بينما الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة اختار Option A (Inflammatory bowel disease) استناداً إلى سلبية التحاليل وغياب المؤشرات الالتهابية.

3. الأعراض الليلية Red Flags (سؤال Q335): استيقاظ المريض من النوم ليلاً بسبب الإسهال أو الألم (Nocturnal symptoms) هو علامة خطر عضوية (Red Flag) تنفي تماماً أن يكون المرض وظيفياً مثل IBS! ومع ذلك فإن الـ Model Answer في الامتحان اختار بالخطأ Option D (Nocturnal symptoms) كعرض مرافق للمرض.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 12 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

12. Acute Appendicitis & Small Bowel Emergencies

🔥 High Yield (82×) 13 Qs

1. Appendicitis vs. Meckel Diverticulitis vs. Intussusception 🔥 x36

Feature Acute Appendicitis 🔥 x23 Meckel Diverticulitis 🔥 x5 Pediatric Intussusception 🔥 x8
Patient & Presentation Visceral periumbilical pain shifting within 12–24h to somatic McBurney's point tenderness in RLQ; anorexia, low fever; Rovsing sign (RLQ pain on LLQ palpation) x1 Young child or adult with history of painless massive rectal bleeding now presenting with acute RLQ abdominal pain and fever mimicking appendicitis Infant (6–12 months) with paroxysms of severe colicky abdominal pain (screaming, drawing legs up), vomiting, and sausage-shaped abdominal mass in RUQ
Pathophysiology & Signs Lumen obstruction by fecalith; if perforated: diffuse peritonitis and pelvic free pus / fluid on US 🔥 x18; misdiagnosed initially in kids as Acute Gastroenteritis 🔥 x4 Persistence of omphalomesenteric (vitelline) duct; ectopic gastric mucosa secretes acid causing adjacent ileal ulceration Invagination of ileum into colon (ileocolic); bowel ischemia produces mucosal sloughing with dark red "currant-jelly" stools
Diagnostic Hallmark & Action Graded-compression Ultrasound or CT; urgent Appendectomy Technetium-99m pertechnetate scan (Meckel Scan) → Surgical resection of the diverticulum Ultrasound shows "Target / Doughnut sign" → First-line therapeutic reduction: Air or Contrast Enema

2. Bowel Obstruction, Paralytic Ileus & Mesenteric Ischemia 🔥 x46

Feature Acute Mesenteric Ischemia 🔥 x8 Chronic Mesenteric Ischemia 🔥 x13
Clinical Presentation Elderly patient with history of Atrial Fibrillation or recent MI; sudden catastrophic abdominal pain markedly out of proportion to physical exam findings; rapid lactic acidosis Older patient with generalized atherosclerosis and smoking history; severe postprandial dull epigastric pain occurring 30–60 minutes after eating, resulting in "fear of food" (sitophobia) and marked weight loss
Diagnostic Hallmark & Action Emergency CT Angiography of the abdomen → emergency laparotomy, surgical embolectomy, and resection of non-viable bowel Confirm with Mesenteric Angiography (demonstrating severe narrowing in celiac and superior mesenteric arteries) → endovascular stenting or surgical bypass
  • Mechanical SBO vs. Postoperative Paralytic Ileus: 🔥 x25
    • Mechanical Small-Bowel Obstruction (SBO): Colicky pain, bilious vomiting, abdominal distension, obstipation, high-pitched "tinkling" bowel sounds; abdominal X-ray: multiple dilated small-bowel loops (>3 cm) with air-fluid levels. (Most common cause: postoperative adhesions). x3
    • Postoperative Paralytic Ileus: Absent or hypoactive bowel sounds, diffuse painless distension; first-line management = Nasogastric Tube Decompression, NPO, and IV fluid support. 🔥 x11
    • High-Output Proximal Jejunal Fistula Fluid Replacement: High volume and rich in electrolytes (sodium, potassium, bicarbonate); optimal replacement IV solution is Lactated Ringer's Solution. 🔥 x11
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 13 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

13. Diverticular Disease

⚡ Medium Yield (37×) 8 Qs

1. Diverticular Disease Spectrum 🔥 x37

Feature Uncomplicated Diverticulosis x3 Acute Sigmoid Diverticulitis 🔥 x14 Diverticular Pelvic Abscess 🔥 x20
Clinical Presentation Most common in sigmoid colon; asymptomatic or chronic constipation and painless bright red hematochezia; zero inflammatory signs "Left-Sided Appendicitis": Acute severe constant Left Lower Quadrant (LLQ) pain, localized guarding, fever (>38°C) + leukocytosis Complication of acute diverticulitis: Persistent LLQ pain (≥5–10 days), spiking high-grade fever (>39°C), and a tender palpable LLQ / pelvic mass
Diagnostic Hallmark Colonoscopy or barium enema (incidentally noted mucosal outpouchings) Abdominal/Pelvic CT with IV contrast: Sigmoid wall thickening & pericolic fat stranding (Colonoscopy is contraindicated acutely due to perforation risk!) Abdominal/Pelvic CT: Encapsulated fluid collection with air-fluid levels in the pericolic or pelvic space
Primary Management High-fiber diet (25–35 g/day), adequate hydration, regular exercise Bowel rest + IV fluids + broad-spectrum IV antibiotics covering enteric gram-negative bacilli and anaerobes (e.g., Ciprofloxacin + Metronidazole) Broad-spectrum IV antibiotics + CT-Guided Percutaneous Catheter Drainage 🔥 x12 (surgical resection if percutaneous route is inaccessible or fails)
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص تشخيص كتلة الـ Left Lower Quadrant (سؤال Q373):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: مريض مسن يبلغ 70 عاماً يعاني من ألم في الحفرة الحرقفية اليسرى منذ 5 أسابيع مع نقص وزن وحرارة خفيفة وكتلة مؤلمة في LLQ. سريرياً يمكن أن تنجم هذه الكتلة عن Colonic Carcinoma متقدم أو Pelvic/Pericolic Diverticular Abscess.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option E (Pelvic abscess) باعتباره الخراج الحوضي/حول القولون الناجم عن مضاعفات Diverticulitis المتكررة.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 14 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

14. Colorectal Polyps & Cancer

🔥 High Yield (75×) 15 Qs

1. Peutz-Jeghers Syndrome & Colorectal Carcinoma 🔥 x75

  • Peutz-Jeghers Syndrome (PJS): 🔥 x9
    • Autosomal dominant STK11/LKB1 mutation. Clinical triad: Multiple gastrointestinal hamartomatous polyps + mucocutaneous hyperpigmented melanin macules on lips, perioral skin & buccal mucosa.
    • Complications & Management: Risk of recurrent small-bowel intussusception and GI bleeding; increased risk of GI and extra-intestinal malignancies; managed with surveillance and endoscopic polypectomy.
  • Colorectal Carcinoma (CRC) Clinical Presentation: 🔥 x31
    • Right-Sided (Ascending) Colon Cancer: Tends to be bulky and exophytic; presents with occult bleeding, microcytic iron deficiency anemia, fatigue, and weight loss; bowel obstruction is uncommon.
    • Left-Sided (Descending / Sigmoid) Colon Cancer: Tends to be infiltrating and constricting ("napkin-ring"); presents with progressive change in bowel habits (alternating constipation and diarrhea), colicky lower abdominal pain, hematochezia / dark blood mixed with mucus, partial obstruction, or a palpable Left Iliac Fossa (LIF) mass. 🔥 x13
  • Mandatory Indications for Colonoscopy (Gold Standard): 🔥 x27
    • Any adult (>45–50y) presenting with an unexplained recent change in bowel habits lasting >3–4 weeks. 🔥 x13
    • Unexplained rectal bleeding or blood mixed with stool. 🔥 x8
    • Positive Fecal Occult Blood Test (FOBT / FIT): Mandatory indication for full colonoscopy (never repeat FOBT or check CEA first!). x2
    • First-Degree Relative with CRC Diagnosed at Age <60: Begin screening colonoscopy at age 40 (or 10 years younger than the earliest diagnosed relative). 🔥 x7
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص النزيف السفلي الحاد مع عدم الاستقرار (سؤال Q371):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: عند حدوث نزيف شرجي حاد مستمر مع صدمة وعدم استقرار في العلامات الحيوية (BP 90/60 و HR 120)، يصعب تحضير القولون بالمسهلات (Bowel Prep)، ويكون الفحص الأولي لتحديد موضع النزيف النشط هو Radioactive labeled RBCs study (Option E) أو CT Angiography.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اعتماد Option B (Colonoscopy) كخطوة أولى مباشرة بعد الإنعاش الأولي بالسوائل والدم.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 15 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

15. Anorectal Disorders

⚡ Medium Yield (37×) 9 Qs

1. Benign Anorectal Disorders & Anal Canal Carcinoma 🔥 x37

Condition Clinical Presentation & Grading Definitive Management
Internal Hemorrhoids (Piles) 🔥 x21 Origin above dentate line; painless bright red drops coating the stool or in toilet bowl:
• Grade I: Bleed without prolapse.
• Grade II: Prolapse on defecation, reduce spontaneously.
• Grade III: Prolapse on defecation, require manual reduction.
• Grade IV: Permanently prolapsed, irreducible manually.		 • Grades I–II: Conservative: High-fiber diet, stool softeners, sitz baths, rubber band ligation.
• Grades III–IV: Definitive Surgical Hemorrhoidectomy (excisional surgery).
Pediatric Anal Fissure x2 Toddler (3y) screaming and crying during defecation + streaks of bright red blood on stool surface + sentinel skin tag at anal verge Underlying root cause is Functional Constipation with hard stools → Stool softeners (Polyethylene glycol), dietary fiber, warm sitz baths
Rectal Prolapse 🔥 x8 Full-thickness concentric protrusion of rectal wall through anus; severe chronic constipation and excessive straining; can occur on exam even in young adults (27y) Pelvic floor rehabilitation, high-fiber diet, avoiding straining; surgical rectopexy if persistent or full-thickness
Squamous Cell Carcinoma of Anal Canal 🔥 x6 Ulcerated, indurated, bleeding 2–3 cm mass at the anal verge or anal canal; strongly associated with Human Papillomavirus (HPV types 16 & 18) Primary definitive treatment: Combined Chemoradiotherapy (Nigro Protocol: 5-FU + Mitomycin C + Radiation)
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص علاج البواسير المتدلية غير المرتجعة (سؤال Q374):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: مريض يعاني من نزيف شرجي غير مؤلم مع تدلي نسيجي دائم لا يمكن إرجاعه يدوياً (Grade IV Internal Hemorrhoids). العلاج الجذري القطعي للبواسير من الدرجة الرابعة هو جراحي Treatment is mainly surgical (Option C).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option E (A high-residue diet should be advised for those patients) كإجراء وقائي تحفظي أولي أساسي لجميع مرضى البواسير.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 16 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

16. Gastroenteritis & Enteric Infections

🔥 High Yield (74×) 20 Qs

1. Enteric Pathogens & Diarrheal Syndromes 🔥 x40

Infection / Organism Clinical Clues & Risk Factors Diagnostic Hallmark First-Line Treatment
Typhoid Fever (Salmonella typhi) 🔥 x14 Step-ladder fever, relative bradycardia (Faget sign), coated tongue, rose spots, hepatosplenomegaly Week 1: Blood Culture | Week 2–3: Stool culture; complication: Ileal Perforation x1 Fluoroquinolones (Ciprofloxacin) or Ceftriaxone 🔥 x11
C. difficile Colitis 🔥 x7 Watery diarrhea & cramps after Clindamycin or cephalosporins; elderly, hospitalized Endoscopy: Yellow-white pseudomembranous mucosal plaques; stool toxin PCR 🔥 x4 Oral Vancomycin 125 mg QID (or Fidaxomicin); environmental disinfection: 1:10 Bleach (Sodium hypochlorite) x2
Giardiasis (G. lamblia) x2 ≥2 weeks of foul-smelling, non-bloody watery diarrhea, greasy stools, abdominal distension, sulfur burps Stool microscopy: Pear-shaped flagellated trophozoites / cysts; duodenal aspirate Oral Metronidazole (250–500 mg TID for 5–7 days)
Traveler's Diarrhea 🔥 x12 Watery diarrhea, cramps & nausea after travel to developing countries; most common: ETEC x1 Diagnostic confirmation: Stool Culture / Analysis 🔥 x6 Ciprofloxacin (covers Salmonella, Shigella, Yersinia, Vibrio, ETEC) ± Azithromycin 🔥 x5
Mild Foodborne Gastroenteritis 🔥 x5 Afebrile watery diarrhea lasting 2–3 days following ingestion of contaminated poultry Self-limiting foodborne enterotoxin disease Oral Rehydration Solution (ORS) & supportive care 🔥 x4

2. Pediatric Diarrheal Dehydration, Shock & Acid-Base Status 🔥 x34

  • Decompensated Hypovolemic Shock in Infantile Diarrhea (Q414): Infant (6m) with profuse diarrhea for 2 days, lethargic, sunken fontanelle, cold extremities, capillary refill ≥5 seconds, tachycardia (HR 190 bpm), hypotension (BP 58/35 mmHg), and anuria → Immediate emergency intervention: IV Isotonic Fluid Bolus: 0.9% Normal Saline 20 mL/kg over 10–20 minutes. 🔥 x17
  • Acid-Base Disorder in Severe Diarrhea: Massive loss of bicarbonate (HCO3−) and water in stool produces Normal Anion Gap (Hyperchloremic) Metabolic Acidosis. 🔥 x17
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيهات هامة للامتحان بخصوص العدوى المعوية (Enteric Infections):

1. علاج عدوى Clostridioides difficile (سؤال Q274): التوصيات الدولية الحديثة (IDSA/ACG Guidelines) توصي بـ Oral Vancomycin (Option C) أو Fidaxomicin كخط أول لعلاج C. difficile، بينما الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة يتبع الدلائل الإرشادية القديمة ويختار Oral Metronidazole (Option D).

2. الفحص التشخيصي لإسهال المسافرين Traveler's Diarrhea (سؤال Q381): مسافر عائد يعاني من إسهال مائي لمدة أسبوع. علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer الفحص التأكيدي لتحديد البكتيريا الممرضة هو Stool culture (Option A)، بينما في الـ Model Answer وتجميعات د. أحمد عامر تم اعتماد Option E (Stool analysis) كفحص براز أولي روتيني للمسافرين.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 17 of 17 • Internal Medicine • GIT, Liver & Infection

17. Systemic & Surgical Infections

🔥 High Yield (73×) 24 Qs

1. Systemic Viral, Zoonotic & Nosocomial Infections 🔥 x39

Feature Infectious Mononucleosis (EBV) 🔥 x17 Brucellosis (Undulant Fever) 🔥 x8
Patient & Clinical Clues Adolescent / young adult (14y); triad: fever, exudative tonsillitis, posterior cervical and generalized lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly Livestock farmer / dairy worker; prolonged undulant fever, drenching night sweats, severe arthralgia, hepatosplenomegaly, sacroiliitis
Diagnostic Hallmark & Rule Lymphocytosis with ≥10% Atypical Lymphocytes + (+) Monospot test (heterophile antibodies); Rule: Avoid contact sports for 3–4 weeks (prevents splenic rupture!) Confirmation: Standard Tube Agglutination Test (SAT) serology; Treatment: Doxycycline + Rifampin (or Streptomycin) for 6 weeks
  • ICU & Device-Related Nosocomial Infections: 🔥 x15
    • Most Common Nosocomial Pathogens in ICU (≥48h): Aerobic Gram-Negative Bacilli (Pseudomonas aeruginosa, Klebsiella pneumoniae, Acinetobacter). 🔥 x9
    • TPN Central Line Infection: New spiking fever in a patient with a central line → Immediate catheter removal + blood cultures + empiric broad-spectrum IV antibiotics. x2
    • Severe Sepsis & MODS: Systemic hyperinflammatory response driven primarily by Interleukin-6 (IL-6) and Tumor Necrosis Factor-alpha (TNF-α); frequently complicated by ARDS. 🔥 x4
    • Suspected Meningococcal Septicemia: If empiric pre-hospital IM Benzylpenicillin was administered, blood cultures may be sterilized → Confirm diagnosis via whole-blood PCR for meningococcus. 🔥 x7

2. Wound Infections, Gas Gangrene, Rabies & Syphilis 🔥 x16

  • Pseudomonas Wound Infection: Blue-green purulent exudate (pyocyanin) with a characteristic sweet, fruity odor in burn wounds or chronic ulcers → Treat with antipseudomonal beta-lactams: Ceftazidime. 🔥 x5
  • Gas Gangrene (Clostridium perfringens): Anaerobic gram-positive spore-forming rod causing acute myonecrosis; clinical hallmarks: severe pain, crepitus, dishwater drainage, gas in soft tissues on X-ray; mediated by Exotoxins (Alpha-toxin / Lecithinase), NOT endotoxins! → Immediate emergency surgical debridement + high-dose IV penicillin + clindamycin. 🔥 x5
  • Wild Animal Bite Rabies Prophylaxis: High-risk bite (bat, fox, raccoon) → Immediate thorough wound cleansing with soap/water + Infiltrate Human Rabies Immune Globulin (HRIG) into and around the wound site + 4-dose rabies vaccine series. x1
  • Primary Syphilis (Treponema pallidum): Painless, clean-based, indurated genital ulcer (hard chancre) associated with non-tender regional lymphadenopathy. x2

3. Tetanus Wound Prophylaxis Protocol x3

Immunization History Clean, Minor Wound Dirty / Tetanus-Prone Wound
Fully Immunized (≥3 Doses), Last Booster <5 Years Ago No vaccine or TIG required x3 No vaccine or TIG required
Fully Immunized (≥3 Doses), Last Booster 5–10 Years Ago No vaccine or TIG required Administer Tetanus Toxoid (Td / Tdap) booster only
Fully Immunized (≥3 Doses), Last Booster >10 Years Ago Administer Tetanus Toxoid booster only Administer Tetanus Toxoid booster only
Incomplete (<3 Doses) or Unknown Immunization Administer Tetanus Toxoid vaccine (complete 3-dose series) Administer BOTH Tetanus Toxoid vaccine AND Tetanus Immunoglobulin (TIG)

4. Surgical, Environmental & Safeguarding Emergencies 🔥 x18

  • Abdominal Trauma & Burn Malignancy: 🔥 x6
    • Abdominal Compartment Syndrome: Sustained intra-abdominal pressure (IAP) >20–25 mmHg with new organ dysfunction (oliguria, high peak airway pressures) → Immediate Surgical Decompressive Laparotomy. x3
    • Stable Blunt Liver Trauma (Grade III Laceration): Hemodynamically stable patient → Non-operative management in SICU with serial hemoglobin monitoring. x1
    • Marjolin Ulcer: Highly aggressive squamous cell carcinoma arising in chronic non-healing burn scars with everted, indurated borders → Confirm via Full-Thickness Biopsy of the Ulcer Edge. x2
  • Heat Illness Spectrum: 🔥 x7
    • Heat Stroke (Medical Emergency): Child trapped in enclosed car; core body temperature >40.5°C (41.2°C), altered mental status / coma, hot dry skin, hypotension → Immediate rapid evaporative / ice-water cooling + IV isotonic fluids. x2
    • Heat Exhaustion: Elevated core temperature (38–40°C), heavy sweating, dehydration, completely normal neurological status → Move to shade, remove clothing, evaporative fan cooling, oral/IV rehydration. 🔥 x5
  • Safeguarding & Non-Accidental Injury (Child Abuse): x2
    • Domestic Abuse: Adult patient with unexplained injuries → Interview the patient alone in private and provide confidential local support resources. x1
    • Child Physical Abuse (Q303): Toddler (2y) with skull fracture attributed to low fall from sofa, presenting with multiple bruises of varying ages on back/buttocks and circular punched-out cigarette burn scar on palm → Immediate notification of Child Protective Services (CPS) / hospital child safeguarding team. x1
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Verified Recommendation

تنبيهات هامة للامتحان بخصوص العدوى الجهازية والجراحية:

1. تشخيص الـ Infectious Mononucleosis (سؤال Q298): شاب عمره 14 عاماً يعاني من حمى وتضخم غدد ليمفاوية في الرقبة والمنطقة الإربية مع Exudative tonsillitis وتضخم طحال مع 10% Atypical lymphocytes. هذه هي الصورة النمطية لـ Infectious Mononucleosis (Option A)، بينما الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة يحتوي على خطأ إدخال واضح بتحديد Option C (Tuberculosis).

2. النقطة التشريحية لسحب شريان الفخذ ABG Sampling (سؤال Q511): تشريحياً وجراحياً، يقع نبض Femoral Artery عند Mid-Inguinal Point وهي النقطة الواقعة في منتصف المسافة بين Anterior Superior Iliac Spine (ASIS) و Symphysis Pubis (Option A). بينما الـ Model Answer في الامتحان اختار Option B (Midway between ASIS and pubic tubercle) والتي تمثل تشريحياً منتصف الرباط الإربي (Midpoint of Inguinal Ligament) وتقع على بعد 1–2 سم جانبياً.

Topic 1 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

1. Iron Deficiency Anemia

🔥 High Yield (28×) 7 Qs

1. Iron Deficiency Anemia Diagnostic Profile & Clinical Clues 🔥 x28

Parameter Iron Deficiency Anemia (IDA) Clinical & Diagnostic Significance
RBC Indices Microcytic, hypochromic anemia (MCV <80 fL, MCH <27 pg) Anisocytosis with markedly elevated RDW (>15%); pencil/cigar cells on blood smear
Iron Studies Profile Low Serum Ferritin (<15–30 ng/mL) + Low Serum Iron + High TIBC (>400 μg/dL) Serum Ferritin is the single most sensitive & specific initial test; Transferrin Saturation <15%
Etiology & Golden Rule • Premenopausal females: Menorrhagia (most common)
• Adult males & postmenopausal females: GI blood loss		 Rule: Unexplained IDA in adult males or postmenopausal females is GI Malignancy (Colorectal Cancer) until proven otherwise → Mandatory Upper & Lower Endoscopy (OGD + Colonoscopy)
Plummer-Vinson Syndrome (Paterson-Kelly) Triad: Iron deficiency anemia + Post-cricoid esophageal web + Dysphagia to solids (± koilonychia, atrophic glossitis, angular cheilitis) High risk of malignant transformation → Premalignant for Squamous Cell Carcinoma of upper esophagus; Treat with iron repletion + endoscopic web dilation

2. Therapeutic Protocol & Treatment Response Monitoring 🔥 x12

  • Oral Iron Therapy (First-Line): Oral Ferrous Sulfate (200 mg TID, providing 65 mg elemental iron per dose) taken on an empty stomach with Vitamin C (ascorbic acid) to maximize intestinal absorption; avoid co-administration with antacids, calcium, or tea/coffee. x3
  • Earliest Indicator of Response: Reticulocytosis peaking at days 7–10 following oral iron initiation (confirms bone marrow response). Hemoglobin normalizes over 6–8 weeks.
  • Duration of Treatment: Continue oral iron therapy for 3–6 months AFTER hemoglobin normalizes to completely replenish depleted tissue iron stores (ferritin stores). x2
  • Indications for IV Iron (Iron Sucrose / Carboxymaltose): Intolerance to oral iron, severe non-compliance, active malabsorption (Celiac disease, post-gastric bypass), or ongoing severe blood loss outstripping oral replacement.
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 2 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

2. Thalassemia

🔥 High Yield (32×) 6 Qs

1. Thalassemia Syndromes: Beta vs. Alpha Comparison 🔥 x32

Feature Beta-Thalassemia Major (Cooley) 🔥 x14 Beta-Thalassemia Minor (Trait) 🔥 x12 Alpha-Thalassemia Syndromes x3
Genetics & Onset Homozygous β0/β0 or β+/β+; clinical onset at 6–9 months of age (when protective HbF declines) Heterozygous β/β0 or β/β+; lifelong mild asymptomatic carrier state Gene deletions on chromosome 16 (α-globin genes 1 to 4 deletions); clinical onset in utero or at birth
Clinical Presentation Severe anemia, failure to thrive, "Chipmunk facies" / frontal bossing (extramedullary hematopoiesis), massive hepatosplenomegaly, jaundice Asymptomatic; normal physical examination; discovered incidentally on routine CBC during premarital or pregnancy screening • 4-gene deletion: Hb Barts (γ4 tetramers) → Hydrops Fetalis (fatal in utero)
• 3-gene deletion: Hb H disease (β4 tetramers)
CBC & Iron Studies Severe microcytic anemia (Hb 3–6 g/dL, MCV <60 fL); Target cells, nucleated RBCs; Serum Ferritin markedly elevated Mild microcytic anemia (Hb 10–11 g/dL, disproportionately low MCV <70 fL); Mentzer Index (MCV/RBC) <13; Normal Ferritin Microcytic hypochromic anemia; fast-migrating Hb H / Hb Barts bands on electrophoresis
Diagnostic Hallmark & Action Hemoglobin Electrophoresis: Markedly elevated HbF (>90%) + elevated HbA2; absent/markedly reduced HbA → Chronic transfusions + iron chelation (Deferasirox) Hemoglobin Electrophoresis: Elevated HbA2 >3.5% (± slightly elevated HbF 1–3%) → Reassurance; Iron therapy is strictly CONTRAINDICATED Hemoglobin electrophoresis ± DNA gene analysis; chronic folate supplementation; splenectomy if severe Hb H
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 3 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

3. Megaloblastic & Pernicious Anemia

🔥 High Yield (24×) 6 Qs

1. Megaloblastic Anemia: Vitamin B12 vs. Folate Deficiency 🔥 x24

Feature Vitamin B12 (Cobalamin) Deficiency 🔥 x14 Folate (Vitamin B9) Deficiency 🔥 x10
Body Stores & Depletion Rate Massive hepatic stores (3–5 mg) lasting 3 to 5 years; develops slowly after years of complete malabsorption Small body stores (5–10 mg) depleted rapidly within 2 to 4 months of dietary deficiency or increased demand
Etiologies & Risk Context Pernicious Anemia (most common), Total/partial gastrectomy, Ileal resection/Crohn's disease, Strict veganism (>5–10y) Poor dietary intake ("tea and toast" diet in elderly, chronic alcoholism), pregnancy, hemolytic anemia, antifolate drugs (Methotrexate, Phenytoin)
Neurological Complications PRESENT (Subacute Combined Degeneration of Spinal Cord): Symmetrical loss of vibration & proprioception, sensory ataxia, spastic paraparesis, positive Babinski sign, peripheral neuropathy, dementia STRICTLY ABSENT: Pure hematologic and epithelial manifestations; zero neurological deficits
Metabolic Biomarkers Elevated Methylmalonic Acid (MMA) AND Elevated Homocysteine NORMAL Methylmalonic Acid (MMA); Elevated Homocysteine only
Blood Smear & Treatment Macrocytic anemia (MCV >100–110 fL) + Hypersegmented Neutrophils (≥5 lobes) → Intramuscular Hydroxocobalamin / Cyanocobalamin Macrocytic anemia + Hypersegmented Neutrophils → Oral Folic Acid (1–5 mg daily) (Always rule out/treat B12 deficiency first!)
  • Pernicious Anemia Pathogenesis & Diagnostic Serology: 🔥 x6
    • Autoimmune destruction of gastric parietal cells → Achlorhydria and failure of Intrinsic Factor (IF) synthesis → B12 malabsorption in terminal ileum.
    • Serologic Testing: Anti-Intrinsic Factor Antibodies (high specificity >98%) and Anti-Parietal Cell Antibodies (high sensitivity ~90%). Associated with increased risk of Gastric Adenocarcinoma (2–3x risk).
    • Critical Treatment Caveat: Treating Vitamin B12 deficiency with folate alone will correct the anemia but allows irreversible subacute combined degeneration of the spinal cord to progress!
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص سبب Macrocytic Anemia في المسنين وسوء التغذية (سؤال Q265):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: سيدة مسنة (82 عاماً) تعاني من سوء تغذية ونقص في الأكل (Poor dietary intake) دون أي أعراض أو علامات عصبية (No focal neurologic findings). مخزون Folate في الكبد ينفد سريعاً خلال 2–4 شهور مع سوء التغذية، بينما مخزون Vitamin B12 يستمر من 3 إلى 5 سنوات ولا ينضب بمجرد سوء التغذية، كما أن نقص B12 يتميز بظهور Subacute Combined Degeneration. ولذلك علمياً الإجابة الأدق لنقص التغذية الصرف هي Folate Deficiency (Option B).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اعتماد Option A (Vitamin B12) كإجابة نموذجية في تجميعات د. أحمد عامر ومفتاح الامتحان القديم.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 4 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

4. Hemolytic Anemia & Sickle Cell Disease

🔥 High Yield (26×) 7 Qs

1. Hemolytic Anemia Diagnostic Workup & Classic Entities 🔥 x26

Entity Pathophysiology & Trigger Diagnostic Hallmark Definitive Management
General Hemolysis Workup 🔥 x6 Premature destruction of RBCs (intravascular vs. extravascular in spleen/liver) ↑ Indirect Bilirubin, ↑ LDH, ↓↓ Serum Haptoglobin, ↑ Reticulocyte count (>2–3%) Identify etiology; supportive folic acid; blood transfusion if severe anemia
Hereditary Spherocytosis 🔥 x8 Autosomal dominant defect in RBC membrane cytoskeleton (Spectrin / Ankyrin) → spherical, rigid RBCs trapped in splenic cords Osmotic Fragility Test (+) / Eosin-5-maleimide (EMA) binding test; Spherocytes without central pallor on smear; Triad: Anemia + Jaundice + Splenomegaly + Gallstones Splenectomy (curative for anemia; delay until age >5y; administer Pneumococcal, Meningococcal, and H. influenzae vaccines preoperatively)
G6PD Deficiency 🔥 x6 X-linked recessive enzyme deficiency; episodic acute intravascular hemolysis after oxidative stress (fava beans, sulfa drugs/TMP-SMX, primaquine, infections) Blood smear: Heinz bodies (denatured Hb precipitates) & Bite cells (degmacytes); Diagnostic: G6PD enzyme assay (measure in steady state!) Avoid oxidative triggers; supportive hydration; transfuse if severe acute hemolysis
Autoimmune Hemolytic Anemia (AIHA) x3 • Warm AIHA: IgG antibodies (37°C, extravascular in spleen; secondary to SLE, CLL)
• Cold AIHA: IgM antibodies (4°C, intravascular; secondary to Mycoplasma, EBV)		 Positive Direct Antiglobulin Test (Direct Coombs Test): detects IgG or C3d on RBC surface; microspherocytes on smear • Warm: Systemic Corticosteroids (Prednisone) ± Rituximab / Splenectomy
• Cold: Keep patient warm; Rituximab (steroids/splenectomy ineffective!)

2. Sickle Cell Disease (HbSS) Acute Crises & Maintenance 🔥 x15

  • Molecular Pathogenesis: Single base mutation (point mutation in β-globin gene substituting valine for glutamic acid at codon 6) producing HbS polymerizing under deoxygenation. x2
  • Acute Painful Vaso-Occlusive Crisis: Microvascular occlusion by sickled erythrocytes → Bone/joint pain; Treatment: Aggressive IV hydration + Analgesia (Opioids) + Supplemental Oxygen + Empirical IV antibiotics if fever ≥38.5°C. 🔥 x5
  • Acute Chest Syndrome (ACS): Leading cause of death; defined as new pulmonary infiltrate on CXR + fever, chest pain, tachypnea, hypoxemia → Emergency: Urgent Simple or Exchange Blood Transfusion + broad-spectrum IV antibiotics + oxygen. x3
  • Infection Risk & Autosplenectomy: Repeated splenic microinfarctions cause functional asplenia by age 3–5 → Extreme susceptibility to encapsulated organisms (Streptococcus pneumoniae sepsis and Salmonella osteomyelitis) → Prophylactic oral penicillin until age 5 + conjugated pneumococcal/meningococcal vaccines. x3
  • Disease-Modifying Therapy: Hydroxyurea increases synthesis of Fetal Hemoglobin (HbF), markedly reducing the frequency and severity of painful vaso-occlusive crises and acute chest syndrome. x2
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 5 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

5. Pancytopenia & Aplastic Anemia

⚡ Medium Yield (14×) 2 Qs

1. Aplastic Anemia Diagnostic Hallmark & Management 🔥 x14

Clinical Parameter Aplastic Anemia Characteristics Clinical & Exam Relevance
Diagnostic Triad (Pancytopenia) Severe Pancytopenia:
• Anemia: Pallor, fatigue, dyspnea (reticulocytopenia <1%)
• Neutropenia: Recurrent bacterial/fungal infections, fever
• Thrombocytopenia: Petechiae, purpura, mucosal bleeding		 Absence of Splenomegaly or Lymphadenopathy (their presence strongly suggests leukemia, lymphoma, or myelodysplastic syndrome instead)
Etiologies & Triggers • Idiopathic Autoimmune: T-cell-mediated destruction of hematopoietic stem cells (>70%)
• Drugs & Toxins: Chloramphenicol, Carbamazepine, gold salts, benzene exposure
• Post-Viral: Seronegative hepatitis, EBV, CMV, Parvovirus B19		 Congenital form: Fanconi Anemia (autosomal recessive; cafe-au-lait spots, short stature, absent/hypoplastic thumbs, microcephaly)
Definitive Bone Marrow Test Bone Marrow Aspiration & Trephine Biopsy: Profoundly hypocellular marrow (<10–20% cellularity) with extensive replacement by fat cells (adipocytes) and fibrous stroma Zero evidence of malignant blasts, dysplastic cells, or abnormal fibrosis
Definitive Management Strategy • Age <40–50y with HLA-matched sibling: Allogeneic Hematopoietic Stem Cell Transplantation (HSCT) (curative in >80%)
• Age >50y or lacking matched donor: Immunosuppressive Therapy: Antithymocyte Globulin (ATG) + Cyclosporine + Eltrombopag		 Supportive: Filtered, irradiated PRBCs and platelet transfusions; prophylactic antifungal & antibacterial coverage
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 6 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

6. Platelet Disorders, ITP, HUS & TTP

🔥 High Yield (26×) 9 Qs

1. Immune Thrombocytopenia (ITP): Pediatric vs. Adult 🔥 x26

Feature Pediatric ITP 🔥 x12 Adult ITP 🔥 x14
Onset & Trigger Sudden, acute onset 1–3 weeks following a viral respiratory or GI infection / childhood vaccination; peak age 2–6 years Insidious, gradual onset; chronic course without preceding viral illness; peak age 20–50 years (female predominance)
Clinical Presentation Sudden appearance of generalized petechiae, ecchymoses, and epistaxis in an otherwise completely healthy, playful child; No splenomegaly, no lymphadenopathy Mucocutaneous bleeding: easy bruising, menorrhagia, gingival bleeding; completely normal physical examination (no hepatosplenomegaly)
Laboratory Profile Isolated Severe Thrombocytopenia (platelets often <20×109/L); completely normal Hb, normal WBC, and normal blood smear (large young platelets); Anti-GPIIb/IIIa antibodies Isolated thrombocytopenia; bone marrow biopsy (indicated in age >60y or atypical features) shows normal or increased megakaryocytes
Management Strategy • Platelets >20k–30k and mild cutaneous bleeding only: Observation & watchful waiting (>80% resolve spontaneously within 6 months)
• Severe mucosal bleeding or platelets <20k: Short course of Oral Corticosteroids or IVIG		 • First-Line Therapy: Systemic Corticosteroids (Oral Prednisone 1 mg/kg or High-Dose Dexamethasone) ± IVIG
• Second-Line / Refractory: TPO-Receptor Agonists (Romiplostim, Eltrombopag), Rituximab, or Splenectomy

2. Thrombotic Microangiopathies: TTP vs. HUS Comparison 🔥 x18

Feature Thrombotic Thrombocytopenic Purpura (TTP) 🔥 x10 Hemolytic Uremic Syndrome (HUS) 🔥 x8
Pathophysiology Severe deficiency of ADAMTS13 metalloprotease (<10%) due to acquired autoantibodies → ultra-large vWF multimers shear RBCs and consume platelets Shiga toxin-producing E. coli (STEC O157:H7) enterotoxin damages glomerular endothelial cells → platelet microthrombi in renal arterioles
Clinical Hallmark Pentad vs. Triad Classic Pentad:
1. MAHA (schistocytes, high LDH, low haptoglobin)
2. Severe Thrombocytopenia (petechiae, purpura)
3. Neurological signs dominant (confusion, seizures, stroke-like)
4. Renal dysfunction (mild-moderate)
5. Fever		 Classic Triad:
1. MAHA (schistocytes on peripheral blood smear)
2. Thrombocytopenia
3. Acute Kidney Injury (AKI dominant): oliguria, uremia, hypertension; typically follows bloody diarrheal prodrome in children
Emergency Management Emergency Plasma Exchange (PEX / Plasmapheresis) + High-dose Corticosteroids ± Caplacizumab.
RULE: Platelet transfusions are STRICTLY CONTRAINDICATED (fuels microvascular thrombosis!)		 Supportive care: Careful IV fluid hydration, correction of electrolyte disturbances, emergency hemodialysis if needed.
Avoid antibiotics and antimotility agents (they increase Shiga toxin release).
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 7 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

7. Von Willebrand Disease & Hemophilia

🔥 High Yield (35×) 10 Qs

1. Coagulation Cascade & Primary vs. Secondary Hemostasis 🔥 x35

Feature Primary Hemostasis Defect (Platelets / vWF) 🔥 x16 Secondary Hemostasis Defect (Coagulation Factors) 🔥 x19
Bleeding Characteristics Mucocutaneous bleeding: Epistaxis, menorrhagia, gingival bleeding, petechiae, purpura, immediate bleeding after minor cuts or dental extraction Deep tissue bleeding: Hemarthrosis (bleeding into joints: knees, elbows), intramuscular hematomas, retroperitoneal hemorrhage, delayed post-surgical bleeding
Laboratory Screening Tests Prolonged Bleeding Time; normal or low platelet count; normal PT/INR; normal or prolonged aPTT (vWD) Prolonged PT/INR and/or prolonged aPTT; normal Bleeding Time; normal platelet count

2. Von Willebrand Disease vs. Hemophilia A & B Comparison 🔥 x28

Disorder Genetics & Factor Defect Diagnostic Lab Profile Specific Management
Von Willebrand Disease (vWD) 🔥 x12 Autosomal Dominant (most common inherited bleeding disorder); deficiency or defect in von Willebrand Factor (vWF) (which mediates platelet-subendothelial adhesion and stabilizes Factor VIII) Prolonged Bleeding Time + Prolonged or normal aPTT (secondary to low Factor VIII); Normal PT/INR; Normal platelet count; Decreased Ristocetin Cofactor Activity • Mild/Type 1: Desmopressin (DDAVP) (stimulates vWF release from Weibel-Palade bodies)
• Severe/Type 2–3: vWF / Factor VIII concentrate; Antifibrinolytics (Tranexamic acid) for dental/mucosal bleeding
Hemophilia A 🔥 x11 X-linked Recessive (affects males; females are carriers); deficiency of Coagulation Factor VIII (~80–85% of hemophilia cases) Isolated Prolonged aPTT; Normal PT/INR; Normal Bleeding Time; Normal platelets; Mixing Study (50:50 mix with normal plasma) corrects aPTT (confirms factor deficiency) • Recombinant Factor VIII concentrate replacement (on-demand and prophylaxis)
• Mild cases: Desmopressin (DDAVP) raises Factor VIII levels
• Avoid IM injections, NSAIDs, and contact sports
Hemophilia B (Christmas Disease) 🔥 x5 X-linked Recessive; deficiency of Coagulation Factor IX (~15% of cases); clinically indistinguishable from Hemophilia A Isolated Prolonged aPTT; Normal PT/INR; Normal Bleeding Time; Normal platelets; Decreased specific Factor IX assay Recombinant Factor IX concentrate replacement; Note: Desmopressin (DDAVP) has NO effect in Hemophilia B!
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 8 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

8. Coagulation Disorders, DIC & Anticoagulation

🔥 High Yield (20×) 6 Qs

1. Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation (DIC) Diagnostic Profile 🔥 x20

Clinical Parameter Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation (DIC) Clinical Significance & Action
Pathophysiology & Triggers Uncontrolled systemic activation of coagulation → widespread microvascular fibrin deposition + secondary hyperfibrinolysis consuming platelets and clotting factors Triggers: Severe Sepsis (Gram-negative endotoxemia), Massive Trauma, Obstetric emergencies (Placental Abruption, Amniotic Fluid Embolism), Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (M3)
Clinical Hallmark Simultaneous thrombosis and hemorrhage: Active oozing from venipuncture/cannulation sites, surgical incisions, and mucosal surfaces + end-organ ischemia (gangrene, AKI) High mortality medical emergency requiring immediate ICU care
Diagnostic Laboratory Quartet 1. Markedly Prolonged PT / INR
2. Prolonged aPTT
3. Severe Thrombocytopenia (<50–100×109/L)
4. Markedly Decreased Fibrinogen (<100–150 mg/dL) + Markedly Elevated D-Dimer / FDPs		 Blood smear demonstrates Schistocytes (fragmented RBCs / helmet cells) secondary to microangiopathic mechanical shearing
Definitive Management • Treat the Underlying Inciting Cause Immediately (IV antibiotics, emergency delivery, source control)
• Targeted Blood Product Replacement:
– Platelet concentrates if platelets <50k with active bleeding
– Fresh Frozen Plasma (FFP) for prolonged PT/aPTT and active bleeding
– Cryoprecipitate if Fibrinogen <100–150 mg/dL		 Routine heparin is strictly avoided unless predominant arterial/venous thromboembolism with minimal bleeding

2. Anticoagulant Pharmacotherapy, Monitoring & Reversal 🔥 x14

Anticoagulant Mechanism of Action Monitoring Parameter Specific Antidote / Reversal
Warfarin (Coumarin) 🔥 x6 Inhibits Vitamin K Epoxide Reductase → blocks carboxylation of Factors II, VII, IX, X, Protein C & Protein S Prothrombin Time (PT) / INR
(Target: 2.0–3.0 for DVT/PE/AF; 2.5–3.5 for mechanical mitral valve)		 • Non-urgent: Oral/IV Vitamin K1 (Phytonadione)
• Major bleeding: 4-Factor Prothrombin Complex Concentrate (PCC) + IV Vitamin K
Unfractionated Heparin (UFH) 🔥 x4 Potentiates Antithrombin III (AT-III) → rapid inactivation of Thrombin (Factor IIa) and Factor Xa Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time (aPTT)
(Target therapeutic range: 1.5–2.5x normal control)		 Protamine Sulfate (1 mg neutralizes ~100 units of circulating unfractionated heparin)
Low-Molecular-Weight Heparin (LMWH) x3 Inactivates Factor Xa >> Factor IIa via Antithrombin III; predictable bioavailability and renal excretion No routine monitoring required; monitor Anti-Factor Xa levels in severe renal impairment (CrCl <30) or pregnancy Partial reversal with Protamine Sulfate; adjust dose in renal insufficiency
Heparin-Induced Thrombocytopenia (HIT) x1 Autoantibodies against Platelet Factor 4 (PF4)-Heparin complexes → platelet activation → consumption + thrombosis >50% drop in platelet count occurring 5–10 days post-heparin + paradoxical new venous/arterial thrombosis Stop all heparin products immediately! Start non-heparin anticoagulant: Direct Thrombin Inhibitor (Argatroban, Bivalirudin) or Fondaparinux
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 9 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

9. Blood Transfusion & Complications

🔥 High Yield (22×) 7 Qs

1. Blood Component Therapy Guidelines 🔥 x12

  • Packed Red Blood Cells (PRBCs): 1 unit increases adult Hemoglobin by ~1 g/dL and Hematocrit by ~3%. Indicated in symptomatic acute anemia or Hb <7 g/dL in stable patients (Hb <8 g/dL in patients with pre-existing acute coronary syndrome or cardiovascular disease). 🔥 x4
  • Platelet Concentrates: 1 apheresis unit increases platelet count by 30,000–50,000/μL. Prophylactic transfusion threshold is <10,000/μL in stable non-bleeding patients; <50,000/μL before major surgery or in actively bleeding patients; <100,000/μL for neurosurgery or ocular surgery. x3
  • Fresh Frozen Plasma (FFP): Contains all coagulation factors. Indicated for reversal of multiple coagulation factor deficiencies with active bleeding (e.g., cirrhosis, massive transfusion, DIC). x3
  • Cryoprecipitate: Concentrated source of Fibrinogen, Factor VIII, Factor XIII, and vWF. Indicated when Fibrinogen is <100–150 mg/dL (DIC, massive obstetric hemorrhage). x2

2. Acute Transfusion Reactions Comparison 🔥 x22

Reaction Timing & Pathophysiology Clinical Hallmark Findings Emergency Action
Acute Hemolytic (AHTR) 🔥 x6 Within minutes (<24h); ABO incompatibility (clerical/identification error); preformed recipient IgM antibodies lyse donor RBCs via complement Sudden fever, chills, flank / loin pain, dyspnea, hypotension, hemoglobinuria (dark red urine), and rapidly progressive DIC / shock STOP TRANSFUSION IMMEDIATELY! Maintain IV access with normal saline; aggressive hydration to maintain urine output >100 mL/h; return blood unit to blood bank for repeat cross-match
Febrile Non-Hemolytic (FNHTR) 🔥 x7 During or within 1–4 hours post-transfusion; recipient antibodies react against donor leukocyte antigens, or cytokines released from leukocytes during storage Mild-to-moderate fever (temperature rise ≥1°C), chills, and rigors WITHOUT hemolysis (normal BP, clear urine, normal LDH/haptoglobin) Stop transfusion temporarily; administer Acetaminophen (antipyretics); Rule out hemolysis; Prevent with Leukoreduced blood products
Allergic / Urticarial Reaction x3 Within minutes to 1 hour; IgE-mediated mast cell degranulation triggered by donor plasma proteins Isolated pruritus, erythema, and urticaria (hives) WITHOUT fever or respiratory distress Pause transfusion; administer Antihistamine (Diphenhydramine); if hives resolve and no anaphylaxis, transfusion may be safely resumed
Transfusion-Related Acute Lung Injury (TRALI) 🔥 x4 Within 6 hours; donor anti-HLA or anti-neutrophil antibodies activate recipient neutrophils in pulmonary capillaries → non-cardiogenic pulmonary edema Sudden severe dyspnea, hypoxemia (PaO2/FiO2 <300), fever, hypotension, and diffuse bilateral pulmonary infiltrates on CXR; Normal JVP & Normal PCWP (<18 mmHg) Leading cause of transfusion-related mortality! Stop transfusion immediately; aggressive respiratory support (supplemental O2 / mechanical ventilation); Diuretics are NOT indicated
Transfusion-Associated Circulatory Overload (TACO) x2 Within 6 hours; rapid infusion of hypervolemic blood products in patients with compromised cardiac or renal reserve Severe dyspnea, tachypnea, hypertension, elevated JVP, bilateral basilar crackles, peripheral edema, elevated BNP / NT-proBNP Stop transfusion; place patient upright with oxygen; administer IV Furosemide (loop diuretics)
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 10 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Hematology

10. Lymphoma & Vascular Purpura

🔥 High Yield (22×) 6 Qs

1. Hodgkin vs. Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma Comparison 🔥 x22

Feature Hodgkin Lymphoma (HL) 🔥 x12 Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma (NHL) 🔥 x10
Epidemiology & Spread Bimodal age distribution (peaks at 15–35 years and >55 years); spreads contiguously along adjacent lymph node chains More common in older adults (>65 years); non-contiguous, unpredictable hematogenous spread with early bone marrow and extranodal involvement
Clinical Presentation Painless, rubbery, firm cervical and supraclavicular lymphadenopathy (75%) ± mediastinal mass; Pel-Ebstein Fevers (cyclical high fevers for 1–2 weeks followed by afebrile intervals); Lymph node pain induced by alcohol ingestion Painless generalized lymphadenopathy; frequent extranodal involvement (GI tract, liver, bone marrow, skin); abdominal distension or bowel obstruction
Systemic B-Symptoms Present in ~40% (defines advanced stage):
1. Unexplained fever >38°C
2. Drenching night sweats
3. Unintentional weight loss >10% within 6 months		 Frequently present in aggressive subtypes (Diffuse Large B-Cell Lymphoma, Burkitt Lymphoma); carries poor prognostic value
Histopathological Hallmark Excisional lymph node biopsy demonstrates pathognomonic Reed-Sternberg Cells (giant binucleated / multinucleated B-cells with prominent "owl-eye" inclusion-like nucleoli, immunopositive for CD15 and CD30) Malignant monoclonal proliferation of B-cells (85%) or T-cells (15%):
• Burkitt Lymphoma: t(8;14) c-myc translocation; "Starry-sky" histology (high mitotic rate + macrophage tingible bodies); linked to EBV

2. Henoch-Schönlein Purpura (IgA Vasculitis) 🔥 x14

  • Etiology & Pathophysiology: Most common systemic small-vessel vasculitis in children (peak age 3–10 years); commonly occurs 1–3 weeks following an Upper Respiratory Tract Infection (Group A Strep, viral); driven by systemic deposition of IgA1-containing immune complexes in dermal, gastrointestinal, and glomerular capillary walls. 🔥 x5
  • Pathognomonic Clinical Tetrad: 🔥 x6
    • 1. Palpable Purpura: Symmetrical, non-thrombocytopenic purpuric lesions distributed predominantly over the buttocks, thighs, and extensor surfaces of lower extremities. (Crucial exam clue: Completely NORMAL platelet count!).
    • 2. Arthralgia / Arthritis: Non-migratory, non-deforming oligopolyarthritis of large weight-bearing joints (knees, ankles).
    • 3. Gastrointestinal Symptoms: Colicky abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, melena / hematochezia, and risk of ileo-ileal intussusception.
    • 4. Renal Involvement (IgA Nephropathy): Microscopic/macroscopic hematuria, mild proteinuria, and red cell casts.
  • Diagnostic Hallmark: Clinical diagnosis; skin punch biopsy demonstrates Leukocytoclastic Vasculitis with prominent IgA and C3 deposition on Direct Immunofluorescence. x2
  • Management & Prognosis: Primarily supportive (hydration, rest, NSAIDs for joint pain). Systemic Corticosteroids (Prednisone) are reserved for severe abdominal colic or acute nephritis. Prognosis is generally excellent, with spontaneous complete resolution in >90% of children. x1

Topic 1 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Nephrology & Urology

1. Nephrotic Syndrome

🔥 High Yield (35×) 10 Qs

1. Nephrotic Syndrome Diagnostic Hallmark & Pathophysiology 🔥 x35

Clinical Parameter Nephrotic Syndrome Spectrum Clinical & Diagnostic Relevance
Diagnostic Tetrad 1. Heavy Proteinuria: >3.5 g/24 hours (>40 mg/m²/hr in children)
2. Hypoalbuminemia: Serum albumin <3.0 g/dL (often <2.0 g/dL)
3. Generalized Edema: Morning periorbital puffiness → dependent pitting edema → anasarca
4. Hyperlipidemia & Lipiduria: Total cholesterol >300–400 mg/dL; "Maltese cross" oval fat bodies under polarized microscopy		 Proteinuria threshold (>3.5 g/day) defines nephrotic syndrome regardless of mild coexisting hypertension or minimal microscopic hematuria (2–3 RBCs/hpf)
Pathophysiologic Mechanisms Disruption of glomerular filtration barrier charge/size selectivity → massive urinary loss of albumin → ↓ oncotic pressure → fluid extravasation + hepatic lipoprotein synthesis Loss of Antithrombin III (AT-III) in urine → Hypercoagulable state (Renal Vein Thrombosis, DVT, PE); loss of immunoglobulins → ↑ infection risk (S. pneumoniae)

2. Major Nephrotic Etiologies: Minimal Change vs. FSGS vs. Membranous 🔥 x28

Condition Patient Demographics & Associations Pathology (LM, IF, EM) Treatment & Prognosis
Minimal Change Disease (MCD) 🔥 x14 Most common cause of nephrotic syndrome in children (2–6 years, >80%); associated with Hodgkin lymphoma or NSAIDs • LM: Normal glomeruli
• IF: Negative (no immune deposits)
• EM: Diffuse effacement / fusion of podocyte foot processes		 First-line: Oral Corticosteroids (Prednisone 60 mg/m²/day); dramatic complete response in >90%; renal biopsy is NOT required initially in children with typical presentation
Focal Segmental Glomerulosclerosis (FSGS) 🔥 x8 Most common cause in African Americans & Hispanic adults; secondary causes: HIV infection, heroin use, massive obesity, sickle cell disease • LM: Segmental sclerosis and hyalinosis in some (<50%) glomeruli
• IF: Non-specific IgM and C3 in sclerotic areas
• EM: Diffuse foot process effacement		 High resistance to corticosteroids; progressive decline to ESRD in 50%; high recurrence rate in kidney allografts (30–50%) post-transplantation
Membranous Nephropathy 🔥 x6 Most common primary cause in non-diabetic Caucasian adults (40–60y); primary: Anti-PLA2R antibodies (85%); secondary: Hepatitis B, SLE (Class V), NSAIDs, solid tumors • LM: Diffuse capillary loop thickening
• IF: Granular IgG & C3 along GBM
• EM: Subepithelial immune deposits with "spike and dome" pattern		 Rule out occult malignancy; ACE inhibitors/ARBs + immunosuppression (Cyclophosphamide + steroids or Rituximab); highest risk of Renal Vein Thrombosis
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيهات هامة للامتحان بخصوص متلازمة Nephrotic Syndrome:

1. تشخيص الـ 24-Hour Proteinuria >3.5 g/day (سؤال Q529): سيدة تبلغ 36 عاماً تعاني من شحوب ووذمة وتورم بالجفون مع إفراز بروتين في البول بمقدار 8 g/day ووجود 2–3 كرات دم حمراء وضغط 180/100. علمياً وطبياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer وجود بروتين يتجاوز 3.5 g/day يحدد قطعياً Nephrotic Syndrome (Option D) (وهي هنا غالباً FSGS أو Membranous لوجود ارتفاع الضغط)، بينما الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة تم تدوينه بالخطأ Option A (Nephritic syndrome) بسبب التركيز على ارتفاع ضغط الدم.

2. تشخيص المتلازمة النفروتية في الأطفال (سؤال Q537): طفل عمره 5 سنوات يعاني من Periorbital edema صباحية مع نقص ألبومين شديد (1.4 g/dL) وارتفاع كولسترول (360 mg/dL) و +++ بروتين وضغط طبيعي 80/45. طبياً السبب المسؤول عن أكثر من 80% من الحالات في هذا العمر هو Minimal Change Disease (Option A)، بينما الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة اختار Option C (Membranous glomerulopathy) وهو مرض يصيب البالغين نادراً ما يظهر في سن 5 سنوات.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 2 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Nephrology & Urology

2. Glomerulonephritis

🔥 High Yield (30×) 11 Qs

1. Nephritic Syndrome vs. Major Glomerulonephritides 🔥 x32

Feature Post-Streptococcal (PSGN) 🔥 x14 IgA Nephropathy (Berger) 🔥 x12 Rapidly Progressive (RPGN) 🔥 x6
Epidemiology & Timing Children (5–12y); latent period of 1–3 weeks post-pharyngitis or 3–6 weeks post-impetigo (skin infection) Young adult males (15–30y); episodic macroscopic hematuria occurring DURING or 1–2 days after an upper respiratory infection (Synpharyngitic); NO latent period! Adults (any age); rapid subacute renal failure progressing to ESRD within days to weeks; associated with vasculitis / Goodpasture
Clinical Presentation Smoky / "tea-colored" or "cola-colored" urine, morning periorbital edema, oliguria, moderate hypertension Recurrent asymptomatic gross hematuria resolving within days; mild proteinuria; normal blood pressure initially Severe nephritic syndrome: gross hematuria, oliguria/anuria, severe hypertension, rapidly rising BUN/Creatinine
Complement Levels (C3/C4) Markedly LOW Serum C3 (normalizes within 6–8 weeks); elevated ASO titer & Anti-DNase B Completely NORMAL Serum C3 & C4; elevated serum IgA levels in 50% Variable: Low in immune-complex RPGN (Lupus, PSGN); Normal in Pauci-Immune ANCA & Anti-GBM
Renal Biopsy Hallmark • LM: Diffuse endocapillary proliferation
• IF: Granular "starry-sky" IgG & C3
• EM: Subepithelial electron-dense "humps"		 • LM: Mesangial cell proliferation
• IF: Dominant mesangial IgA & C3 deposition
• EM: Mesangial immune deposits		 Crescentic Glomerulonephritis: Glomerular crescents (>50% of glomeruli) composed of proliferated parietal epithelial cells, monocytes, and fibrin
Management & Prognosis Supportive care: Sodium & fluid restriction, loop diuretics for fluid overload/HTN; >95% of children achieve full spontaneous recovery ACE inhibitors / ARBs to reduce proteinuria (<1 g/day) and slow progression; corticosteroids if progressive; 20–30% reach ESRD Emergency: High-dose IV Methylprednisolone pulse + Cyclophosphamide ± Plasmapheresis (in Anti-GBM / Goodpasture)
  • Alport Syndrome (Hereditary Nephritis): x2
    • X-linked mutation in COL4A5 gene encoding type IV collagen α5 chain.
    • Clinical Triad: Hereditary nephritis (microscopic hematuria progressing to ESRD) + Bilateral Sensorineural Hearing Loss + Ocular anomalies (anterior lenticonus, macular flecks).
    • Electron Microscopy: Characteristic "basket-weave" alternating thinning and thickening / lamellation of the glomerular basement membrane (GBM).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص تشخيص التهاب كبيبات الكلى الحاد (سؤال Q546):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: طفل عمره 5 سنوات يعاني من Hematuria و Proteinuria و Hypertension، وتظهر الخزعة الكلوية Diffuse proliferation of glomerular cells and immune deposits. هذا الوصف النسيجي التكاثري المنتشر يصف كلاسيكياً Acute Diffuse Proliferative Glomerulonephritis (Option D) (وهو نمط PSGN)، بينما IgA Nephropathy تظهر تكاثراً بؤرياً محصوراً في Mesangium (Mesangial proliferation).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم تسجيل الإجابة Option C (IgA nephropathy) في مفتاح الامتحان.

Topic 3 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Nephrology & Urology

3. Acute Kidney Injury

🔥 High Yield (40×) 15 Qs

1. Acute Kidney Injury (AKI) Differentiation: Prerenal vs. Intrinsic ATN 🔥 x42

Diagnostic Parameter Prerenal Azotemia 🔥 x20 Acute Tubular Necrosis (ATN) 🔥 x22
Pathophysiology & Causes Renal hypoperfusion with intact parenchymal architecture: Hypovolemia, dehydration, hemorrhage, heart failure, sepsis, NSAIDs / ACEi Direct parenchymal tubular epithelial cell injury: Prolonged ischemic shock, nephrotoxins (Aminoglycosides, IV radiocontrast, Myoglobinuria)
BUN : Serum Creatinine Ratio >20 : 1 (enhanced proximal tubular urea reabsorption driven by aldosterone/ADH) 10–15 : 1 (impaired tubular handling; parallel urea and creatinine loss)
Fractional Excretion of Sodium (FENa) <1% (intact tubular reabsorption preserving sodium) >2% (tubular epithelial dysfunction impairs sodium conservation)
Urinary Sodium Concentration <20 mEq/L >40 mEq/L
Urine Specific Gravity & Osmolality Concentrated urine: Specific gravity >1.020; Osmolality >500 mOsm/kg Isosthenuric urine: Specific gravity ~1.010; Osmolality <350 mOsm/kg
Urine Microscopy Hallmark Normal sediment or Hyaline Casts Pathognomonic "Muddy Brown" Granular Casts & renal tubular epithelial cells
Response to Fluid Challenge Prompt normalization of urine output & creatinine within 24–48 hours of IV fluid bolus Oliguria persists despite adequate fluid repletion; requires supportive renal replacement

2. Acute Interstitial Nephritis, Rhabdomyolysis & Dialysis Indications 🔥 x22

  • Drug-Induced Acute Interstitial Nephritis (AIN): 🔥 x8
    • Type IV hypersensitivity reaction caused by medications: Beta-lactam antibiotics (Ampicillin, Methicillin, Cephalosporins), NSAIDs, Sulfonamides, PPIs.
    • Clinical Triad: Fever + Maculopapular cutaneous rash + Arthralgia / Oliguric AKI occurring 1–2 weeks post-drug exposure.
    • Urinalysis Hallmark: Sterile pyuria, White Blood Cell (WBC) casts, and Eosinophiluria (Hansel stain); peripheral eosinophilia. (No heavy proteinuria). Discontinue drug immediately; corticosteroids if severe.
  • Rhabdomyolysis-Induced AKI: 🔥 x6
    • Crush injury, prolonged immobilization, statin myopathy, or extreme exertion → Massive release of myoglobin into circulation → heme pigment nephropathy.
    • Urinalysis Discrepancy: Dipstick is strongly positive for "blood" (heme), but urine microscopy reveals NO red blood cells (0–2 RBCs/hpf).
    • Biomarker: Markedly elevated Serum Creatine Kinase (CK >10,000–50,000 U/L). Treatment: Aggressive IV isotonic saline hydration (target urine output 200–300 mL/h) ± urinary alkalinization with sodium bicarbonate.
  • Absolute Indications for Urgent Hemodialysis (Mnemonic: AEIOU): 🔥 x8
    • A: Severe refractory Acidosis (pH <7.10 despite medical therapy).
    • E: Severe refractory Electrolytes: Hyperkalemia (K >6.5 mEq/L) with ECG changes unresponsive to medical shifting.
    • I: Toxic Ingestions (Methanol, Ethylene glycol, Salicylates, Lithium).
    • O: Fluid Overload: Pulmonary edema refractory to high-dose loop diuretics.
    • U: Symptomatic Uremia: Uremic Pericarditis (pericardial friction rub), Uremic Encephalopathy (asterixis, confusion), or Uremic Bleeding.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص تشخيص التهاب الكلى الخلالي الحاد (سؤال Q563):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: فتاة عمرها 16 عاماً ظهرت عليها حرارة (38.7°C) وطفح جلدي Maculopapular rash مع قلة بول وارتفاع كرياتينين (2.5 mg/dL) بعد أسبوع من تناول Ampicillin لعلاج التهاب الحلق، ويظهر فحص البول White Blood Cell Casts مع Eosinophils كثيرة وغياب تام للبروتين (No proteinuria). هذه الصورة تمثل كلاسيكياً تفاعل فرط حساسية دوائي Acute Interstitial Nephritis (Option A) (وهو نفس السؤال المكرر برقم Q564 المعتمد فيه الخيار A).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: في سؤال Q563 تم تضليل المفتاح بارتفاع بسيط في ASO Titer فسجل الإجابة بالخطأ Option D (Post streptococcal glomerulonephritis) على الرغم من أن البول خالٍ تماماً من البروتين ومليء بالـ Eosinophils والـ WBC casts، وهي علامات AIN خالصة.

Topic 4 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Nephrology & Urology

4. Chronic Kidney Disease

⚡ Medium Yield (18×) 7 Qs

1. Chronic Kidney Disease (CKD) Staging & Complications 🔥 x20

Clinical Domain Pathophysiology & Hallmarks Management & Targets
CKD Definition & Staging Evidence of kidney damage (proteinuria) or eGFR <60 mL/min/1.73m² persisting for ≥3 months:
• Stage 1: GFR ≥90 with kidney damage
• Stage 2: GFR 60–89
• Stage 3a: GFR 45–59 | Stage 3b: GFR 30–44
• Stage 4: GFR 15–29 (prepare for dialysis/access)
• Stage 5: GFR <15 (End-Stage Renal Disease, ESRD)		 Target blood pressure: <130/80 mmHg; first-line renoprotective therapy: ACE Inhibitors / ARBs (reduces intraglomerular pressure & proteinuria) + SGLT2 inhibitors
Renal Osteodystrophy (CKD-MBD) 🔥 x8 ↓ GFR → Phosphate retention (Hyperphosphatemia) + impaired 1α-hydroxylation in kidneys → Calcitriol (↓ 1,25-(OH)2D3) deficiency → Hypocalcemia → Secondary Hyperparathyroidism (↑↑ PTH) → Osteitis fibrosa cystica ("rugger-jersey spine", subperiosteal bone resorption) • Dietary phosphate restriction
• Oral Phosphate Binders: Calcium acetate or Non-calcium binders (Sevelamer) taken with meals
• Active Vitamin D (Calcitriol) to suppress PTH
• Calcimimetics (Cinacalcet) for refractory hyperparathyroidism
Anemia of Chronic Kidney Disease 🔥 x6 Deficiency of Erythropoietin (EPO) produced by peritubular capillary interstitial cells → Normocytic, normochromic anemia developing when GFR falls <30–45 mL/min • Check iron stores first: Ferritin >100–200 ng/mL & Transferrin Saturation >20%
• Erythropoiesis-Stimulating Agents (ESAs: Epoetin alfa, Darbepoetin); Target Hb = 10–11.5 g/dL (never exceed 12–13 g/dL due to thrombosis/CVD risk)

Topic 5 of 10 • Internal Medicine • Nephrology & Urology

5. Diabetic & Renovascular Nephropathy

⚡ Medium Yield (16×) 7 Qs

1. Diabetic Nephropathy Progression & Screening 🔥 x16

  • Natural History & Earliest Marker: 🔥 x6
    • Hyperfiltration → Microalbuminuria (moderately increased albuminuria: Urine Albumin-to-Creatinine Ratio [UACR] 30–300 mg/g) → Overt macroalbuminuria (>300 mg/g) → Progressive decline in GFR.
    • Screening Guidelines: Annually starting at diagnosis for Type 2 Diabetes and 5 years post-diagnosis for Type 1 Diabetes using a spot morning urine sample for UACR.
  • Pathology Hallmark: Glomerular basement membrane thickening and mesangial matrix expansion forming nodular intercapillary glomerulosclerosis: Kimmelstiel-Wilson Nodules (eosinophilic PAS-positive nodules in the glomerular mesangium). x2
  • Management Protocol: Optimal glycemic control (HbA1c <7%) + ACE Inhibitors (Lisinopril) or ARBs (Losartan) (dilate efferent arterioles, reducing intraglomerular capillary hypertension and microalbuminuria) + SGLT2 Inhibitors (Dapagliflozin/Empagliflozin). x3

2. Renovascular Hypertension (Renal Artery Stenosis) 🔥 x14

Feature Atherosclerotic Renal Artery Stenosis (ARAS) 🔥 x8 Fibromuscular Dysplasia (FMD) 🔥 x6
Patient Demographics Elderly males (>55 years) with generalized atherosclerosis (coronary, peripheral vascular, smoking history) Young females (<35 years) with no cardiovascular risk factors
Lesion Location Proximal one-third of the main renal artery (ostial lesion) Distal two-thirds of the renal artery and branches
Angiography Finding Atherosclerotic plaque narrowing renal artery origin Pathognomonic "String of Beads" appearance (medial fibroplasia)
Diagnostic Clues & Action • Severe refractory hypertension on ≥3 antihypertensives
• Systolic-diastolic epigastric/flank abdominal bruit
• >30% acute rise in Serum Creatinine after initiating ACE inhibitor or ARB (loss of compensatory Angiotensin II efferent constriction)
• Screening: Renal Duplex Ultrasound; Definitive: Renal Angiography		 • Severe headache, hypertension, carotid bruits
• Treatment: Percutaneous Transluminal Angioplasty (PTA) WITHOUT stenting (curative in >80% of young females)
• For ARAS: Medical management first; angioplasty with stenting for refractory cases or flash pulmonary edema
