7. Pediatric Respiratory & ENT Disorders

🔥 High Yield (55×) 58 Qs

1. Pediatric Upper Airway Stridor: Croup vs. Epiglottitis vs. Laryngomalacia 🔥 x14

Feature Croup (Laryngotracheobronchitis) 🔥 x4 Acute Epiglottitis 🔥 x8 Congenital Laryngomalacia x2
Causative Pathogen / Mechanism Parainfluenza virus (types 1 & 2) Haemophilus influenzae type b (Hib) in unimmunized children Congenital flaccid supraglottic laryngeal cartilage
Typical Age Group 6 m – 3 years 2 – 6 years Starts at 2–4 w of life (#1 chronic infant stridor)
Classic Clinical Clues Low-grade fever, barking (“seal-like”) cough, hoarseness, nocturnal stridor Abrupt high fever, toxic look, drooling, dysphagia, muffled voice, tripod posture Inspiratory stridor worse supine/feeding/crying and relieved when prone
Diagnostic / X-Ray Sign Subglottic narrowing: “Steeple sign” on AP neck X-ray Swollen epiglottis: “Thumbprint sign” on lateral neck X-ray Flexible laryngoscopy shows omega-shaped (Ω) epiglottis
1st-Line Tx Single-dose Dexamethasone + Nebulized Racemic Epinephrine if stridor at rest Immediate endotracheal intubation in OR + IV Ceftriaxone / Cefotaxime Reassurance; resolves spontaneously by 12–18 m

2. Lower Respiratory Infections: Bronchiolitis vs. Pertussis vs. Lobar Pneumonia 🔥 x25

Feature Acute Viral Bronchiolitis 🔥 x14 Pertussis (Whooping Cough) 🔥 x6 Bacterial Lobar Pneumonia 🔥 x5
Causative Pathogen Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) (#1 winter cause) Bordetella pertussis (Gram-negative coccobacillus) Streptococcus pneumoniae (#1 bacterial cause)
Typical Age Group Infants <2 years (peak 2–6 m) Unvaccinated or incompletely immunized infants & children All pediatric age groups
Classic Clinical Clues Coryza/rhinorrhea followed by tachypnea, retractions, and diffuse wheezing Paroxysmal hacking cough with inspiratory “whoop”, post-tussive emesis, or infant apnea High fever, tachypnea, productive cough, focal bronchial breath sounds, or referred abdominal
Diagnostic Workup Clinical diagnosis (or nasopharyngeal swab PCR); CXR shows hyperinflation Marked absolute lymphocytosis; nasopharyngeal PCR/culture Lobar consolidation on CXR; sudden distress on CPAP = Pneumothorax (Chest tube!)
1st-Line Tx Strictly supportive care (humidified O2, nasal suctioning, IV/NG hydration) Oral Macrolide (Azithromycin or Erythromycin) for patient & household contacts Outpatient: oral Amoxicillin; Inpatient severe: IV Cefotaxime / Ceftriaxone

3. Pediatric Bronchial Asthma 🔥 x9

  • Diagnostic Confirmation & Triggers: 🔥 x4
    • Clinical Clues: Recurrent episodes of nocturnal/early-morning or exercise-induced wheezing, chest tightness
    • chronic dry cough that improves with bronchodilator trial.
    • Objective Confirmation (≥5 Years):
      • Spirometry before and after inhaled
      • bronchodilator demonstrating reversible airflow obstruction.
    • Drug Trigger to Avoid: Strictly avoid NSAIDs and Aspirin in asthmatic children as they precipitate severe bronchospasm.
  • Acute Exacerbation vs. Long-Term Controller Therapy: 🔥 x5
    • Acute Asthma Exacerbation: Repetitive inhaled short-acting beta-2 agonists + early Systemic Corticosteroids.
    • Daily Maintenance Controller: Low-Dose Inhaled Corticosteroids are the cornerstone of chronic persistent asthma control.

4. Airway/Nasal Foreign Bodies & Pediatric Tuberculosis 🔥 x7

  • Tracheobronchial vs. Nasal Foreign Body: 🔥 x5
    • Bronchial Foreign Body:
      • Toddler with sudden choking episode while playing/eating followed by unilateral wheezing and asymmetric breath sounds
      • → remove via rigid bronchoscopy.
    • Laryngeal Foreign Body: Sudden choking followed by hoarse cry, aphonia
    • biphasic stridor → impacted in the larynx.
    • Nasal Foreign Body: Toddler presenting with unilateral foul-smelling purulent or blood-stained nasal discharge.
  • Pediatric Pulmonary Tuberculosis & Bronchiectasis: x2
    • Best Specimen in Young Children (<5 Years):
      • Because toddlers swallow their sputum rather than expectorating
      • obtain 3 consecutive early-morning gastric aspirates for AFB stain and culture!
    • Chronic Wet Cough with Clubbing & Crackles:
      • Indicates Bronchiectasis (evaluate for Cystic Fibrosis or primary ciliary dyskinesia).

5. Pediatric ENT Infections: AOM vs. Serous Otitis Media & Pharyngitis 🔥 x8

Feature Acute Otitis Media (AOM) x3 Otitis Media with Effusion (Serous OM) x3
Clinical Presentation Acute fever, ear pain/tugging, irritability, and poor sleep Afebrile child with middle ear fluid, conductive hearing loss, and speech delay
Otoscopy Findings Bulging, erythematous, opacified, immobile tympanic membrane Dull, retracted tympanic membrane with air-fluid levels or air bubbles
1st-Line Tx High-dose oral Amoxicillin (80–90 mg/kg/day) Observe for 3 m; if persistent with hearing loss → myringotomy with tympanostomy tubes
  • Streptococcal Pharyngitis & Rhinitis Medicamentosa: x2
    • Group A Streptococcal Pharyngitis:
      • High fever, tonsillar exudates, tender anterior cervical lymphadenopathy, and absence of cough
      • → treat with IM Benzathine Penicillin G or oral Amoxicillin to prevent Acute Rheumatic Fever.
    • Rhinitis Medicamentosa:
      • Rebound nasal congestion caused by prolonged use (>
      • 3–5 days) of topical alpha-adrenergic decongestant sprays (phenylephrine / oxymetazoline).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص التهاب لسان المزمار الحاد Epiglottitis (سؤال Q1274):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: طفل عمره 4 سنوات يعاني من صرير حاد مفاجئ وسيلان اللعاب والجلوس في وضعية الانتصاب الثلاثية (Tripod posture). التشخيص هو Acute Epiglottitis، والخطوة الإلزامية المنقذة للحياة فوراً هي تأمين مجرى الهواء والأنبوبة الحنجرية بغرفة العمليات Immediate endotracheal intubation in a controlled setting (Option D) (كما تم تأكيده في الأسئلة المتطابقة Q1211 و Q1279 و Q1280).

في الـ Model Answer في السؤال Q1274 فقط: تم اختيار Racemic adrenaline via nebulization (Option E) بالخطأ، علماً بأن الأدرينالين الاستنشاقي يُستخدم في الـ Croup (الخناق) ولا يفيد إطلاقاً في لسان المزمار بل قد يؤدي إلى تهييج الطفل والانسداد التام الفوري للمجرى الهوائي.

Topic 8 of 12 • Pediatrics • Pediatrics

8. Pediatric Cardiology & Kawasaki Disease

🔥 High Yield (28×) 18 Qs

1. Acyanotic vs. Cyanotic Congenital Heart Defects 🔥 x19

Defect Key Association Classic Auscultatory & Clinical Clues 1st-Line Tx
Ventricular Septal Defect (VSD) x3 #1 most common congenital heart defect overall Harsh holosystolic murmur at the left lower sternal border; feeding dyspnea if large Small muscular VSDs close spontaneously; large VSDs require diuretics + surgical patch
Atrioventricular Septal Defect (AVSD) x3 Strongly associated with Down Syndrome (Trisomy 21) Endocardial cushion defect → early HF, holosystolic + diastolic rumble Complete surgical repair in early infancy before Eisenmenger syndrome
Patent Ductus Arteriosus (PDA) 🔥 x4 Prematurity, maternal congenital Rubella syndrome Continuous “machine-like” murmur at left infraclavicular area + bounding peripheral pulses Close pharmacologically with Indomethacin or Ibuprofen or catheter occlusion
Coarctation of the Aorta 🔥 x4 Strongly associated with Turner Syndrome (45,X) Upper-limb hypertension with lower-limb hypotension and weak/delayed femoral pulses Balloon angioplasty, stenting, or surgical resection of stenotic segment
Tetralogy of Fallot (TOF) 🔥 x5 #1 cyanotic CHD past infancy; 22q11.2 deletion PROVe; “boot-shaped” heart and cyanotic spells relieved by squatting Squatting increases SVR to reduce right-to-left shunt; definitive surgical repair

2. Kawasaki Disease (Mucocutaneous Lymph Node Syndrome) 🔥 x6

  • Diagnostic Criteria (Fever ≥5 Days + ≥4 of 5 CRASH Clues): 🔥 x6
    • C — Conjunctivitis: Bilateral non-purulent (non-exudative) bulbar conjunctival injection.
    • R — Rash: Polymorphous maculopapular or morbilliform truncal rash.
    • A — Adenopathy: Unilateral anterior cervical lymphadenopathy (≥1.5 cm).
    • S — Strawberry Tongue & Oral Mucosa: Erythematous, fissured/cracked lips and strawberry tongue.
    • H — Hands & Feet Changes: Acute erythema and indurated edema of palms/soles followed by periungual desquamation.
  • Most Feared Complication & First-Line Therapy:
    • Major Cardiac Complication: Coronary Artery Aneurysms, coronary thrombosis
    • myocardial infarction (monitor with baseline and serial Echocardiography).
    • First-Line Treatment: Single high-dose IV Immunoglobulin (IVIG 2 g/kg) + High-Dose Aspirin in 1st 10 days of illness.

3. Acute Rheumatic Fever (ARF), Viral Myocarditis & Cardiac Arrest 🔥 x7

  • Acute Rheumatic Fever (ARF): 🔥 x4
    • Trigger & Latent Period:
      • Autoimmune molecular mimicry occurring 2–4 w after untreated
      • Group A Streptococcal pharyngitis in children aged 5–15 years.
    • Revised Jones Major Criteria (J♥NES):
      • J
        • oints, ♥ Pancarditis, SC Nodules, Erythema marginatum
        • Sydenham chorea.
    • First-Line Treatment: Eradicate GAS with Penicillin + control migratory polyarthritis with High-Dose Aspirin.
  • Acute Viral Myocarditis & Pediatric Cardiac Arrest: x3
    • Acute Viral Myocarditis:
      • Infant/child presenting 1–2 w after a viral URI (Coxsackie B) with agitation, disproportionate tachycardia
      • apical S3 gallop, hepatomegaly
      • cardiomegaly on CXR.
    • #1 Cause of Primary Cardiac Arrest in Children: x2
      • Progressive Respiratory Failure and Hypoxia, unlike adults where primary ventricular arrhythmia predominates.

9. Pediatric GI & Surgical Conditions

🔥 High Yield (58×) 63 Qs

1. Infantile Projectile & Bilious Vomiting: IHPS vs. Duodenal Atresia vs. Malrotation 🔥 x31

Feature Hypertrophic Pyloric Stenosis (IHPS) 🔥 x14 Duodenal Atresia 🔥 x10 Malrotation with Midgut Volvulus 🔥 x7
Typical Age & Association 2–6 w old (firstborn male; erythromycin exposure) First 24–48 h of life; Down Syndrome & polyhydramnios Neonate / first month of life (SMA pedicle twist)
Vomiting Character Forceful, projectile NON-BILIOUS vomiting; remains a “hungry vomiter” Bilious vomiting within h of birth Sudden-onset bilious vomiting in a previously healthy infant
Abdominal Exam Clues Palpable epigastric “olive-shaped” mass + visible peristaltic waves Scaphoid lower abdomen (or mild epigastric fullness) Abdominal distension, tenderness, hematochezia, and shock
Diagnostic / Lab Hallmark Hypochloremic, hypokalemic metabolic alkalosis; confirm by Abdominal US Pathognomonic “Double-bubble” sign on plain abdominal X-ray Stable neonate: Upper GI Contrast Series showing “corkscrew” sign
1st-Line Tx 1st: IV 0.9% NaCl + KCl resuscitation BEFORE Ramstedt pyloromyotomy NG decompression, IV fluids, and surgical duodenoduodenostomy Unstable/volvulus: Immediate urgent laparotomy (Ladd procedure)!

2. Failure to Pass Meconium: Hirschsprung Disease vs. Meconium Ileus 🔥 x7

Feature Hirschsprung Disease (Congenital Aganglionic Megacolon) 🔥 x4 Meconium Ileus x3
Pathophysiology Failed neural crest migration → absence of Auerbach & Meissner ganglion cells in rectosigmoid Thick, inspissated meconium plugs obstructing the terminal ileum
Key Genetic Association Down Syndrome (Trisomy 21) and RET proto-oncogene mutation Virtually pathognomonic of Cystic Fibrosis (CFTR mutation)
Clinical & Exam Clues No meconium within 48h, bilious emesis, rectal squirt sign & enterocolitis No meconium on Day 1–2, bilious emesis; X-ray: “soap-bubble” sign in RLQ
Diagnostic Gold Standard Contrast enema (transition zone); confirmed by full-thickness rectal suction biopsy Abdominal X-ray + sweat chloride / CFTR genetic testing
1st-Line Tx Surgical resection of aganglionic segment (pull-through procedure) Hyperosmolar Gastrografin enema; if perforated/volvulus → diverting ileostomy

3. Lower GI Emergencies: Intussusception vs. Meckel Diverticulum 🔥 x20

Feature Ileocolic Intussusception 🔥 x16 Meckel Diverticulum 🔥 x4
Typical Age Group 5 – 24 m old Toddlers <2 yo (“Rule of 2s”)
Pathophysiology Ileum telescopes into cecum (hypertrophied Peyer patches post-viral URI) Persistent remnant of vitelline duct containing ectopic acid-secreting gastric mucosa
Classic Clinical Picture Episodic colicky crying with drawing knees to chest, “sausage” RUQ mass, and “currant-jelly” Sudden, painless, massive bright-red rectal bleeding causing acute anemia/pallor
Diagnostic Gold Standard Abdominal US showing “target / doughnut / bull’s-eye sign” Technetium-99m Pertechnetate Scan (“Meckel scan”)
First-Line Treatment Pneumatic (air) or hydrostatic (contrast) enema (both diagnostic & therapeutic!) Surgical diverticulectomy / small bowel resection

4. Extrahepatic Biliary Atresia & Pediatric Liver Disorders 🔥 x12

  • Extrahepatic Biliary Atresia: 🔥 x8
    • Classic Presentation:
      • Full-term 2–8-week-old infant with persistent jaundice lasting >
      • 2 w, dark urine, pale acholic (clay-colored) stools
      • firm hepatomegaly.
    • Lab & Imaging Workup: Markedly elevated direct (conjugated) bilirubin
    • Abdominal US (absent/contracted gallbladder, triangular cord sign), HIDA scan (no duodenal excretion)
    • percutaneous liver biopsy.
    • Definitive Treatment: Urgent surgical Kasai hepatoportoenterostomy before 60 days of life.
  • Portal Vein Thrombosis, Wilson Disease & Autoimmune Hepatitis: 🔥 x4
    • Extrahepatic Portal Vein Thrombosis:
      • #1 cause of massive upper GI variceal hematemesis
      • splenomegaly with completely normal liver function tests in a young child.
    • Wilson Disease:
      • Adolescent with hepatic dysfunction, neuropsychiatric symptoms
      • Coombs-negative hemolytic anemia, low serum ceruloplasmin
      • corneal Kayser-Fleischer rings.
    • Autoimmune Hepatitis: Child/adolescent with jaundice, elevated transaminases, hypergammaglobulinemia
    • positive ANA / anti-smooth muscle antibodies (ASMA).

5. Pediatric GERD, Celiac Disease & Common Surgical Conditions 🔥 x16

  • Physiologic Infant Regurgitation (GER) vs. Complicated GERD: 🔥 x5
    • “Happy Spitter” (Physiologic GER): Effortless spitting up after feeds in a thriving infant with normal weight gain
    • → reassure parents, give smaller frequent feeds, and hold upright for 20–30 min.
    • Refractory Complicated GERD:
      • Recurrent aspiration pneumonia (4+ episodes) and failure to thrive unresponsive to medical therapy
      • → surgical Nissen Fundoplication.
  • Celiac Disease & Pediatric Enteric Infections: 🔥 x6
    • Celiac Disease: 🔥 x4
      • Toddler after wheat introduction presenting with chronic foul steatorrhea, abdominal distension, gluteal wasting, FTT
      • refractory iron-deficiency anemia
      • → screen with IgA anti-tTG, confirm by duodenal biopsy, and treat with lifelong gluten-free diet.
    • Enterobius vermicularis (Pinworm):
      • Nocturnal perianal itching
      • → diagnose via early-morning perianal Scotch-tape swab and treat with albendazole/mebendazole.
    • Pseudomembranous Colitis:
      • Profuse watery/bloody diarrhea after recent antibiotic therapy
      • → Clostridioides difficile toxin.
  • Congenital Umbilical Hernia, Hydrocele & Acute Appendicitis: 🔥 x5
    • Congenital Umbilical Hernia: x3 Soft reducible umbilical defect in an infant
    • → observe and follow up until age 2–4 years (closes spontaneously).
    • Congenital Non-Communicating Hydrocele: Painless, transilluminating scrotal swelling in a 2-month-old infant
    • → reassure and follow up until 12 m.
    • Acute Appendicitis: Periumbilical pain migrating to McBurney point (RLQ) with anorexia, fever
    • leukocytosis → surgical appendectomy.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص القيء المراري الوليدي والتواء الأمعاء (سؤال Q1291):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: رضيع عمره يومان يعاني من قيء مراري أخضر (Bilious vomiting). التشخيص الطارئ المشتبه به هو سوء الدوران المعوي والتواء المعي المتوسط Intestinal Malrotation with Midgut Volvulus. الفحص المعياري الذهبي لتأكيد التشخيص في وليد مستقر ديناميكياً هو أشعة الجهاز الهضمي العلوي بالصبغة Upper GI contrast series (Option D) لتحديد موقع موصل الاثني عشر بالصائم (Ligament of Treitz) وشكل الكورك-سرو (Corkscrew appearance) (كما تم تأكيده في Q1463).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم تسجيل Ultrasound of the abdomen (Option A)، علماً بأن السونار قد يظهر انقلاب أوعية المساريقا (Whirlpool sign)، إلا أن فحص الصبغة Upper GI هو المعيار الذهبي المعتمد جراحياً.

Topic 10 of 12 • Pediatrics • Pediatrics

10. Pediatric Hematology, Oncology & Allergy

🔥 High Yield (36×) 29 Qs

1. Pediatric Bleeding Disorders: ITP vs. Hemophilia A vs. von Willebrand Disease 🔥 x15

Feature Pediatric Immune Thrombocytopenia (ITP) 🔥 x10 Hemophilia A / B x3 von Willebrand Disease (vWD) x2
Pathophysiology & Inheritance Anti-GpIIb/IIIa IgG autoantibodies 1–3 w after a viral URI (2–6y) X-linked recessive deficiency of Factor VIII (A) or Factor IX (B) Autosomal dominant deficiency/dysfunction of vWF (#1 inherited bleeding disorder)
Primary Bleeding Pattern Mucocutaneous: sudden petechiae, purpura, ecchymoses, and epistaxis Deep tissue bleeding: hemarthrosis (swollen painful knee/ankle) and muscle hematomas Mucocutaneous bleeding: recurrent epistaxis, gingival bleeding, easy bruising
Key Physical Exam Rule Well-appearing child with NO hepatosplenomegaly, lymphadenopathy, or bone pain! Tender, warm, swollen joint with restricted range of motion Normal physical exam outside of mucosal oozing/ecchymoses
Coagulation Profile Isolated severe thrombocytopenia; normal Hb, WBC, PT, aPTT (1st test: CBC + platelets) Isolated prolonged aPTT with normal platelets, bleeding time, and PT Prolonged bleeding time ± mildly prolonged aPTT with normal platelets
1st-Line Tx Skin bruising/petechiae only: Observation; active mucosal bleeding: IVIG or Steroids Acute hemarthrosis: immediate Factor VIII concentrate replacement Desmopressin (DDAVP) or vWF-containing concentrate

2. Pediatric Oncology: Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) vs. Neuroblastoma 🔥 x10

Feature Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) 🔥 x8 Neuroblastoma x2
Epidemiology & Origin #1 childhood malignancy (peak age 2–5y; increased in Down syndrome) #1 extracranial solid tumor of infancy (median age <2y; neural crest adrenal medulla)
Core Clinical Triad Bone marrow failure: pallor (anemia), fever (neutropenia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia) Firm, nodular retroperitoneal mass that crosses the midline
Pathognomonic Exam Clues Severe bone/joint pain (limping), generalized lymphadenopathy, and hepatosplenomegaly Periorbital ecchymoses (“raccoon eyes”) + opsoclonus-myoclonus-ataxia syndrome
Confirmatory Diagnosis Bone Marrow Aspiration showing ≥20% lymphoblasts (mediastinal mass in T-cell ALL) Elevated urinary HVA and VMA; Homer-Wright rosettes on biopsy; MYCN amplification

3. Sickle Cell Crises, Hereditary Spherocytosis & Vascular Lesions 🔥 x9

  • Sickle Cell Disease (HbSS) Acute Crises: 🔥 x5
    • Aplastic Crisis: x3 Triggered by Parvovirus B19 infection (destroys erythroid precursors)
    • → sudden severe drop in hemoglobin with profound reticulocytopenia (<1%) and absence of new jaundice.
    • Acute Chest Syndrome: x2
      • Acute chest pain, fever, tachypnea, wheezing, hypoxia
      • a new pulmonary infiltrate on CXR (#1 cause of death in SCD)
      • → treat with high-flow Oxygen, empiric IV Abx, IV hydration, analgesia, and blood transfusion.
  • Hereditary Spherocytosis: x2
    • Pathophysiology & Clues: Autosomal dominant RBC membrane defect (ankyrin/spectrin)
    • → triad of hemolytic anemia, unconjugated jaundice, and splenomegaly with elevated MCHC and spherocytes
    • Confirm via Osmotic Fragility or EMA binding test.
  • Infantile Hemangioma vs. Kasabach-Merritt Phenomenon: x2
    • Uncomplicated Infantile Strawberry Hemangioma: Bright-red raised truncal lesion in infancy
    • → close observation and reassurance (involutes spontaneously).
    • Kasabach-Merritt Syndrome: Rapidly enlarging giant vascular tumor trapping platelets
    • → consumptive thrombocytopenia and microangiopathic hemolytic anemia.

4. Anaphylaxis, Urticaria, Hereditary Angioedema & Immunodeficiency 🔥 x12

  • Acute Anaphylaxis (Peanut / Food / Bee Sting): 🔥 x6
    • Presentation:
      • Rapid onset after allergen exposure with generalized urticaria, angioedema (lip/tongue swelling), wheezing/stridor
      • hypotension.
    • Immediate 1st-Line Lifesaving Treatment: IM (IM) Epinephrine (1:1000, 0.01 mg/kg into the anterolateral mid-thigh).
  • Acute Urticaria vs. Hereditary Angioedema: 🔥 x5
    • Acute Urticaria: Pruritic, erythematous raised wheals resolving within 24h → 2nd-generation H1 antihistamines.
    • Hereditary Angioedema (HAE): Autosomal dominant C1 esterase inhibitor deficiency (↑ bradykinin, low C4)
    • → recurrent non-pruritic swelling of lips, face, bowel wall, and larynx (risk of fatal suffocation!) without wheals.
  • Primary Immunodeficiency Warning Signs: x1
    • Clinical Clue: Recurrent severe bacterial sinopulmonary infections (3+ pneumonias, otitis media), oral thrush
    • chronic diarrhea/FTT starting after 6 m of age (waning maternal IgG).
↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 11 of 12 • Pediatrics • Pediatrics

11. Pediatric Nephrology, Rheumatology & Orthopedics

🔥 High Yield (30×) 19 Qs

1. Pediatric Glomerular Disease: Minimal Change Disease vs. PSGN 🔥 x12

Feature Minimal Change Disease (Nephrotic Syndrome) 🔥 x7 Post-Streptococcal Glomerulonephritis (PSGN) 🔥 x5
Epidemiology & Trigger #1 pediatric nephrotic syndrome (peak age 2–6y; follows viral URI) #1 pediatric nephritic syndrome (age 5–12y, 1–3w after GAS pharyngitis/impetigo)
Edema & Urine Character Morning periorbital puffiness, generalized pitting edema/ascites, and frothy urine Periorbital/facial edema, oliguria, and cola-colored / smoky-brown gross hematuria
Blood Pressure Normal blood pressure Moderate-to-severe hypertension (± hypertensive encephalopathy)
Urinalysis & Complement Massive proteinuria (3+/4+), hypoalbuminemia (<2.5 g/dL), normal C3 Dysmorphic RBCs, RBC casts, elevated ASO titers, and low serum C3 complement
1st-Line Tx Empiric Oral Corticosteroids (Prednisolone) without renal biopsy Loop diuretics; HTN encephalopathy → IV Sodium Nitroprusside

2. Febrile UTI, Vesicoureteral Reflux & Posterior Urethral Valves 🔥 x6

  • Febrile Urinary Tract Infection & VUR Imaging Protocol: 🔥 x4
    • Initial Imaging After 1st Febrile UTI (<2 Years):
      • Renal and Bladder US (RBUS) to detect hydronephrosis or anatomic defects.
    • Recurrent Febrile UTIs (≥2 Episodes) or Abnormal RBUS:
      • Perform a Voiding Cystourethrogram (VCUG) to diagnose and grade Vesicoureteral Reflux (VUR).
    • Gold Standard to Detect Permanent Renal Cortical Scarring:
      • Technetium-99m DMSA Renal Cortical Scan performed 1–2 m (or 4–6 m) after acute pyelonephritis.
  • Posterior Urethral Valves (PUV): x2
    • Classic Vignette:
      • Newborn male
        • with failure to void in the first 12–24 h, palpable distended suprapubic bladder, maternal oligohydramnios
        • bilateral hydronephrosis
      • → immediate urethral catheterization + endoscopic valve ablation.

3. Henoch-Schönlein Purpura (HSP) & Juvenile Dermatomyositis 🔥 x7

  • Henoch-Schönlein Purpura (IgA Vasculitis): 🔥 x6
    • Pathogenesis:
      • IgA immune complex small-vessel leukocytoclastic vasculitis occurring 1–2 w
      • after an upper respiratory infection in a child (3–15 years).
    • (1) Palpable purpura on the buttocks and lower extremities with a NORMAL platelet count
    • (2) Arthralgia/arthritis (knees/ankles)
    • (3) Colicky abdominal pain, GI bleeding, or ileo-ileal intussusception, and
    • (4) IgA Nephropathy (hematuria/proteinuria).
  • Juvenile Dermatomyositis (JDM): x1
    • Diagnostic Hallmarks:
      • Symmetric proximal muscle weakness
      • violaceous heliotrope eyelid rash
      • Gottron papules over knuckles
      • elevated CK.

4. Septic Arthritis, Osteomyelitis & Benign Growing Pains 🔥 x5

  • Acute Septic Arthritis & Hematogenous Osteomyelitis: 🔥 x4
    • Most Common Causative Organism: Staphylococcus aureus is the #1 pathogen across all pediatric age groups.
    • Septic Arthritis of the Hip/Knee:
      • Acute high fever, refusal to bear weight, flexed/externally rotated joint
      • severe pain on passive motion with elevated ESR/CRP
      • → urgent joint aspiration and surgical irrigation/drainage.
  • Benign Nocturnal Limb Pains (“Growing Pains” — ): x1
    • Distinguishing Features:
      • Healthy 3–8-yo child with bilateral cramping thigh/calf pain strictly at night
      • that resolves completely by morning with zero limp and normal physical exam
      • → reassure parents.
  • Suspected Child Abuse (3-Month Infant with Parietal Skull Fracture “Rolled Off Sofa”): x2
    • Infants cannot roll at 3 m → obtain a complete radiographic skeletal survey.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام بخصوص متابعة التهاب الحويضة والكلية عند الأطفال (سؤال Q1409):

وفقاً لمذكرة د. أحمد عامر والـ Model Answer: في طفلة 4 سنوات بعد علاج التهاب الحويضة والكلية الحاد (Acute Pyelonephritis)، تم اعتماد إجراء Voiding Cystourethrogram (VCUG) in 1 to 2 months (Option E) لتقييم الارتجاع المثاني الحالبي (Vesicoureteral Reflux - VUR).

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: الفحص الأكثر دقة والمعتمد عالمياً لتقييم حدوث التندب النسيجي الكلوي (Renal cortical scarring) بعد مرور شهرين من الالتهاب الحاد هو المسح الذري للكلية DMSA renal scan (Option B).

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 12 of 12 • Pediatrics • Pediatrics

12. Pediatric Neurology & Viral Exanthems

🔥 High Yield (48×) 41 Qs

1. Pediatric Viral & Bacterial Exanthems Comparison Table 🔥 x23

Disease Causative Pathogen Pathognomonic Clinical & Rash Clues Complication & Management
Measles (Rubeola) 🔥 x8 Measles virus (Paramyxovirus) High fever + Cough, Coryza, Conjunctivitis + buccal Koplik spots → cephalocaudal rash Acute: otitis/pneumonia; Late: fatal SSPE; give high-dose Vitamin A
Rubella (German Measles) x2 Rubella virus (Togavirus) Low-grade fever, postauricular & suboccipital lymphadenopathy, 3-day pink rash Congenital rubella triad (PDA, cataracts, deafness); supportive care
Roseola Infantum x2 Human Herpesvirus 6 (HHV-6) 3–4 days of high fever (40°C); trunk rash appears ONLY AFTER fever abruptly resolves! Febrile seizures during febrile phase; supportive care
Erythema Infectiosum (Fifth Disease) x2 Parvovirus B19 “Slapped cheek” facial rash followed by reticular lace-like rash on trunk/limbs Triggers aplastic crisis in sickle cell disease; hydrops fetalis
Varicella (Chickenpox) 🔥 x5 Varicella-Zoster Virus (VZV) Pruritic polymorphous vesicular rash in all stages of healing simultaneously #1 complication: bacterial skin infection; give Paracetamol (NO Aspirin → Reye!)
Scarlet Fever x2 Streptococcus pyogenes (GAS) Exudative pharyngitis, strawberry tongue, Pastia lines, and “sandpaper-like” rash Oral Penicillin V or Amoxicillin for 10 days
Staphylococcal Scalded Skin (SSSS) x2 Staphylococcus aureus exfoliative toxins Fever, diffuse tender erythema, flaccid blisters with positive Nikolsky sign & intact mucosa IV anti-staphylococcal Abx + burn-unit fluid care

2. Pediatric Seizures: Simple vs. Complex Febrile Seizures & Absence Epilepsy 🔥 x11

Feature Simple Febrile Seizure x3 Complex Febrile Seizure x2
Age Range 6 m – 5 years (neurologically normal child) 6 m – 5 years
Duration & 24-Hour Frequency Lasts <15 min and occurs only ONCE in 24 h Duration ≥15 min OR ≥2 seizures within 24 h
Seizure Semiology Strictly generalized tonic-clonic convulsion Focal onset (e.g., jerking of right side of body only) or postictal Todd paresis
Diagnostic Workup & Management No routine neuroimaging/EEG; reassurance and antipyretics Requires EEG, neuroimaging (MRI/CT), and neurological evaluation
  • Childhood Absence Epilepsy (Petit Mal Seizures): 🔥 x6
    • Clinical Picture:
      • School-aged child (4–10 years) with dozens of daily brief (5–10-second) staring spells, behavioral arrest
      • eyelid fluttering with abrupt onset and immediate return to baseline (zero postictal confusion).
    • Provocation & EEG Hallmark: Easily provoked by hyperventilation
    • EEG demonstrates classic generalized 3-Hz spike-and-wave discharges.
    • First-Line Medication: Ethosuximide (blocks thalamic T-type calcium channels).

3. Neuromuscular Disorders: SMA vs. Duchenne vs. Guillain-Barré Syndrome 🔥 x12

Feature Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA Type 1) 🔥 x4 Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy (DMD) x2 Guillain-Barré Syndrome (GBS) 🔥 x6
Genetic / Immune Mechanism AR SMN1 deletion (5q) → degeneration of anterior horn motor neurons X-linked recessive frameshift deletion in DMD → absent dystrophin Autoimmune demyelinating polyneuropathy 1–3w after URI or Campylobacter
Typical Age & Onset Infancy (<6 m; Werdnig-Hoffmann disease) Young boys aged 2 – 5 years Acute onset in children/adolescents 1–3 w post-infection
Motor & Postural Clues Severe “floppy infant”, “frog-leg” posture, and tongue fasciculations Waddling gait, calf pseudohypertrophy, and positive Gowers sign Symmetric ascending flaccid paralysis starting in ankles/legs
Deep Tendon Reflexes Absent (areflexia) Reduced in proximal lower limbs Absent (areflexia)
Diagnosis & Treatment SMN1 genetic test; Nusinersen / Zolgensma Markedly elevated CK + DMD gene test; corticosteroids CSF albuminocytologic dissociation; treat with IVIG or Plasmapheresis

4. Cerebral Palsy, Spina Bifida, CNS Tumors & Meningococcal Prophylaxis 🔥 x16

  • Cerebral Palsy (CP) & Preterm Brain Injury: x3
    • Spastic Diplegic Cerebral Palsy:
      • Classic in infants born prematurely (<
      • 32 w) due to ischemic Periventricular Leukomalacia (PVL)
    • Causes lower-limb spasticity and scissoring gait.
    • Associated Comorbidities:
      • Commonly accompanied by epilepsy/seizures (30–50%), intellectual disability, strabismus
      • dysphagia.
  • Myelomeningocele, Posterior Fossa Tumors & Spinal Cord Compression: 🔥 x10
    • Lumbosacral Myelomeningocele (Spina Bifida — ): x2
      • Strongly associated with Arnold-Chiari Type II malformation and obstructive hydrocephalus.
    • Infantile Hydrocephalus & Pediatric Brain Tumors: x3 Rapidly enlarging head circumference, bulging fontanelle
    • “setting-sun” eyes OR morning headache/vomiting, papilledema
    • ataxia → order Brain MRI.
    • Uncal Herniation / CN III Palsy:
      • Acute unilateral fixed dilated pupil with “down-and-out”
      • eye indicates Third Cranial Nerve (CN III) compression.
    • Spinal Cord Tumor / Compressive Myelopathy: 🔥 x4
      • Progressive gait difficulty, midline back pain, thoracic sensory level, urinary incontinence
      • lower-limb hyperreflexia / Babinski sign.
    • Neurofibromatosis Type 1 (NF1):
      • Autosomal dominant (NF1) with ≥6 café-au-lait macules, axillary freckling, Lisch nodules
      • optic gliomas.
  • Pediatric GCS & Meningococcal Chemoprophylaxis: x3
    • Pediatric Verbal GCS Score: Smiles/coos = 5
    • Cries but consolable = 4
    • Inappropriately crying / inconsolably irritable = 3
    • Grunts/moans = 2
    • None = 1.
    • Meningococcemia Household Contact Prophylaxis:
      • Give all close household contacts Oral Rifampin every 12 h for 2 days (4 doses).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص المضاعفة الأكثر شيوعاً للجديري المائي (سؤال Q1144):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: المضاعفة الأكثر شيوعاً على الإطلاق (Most Common Complication) لمرض الجديري المائي (Chickenpox / Varicella) لدى الأطفال هي العدوى البكتيرية الثانوية للجلد والأنسجة Secondary bacterial skin infection (Option C) ببكتيريا Staphylococcus aureus أو Group A Streptococcus (تسبب Impetigo أو Cellulitis).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option A (Encephalitis) بالخطأ، علماً بأن التهاب الدماغ أو الرنح المخيخي الحاد مضاعفة عصبية خطيرة معروفة ولكنها نادرة جداً مقارنة بالعدوى البكتيرية الجلدية واسعة الانتشار.

Topic 1 of 9 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Obstetrics

1. Normal Pregnancy, Antenatal Care & Physiological Changes

🔥 High Yield (36×) 29 Qs

1. Gestational Age Dating, EDD & Fundal Height Landmarks 🔥 x11

  • Naegele’s Rule & US Dating: 🔥 x7
    • Naegele’s Formula: LMP + 7 days − 3 m (plus 1 year)
    • Example: LMP May 7 → EDD February 14
    • LMP June 30 → EDD April 6/7.
    • Excel Date Serial Alert:
      • For LMP June 30, Excel converted the date options into serial numbers
      • select April 6 / April 7 (39173)!
    • Best Method for Gestational Age: Early 1st-trimester US measuring CRL at 8–13 w (accurate within ±5–7 days)
    • Always obtain fetal US biometry (<20 w) if LMP is uncertain or conception occurred while on OCPs.
  • Uterine Fundal Height & Auscultatory Landmarks: 🔥 x4
    • 10–12 Weeks:
      • Uterine fundus just palpable at the pubic symphysis
      • fetal heart tones detected via handheld electronic Doppler.
    • 16 Weeks: Fundus palpable midway between pubic symphysis and umbilicus.
    • 20 Weeks: Fundus reaches the level of the umbilicus (from 20–36 w, fundal height in cm equals GA in w ± 2 cm).
    • Fetal Sex Determination: Determined strictly by the paternal spermatozoon (50% X, 50% Y)
    • → there is always a ~50% chance of having a boy .

2. Routine Antenatal Supplementation, Screening Timeline & Vaccines 🔥 x18

  • Periconceptional & Antenatal Vitamin Supplementation: 🔥 x5
    • Folic Acid Prophylaxis: 400 mcg/day started ≥1 month before conception through the end of 12 w to prevent NTDs.
    • Routine Early Pregnancy Supplements: Folic Acid + Vitamin D (10 mcg/day) + Iron
    • Avoid high-dose Vitamin A (retinol) (teratogenic).
  • Antenatal Screening & Diagnostic Timeline: 🔥 x7
    • 11–13 Weeks (1st-Trimester Combined Screen): US Nuchal Translucency (NT) + Maternal Serum PAPP-A + Free β-hCG.
    • 15–20 Weeks (2nd-Trimester Quadruple Screen): MSAFP + β-hCG + Unconjugated Estriol (uE3) + Inhibin A.
    • Confirmatory Invasive Testing: Perform Chorionic Villus Sampling (CVS) at 11–13 w or Amniocentesis at ≥15–16 w
    • NIPT = screening only (not diagnostic).
    • 24–28 Weeks: Screening Oral Glucose Tolerance / Challenge Test for gestational diabetes.
    • 35–37 Weeks: Lower vaginal and rectal swab for Group B Streptococcus (GBS) culture.
    • Non-Directive Genetic Counseling:
      • Always provide complete
      • unbiased information on all management options and respect the couple’s autonomy.
  • Rh Isoimmunization & Maternal Vaccinations: 🔥 x6
    • Rh-Negative Mother with Rh-Positive Partner:
      • Order an Indirect Antiglobulin (Coombs) Test at booking to check sensitization
    • If anti-D negative
    • give routine prophylactic Anti-D Immunoglobulin at 28 w and within 72 h postpartum if the infant is Rh-positive.
    • Contraindicated Vaccines in Pregnancy (Live Attenuated): MMR (Measles, Mumps, Rubella) and Varicella vaccines.
    • Safe Vaccines in Pregnancy (Inactivated / Toxoid):
      • Tdap, Tetanus toxoid, inactivated Influenza, and Hepatitis B vaccines.

3. Physiological Changes, Minor Complaints & Post-Term Pregnancy 🔥 x11

  • Physiological Adaptations & Benign Findings in Pregnancy: 🔥 x7
    • Morning Sickness (Mild Nausea/Vomiting without Ketonuria):
      • 1st step = reassurance and dietary modification (small frequent dry meals, ginger)
    • 1st-line drug = Vitamin B6 (Pyridoxine) ± Doxylamine.
    • Gastroesophageal Reflux (Heartburn):
      • Caused by progesterone-mediated relaxation of lower esophageal sphincter tone
      • mechanical upward pressure by the gravid uterus.
    • Physiological Dependent Leg Edema & Trace Glucosuria:
      • Evening ankle swelling with normal BP/negative proteinuria and trace glucosuria are normal → reassure the patient.
    • Incidental Simple Ovarian Cyst <5 cm in 1st Trimester: Represents a physiological corpus luteum of pregnancy
    • → repeat US in the 2nd trimester (resolves spontaneously by 14–16 w).
    • Polymorphic Eruption of Pregnancy (PUPPP):
      • 3rd-trimester intensely pruritic papules/plaques starting in abdominal striae with normal bile acids
      • → treat with topical corticosteroids + oral antihistamines.
  • Post-Term Pregnancy Management (≥41–42 Weeks): 🔥 x4
    • Favorable Cervix (Bishop Score ≥6, Soft/Anterior/Dilated):
      • Proceed directly to Induction of Labor (oxytocin + amniotomy).
    • Unfavorable Cervix (Closed, Long, Firm, Posterior):
      • Perform cervical ripening with vaginal Prostaglandin E2 (Dinoprostone) or Misoprostol before oxytocin induction.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص مكملات الثلث الأول من الحمل (سؤال Q1512):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: في الزيارة الأولى لمتابعة الحمل في الثلث الأول (الأسبوع السادس)، المكمل الدوائي الإلزامي والأساسي لحماية الجنين من عيوب الأنبوب العصبي (Neural tube defects) وعلاج فقر الدم هو Iron and Folic acid (Option B) أو الفيتامينات الشاملة للحمل المحتوية على الفوليك Pregnancy multivitamin formula (Option C).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم في نموذج التصحيح القديم اختيار Vitamin C (Option E) بالخطأ، وهو خطأ واضح في مفتاح الإجابة، حيث أن فيتامين C ليس المكمل الإلزامي الأولي مقارنة بحمض الفوليك والحديد.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 2 of 9 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Obstetrics

2. Normal & Abnormal Labor, Malpresentation & Obstetric Emergencies

🔥 High Yield (42×) 34 Qs

1. Stages of Labor & Partogram Labor Abnormalities 🔥 x14

Labor Pattern Cervical Dilation / Station Criteria Contraction Pattern 1st-Line Tx
Normal Active Phase x3 Cervix ≥4–6 cm dilating at ≥1 cm/hr (e.g., 5 → 9 cm in 3–4h) Strong regular contractions (≥200 Montevideo units) Expectant management (wait for spontaneous vaginal delivery)
Latent Phase / Prolonged Latent x3 Cervix <4 cm (e.g., 2 cm, 50% effaced) with regular contractions Mild-to-moderate contractions Observation, hydration/analgesia, or continue cervical ripening
Protracted Active Phase x3 Cervix ≥4–6 cm dilating slower than <1 cm/hr (e.g., 4 → 6 cm in 4h) Usually hypocontractile (<200 MVU) Amniotomy + IV Oxytocin augmentation
Secondary Arrest of Dilation 🔥 x4 Active phase (≥6 cm) with NO cervical change for ≥2–4 h Assess MVU: Hypotonic (<200 MVU) vs. Adequate (≥200 MVU) If hypotonic: Amniotomy + Oxytocin; if adequate contractions / CPD: CS
Arrest of Descent (2nd Stage) x1 10 cm dilated, pushing ≥3h (nullipara) or ≥2h (multipara) stuck at 0/−1 station Adequate pushing + caput & severe molding (CPD) Operative delivery or Emergency CS

2. Shoulder Dystocia & Fetal Malpresentations 🔥 x10

  • Shoulder Dystocia (Anterior Shoulder Impacted Behind Pubic Symphysis): x3
    • Clinical Sign: Retraction of the delivered fetal head tightly against the maternal perineum (“Turtle sign”).
    • Immediate 1st-Line Maneuver: McRoberts Maneuver combined with Suprapubic Pressure.
    • Strictly Contraindicated: Fundal pressure (worsens impaction and causes uterine rupture or C5–C6 Erb palsy).
  • Breech, Transverse Lie & Mentoposterior Face Presentation: 🔥 x7
    • Transverse Lie at 36–37 Weeks (Intact Membranes, Not in Labor): Attempt External Cephalic Version (ECV)
    • If in active labor or ruptured membranes → Emergency Cesarean Section.
    • Footling Breech in Active Labor: High risk of umbilical cord prolapse and head entrapment
    • → perform Emergency Cesarean Section.
    • No Palpable Presenting Part at 6 cm Dilation with Intact Membranes:
      • Perform bedside pelvic US first (avoid amniotomy!).

3. Intrapartum Emergencies: Uterine Rupture vs. Umbilical Cord Prolapse 🔥 x12

Feature Uterine Rupture x3 Umbilical Cord Prolapse 🔥 x5
Major Risk Factors Prior Cesarean or myomectomy scar, grand multiparity, oxytocin hyperstimulation Polyhydramnios, amniotomy with unengaged head, footling breech, transverse lie
Classic Clinical Picture Sudden tearing abdominal pain, cessation of contractions, vaginal bleeding, and shock Abrupt severe fetal bradycardia / variable decelerations immediately after membrane rupture
Vaginal & Fetal Exam Loss of fetal station (head recedes from +2 to −3) & easily palpable fetal parts Palpable pulsating umbilical cord in vagina or at introitus
Immediate First Step Resuscitate + immediate urgent laparotomy & Cesarean delivery Elevate presenting part off cord + Trendelenburg / knee-chest position until emergency CS
  • Intrapartum Cardiotocography (CTG) Decelerations: 🔥 x4
    • Early Decelerations: Benign vagal response to fetal head compression
    • Mirrors contraction → continue routine labor monitoring.
    • Late Decelerations / Prolonged Bradycardia: Signifies uteroplacental insufficiency
    • → initiate intrauterine resuscitation (left lateral position, IV fluids, O2, stop oxytocin)
    • Proceed to Emergency Cesarean Delivery if severe bradycardia persists.

4. Intrapartum GBS Prophylaxis, Perineal Tears & CS Infection Prevention 🔥 x6

  • Intrapartum Group B Streptococcus (GBS) Prophylaxis: x2
    • Indication & 1st-Line Regimen:
      • Positive GBS swab at 35–37 w (or unknown GBS status with ROM ≥18h / intrapartum fever / preterm)
      • → administer IV Penicillin G (or IV Ampicillin) during labor.
  • Classification of Perineal Lacerations: x2
    • 1st Degree: Vaginal epithelium and perineal skin only.
    • 2nd Degree: Extends into perineal body / muscles but anal sphincter is intact.
    • 3rd Degree: Extends into the external and/or internal anal sphincter complex (rectal mucosa intact).
    • 4th Degree: Extends completely through the anal sphincter AND anorectal mucosa.
  • Epidural Labor Fluids & CS Wound Care: x2
    • Labor with Epidural Analgesia: Encourage oral clear fluids and provide maintenance isotonic IV fluids.
    • Preventing Post-CS Surgical Site Infection:
      • Give prophylactic IV Abx before skin incision and use strict aseptic technique when changing wound dressings.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص تباطؤ نبض الجنين أثناء المخاض النشط (سؤال Q1635):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer والـ Model Answer: سيدة في مرحلة المخاض النشط (اتساع 7 سم) حدث لديها تباطؤ مستمر في نبض الجنين (Prolonged fetal bradycardia 90 bpm لمدة 7 دقائق)، بعد استبعاد تدلي الحبل السري والإنعاش الرحمي بتغيير وضعية الأم إلى الوضع الجانبي الأيسر (Left lateral position)، الإجراء الفوري الحاسم هو الولادة القيصرية الطارئة Emergency Cesarean Section (Option A).

2. تنبيه بخصوص تقييم مراحل المخاض (سؤال Q1529 وسؤال Q1538):
المخاض النشط (Active phase) يبدأ طبياً عند اتساع عنق الرحم ≥4–6 سم مع انمحاء متزايد وتقلصات منتظمة، والتقدم بمعدل 1–1.5 سم/ساعة يمثل تقدماً طبيعياً، بينما الـ Model Answer يميل أحياناً لاعتبار التقدم البطيء قبل 6 سم ضمن الـ Latent phase.

Topic 3 of 9 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Obstetrics

3. Antepartum & Postpartum Hemorrhage (APH & PPH)

🔥 High Yield (45×) 37 Qs

1. Antepartum Hemorrhage: Placenta Previa vs. Placental Abruption 🔥 x23

Feature Placenta Previa 🔥 x13 Placental Abruption (Abruptio Placentae) 🔥 x10
Major Risk Factors Prior CS(s), multiparity, advanced maternal age Maternal hypertension / preeclampsia, trauma, cocaine use, smoking, prior abruption
Bleeding Character Painless, unprovoked, recurrent bright-red vaginal bleeding Painful (sudden severe continuous abdominal/back pain), dark-red or concealed (20%)
Abdominal & Uterine Exam Soft, relaxed, non-tender uterus; high/floating presenting part Firm, rigid, board-like tender (“woody” hypertonic) uterus
Fetal Status & Shock Hemodynamic shock matches visible blood loss; FHR usually normal initially Shock/anemia out of proportion to visible blood; rapid fetal distress / IUFD
Major Complications Placenta Accreta Spectrum (PAS) and severe PPH DIC / consumptive coagulopathy, acute renal cortical necrosis, and Couvelaire uterus

2. Placenta Previa Management Rules & Placenta Accreta Spectrum (PAS) 🔥 x10

  • Diagnostic & Delivery Protocol in Placenta Previa: 🔥 x6
    • Absolute Contraindication:
      • Strictly NO digital vaginal examination in any 2nd/3rd-trimester
      • bleeding until placenta previa is excluded by US!
    • Incidental Low-Lying Placenta at 20-Week Scan:
      • Repeat transabdominal/transvaginal US at 32 w (90% resolve via placental migration).
    • Preterm (<36 Weeks) with Mild/Stopped Bleeding & Stable Mother/Fetus:
      • Admit, stabilize, and expectant management (corticosteroids + cross-matched blood).
    • Term (≥37 Weeks) OR Severe Active Bleeding / Fetal Distress at Any Age: Perform Cesarean Delivery.
  • Placenta Accreta Spectrum (Accreta, Increta & Percreta): 🔥 x4
    • Classic High-Risk Dyad: Anterior Placenta Previa + Prior Cesarean Section Scar(s).
    • Depth of Invasion: Accreta = villi attach to myometrium (absent decidua basalis)
    • Increta = invades into myometrium
    • Percreta = penetrates through serosa into urinary bladder.
    • Clinical Presentation & Definitive Tx:
      • Fails to separate after delivery and causes massive hemorrhage during attempts at manual removal
      • → perform planned Cesarean Hysterectomy!

3. Postpartum Hemorrhage (PPH): The 4 Ts Table 🔥 x10

Cause (4 Ts) Risk Factors Uterine & Pelvic Exam Clues 1st-Line Tx
Tone: Uterine Atony (70–80%) 🔥 x5 Twins, macrosomia, polyhydramnios, prolonged labor, grand multiparity Soft, boggy, enlarged uncontracted uterus palpable above the umbilicus 1st step: Bimanual uterine massage + bladder emptying + IV/IM Oxytocin
Trauma: Genital Tract Laceration x2 Macrosomic infant (≥4 kg), precipitous labor, forceps/vacuum delivery Continuous bright-red trickle of blood despite a firm, well-contracted uterus Speculum examination under good lighting and surgical suturing of cervical/vaginal tear
Tissue: Retained Placenta x1 Succenturiate lobe, prior uterine surgery, premature cord traction Placenta undelivered >30 min or missing cotyledon with placental tissue at os Manual removal of placenta under anesthesia + broad-spectrum Abx
Thrombin: Coagulopathy x1 Placental abruption, amniotic fluid embolism, severe preeclampsia/HELLP, vWD Persistent diffuse oozing 6h postpartum despite firm uterus and intact genital tract Coagulation panel + fresh frozen plasma, cryoprecipitate, and platelet transfusion
  • Postpartum Urinary Retention / Bladder Distension: x1
    • Clinical Clues:
      • Sudden severe sharp suprapubic pain immediately postpartum with a
      • separate firm globular suprapubic mass displacing the uterus upward
      • → treat with immediate urinary catheterization.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص المشيمة المنزاحة عند تمام الحمل ونزيف متكرر (سؤال Q1548):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: حامل في الأسبوع 38+4 تعاني من نزيف مهبلي غير مؤلم متكرر والمشيمة واصلة لعنق الرحم (Placenta Previa). الإجراء الطبي الجراحي المعتمد قطيعاً لحماية الأم والجنين هو الولادة بالعملية القيصرية Cesarean Section is warranted (Option E). وفي الـ Model Answer تم تسجيل Option B (Immediate watchful induction of labor) استناداً إلى بروتوكولات قديمة كانت تسمح بتجربة الولادة في المشيمة الهامشية الخلفية فقط.

2. تنبيه بخصوص نزيف ما بعد الولادة مع رحم مرتخي وصدمة (سؤال Q1558):
علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: نزيف حاد بعد خروج المشيمة مع رحم رخو غير منقبض (Soft, boggy atonic uterus) وهبوط الضغط إلى 80/40. الأولوية العاجلة الأولى المنقذة للحياة هي إنعاش الدورة الدموية بالمحاليل الوريدية وتجهيز الدم وتدليك الرحم وإعطاء قابضات الرحم IV fluids and blood preparation alongside uterine massage/uterotonics (Option A). بينما الـ Model Answer اختار Exploration of genital tract (Option C) وهو إجراء يُجرى لاحقاً أو في حال كان الرحم منقبضاً بقوة (Firm) للبحث عن تمزقات عنق الرحم والمهبل.

Topic 4 of 9 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Obstetrics

4. Hypertensive Disorders in Pregnancy (Preeclampsia & Eclampsia)

🔥 High Yield (38×) 27 Qs

1. Classification & Delivery Timing of Hypertensive Disorders in Pregnancy 🔥 x16

Disorder Gestational Age & BP Criteria Proteinuria & End-Organ Signs Definitive Delivery Timing
Chronic Hypertension x2 BP ≥140/90 mmHg BEFORE 20 w gestation (or persisting >12w postpartum) Absent (unless superimposed preeclampsia develops) 37–38 w gestation
Gestational Hypertension 🔥 x5 New-onset BP ≥140/90 mmHg AFTER 20 w gestation Negative proteinuria and no severe features 37 completed w (outpatient monitoring if mild)
Mild Preeclampsia x3 New-onset BP ≥140/90 mmHg after 20 w Proteinuria ≥300 mg/24h (or dipstick ≥1+) without severe features 37 completed w (induce labor if ≥37 w)
Severe Preeclampsia 🔥 x4 BP ≥160/110 mmHg after 20 w Headache, scotomas, RUQ pain, pulmonary edema, platelets <100k Stabilize mother first (IV MgSO4 + antihypertensives), then deliver at ≥34 w
Eclampsia x2 Preeclampsia complicated by generalized tonic-clonic convulsions Grand mal seizures + severe hypertension & proteinuria Stabilize mother first (ABC + IV MgSO4 + BP control), then deliver within h

2. Pathophysiology, Prophylaxis & Safe vs. Contraindicated Antihypertensives 🔥 x10

  • Pathophysiology & Prophylaxis of Preeclampsia: 🔥 x5
    • Pathophysiologic Hallmarks: Abnormal spiral artery remodeling
    • → systemic vasospasm and endothelial injury
    • → decreased GFR (↑ serum uric acid &
    • Creatinine), hemoconcentration (↓ plasma volume), and retinal arteriolar spasm.
    • #1 Cause of Maternal Death in Preeclampsia/Eclampsia: Intracranial / Cerebral Hemorrhage.
    • Strongest Risk Factors & Aspirin Prophylaxis:
      • History of preeclampsia in a previous pregnancy or chronic hypertension at booking
      • → start Low-Dose Aspirin (81 mg / day) at 12–16 w.
    • Confirming Proteinuria: When dipstick is equivocal/1+, order a 24-hour urine collection for protein (≥300 mg/24h).
  • Antihypertensive Drugs & Magnesium Sulfate Protocol: 🔥 x5
    • Safe Antihypertensives in Pregnancy: Labetalol, Methyldopa, oral extended-release Nifedipine, and IV Hydralazine.
    • Strictly Contraindicated in Pregnancy: ACE inhibitors (Captopril/Lisinopril) &
    • ARBs (cause fetal renal dysplasia and oligohydramnios) and routine diuretics.
    • First Step During Active Eclamptic Convulsion:
      • Protect the patient from self-harm and secure the airway (left lateral position, oxygen) BEFORE administering drugs.
    • Seizure Prophylaxis & Toxicity Antidote:
      • IV Magnesium Sulfate (MgSO4) is 1st-line drug to prevent and treat eclamptic fits
    • Monitor patellar reflex, respiratory rate, and urine output (antidote for toxicity = IV 10% Calcium Gluconate).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص توقيت الولادة في متلازمة أضداد الفوسفوليبيد مع مقدمات الارتعاج البسيطة (سؤال Q1610):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: في مريضة Antiphospholipid Syndrome مستقرة على الهيبارين وتعاني من Preeclampsia بدون علامات شديدة (Without severe features)، يوصى بمواصلة الحمل حتى الأسبوع 36–37 Delivery at 36–37 weeks (Option E/A) مع إيقاف الهيبارين قبل الولادة بـ 12–24 ساعة. بينما اختار الـ Model Answer إنهاء الحمل عند الأسبوع 34 (Option C).

2. تنبيه بخصوص خافضات الضغط الإسعافية في مقدمات الارتعاج الشديدة (سؤال Q1820):
الخيارات المعتمدة للسيطرة السريعة على ضغط الدم الشديد (≥160/110 mmHg) تشمل Oral Nifedipine (Option D, Model Answer) أو IV Hydralazine / IV Labetalol (Option E) كخط أول.

Topic 5 of 9 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Obstetrics

5. Gestational Diabetes & Medical/Surgical Disorders in Pregnancy

🔥 High Yield (48×) 43 Qs

1. Gestational & Pre-Existing Diabetes Mellitus in Pregnancy 🔥 x19

  • Risk Factors & Maternal-Fetal Complications (Pedersen Hypothesis): 🔥 x8
    • Strongest Historical Risk Factor for GDM: Delivery of a previous macrosomic baby (≥4.0–4.5 kg).
    • Classic Presentation of Undiagnosed GDM:
      • Recurrent/resistant vulvovaginal candidiasis
      • glucosuria
      • polyhydramnios or unexplained late intrauterine fetal death (IUFD) at term
      • → order an Oral Glucose Tolerance Test (OGTT).
    • Fetal Hyperinsulinemia Consequences:
      • Maternal glucose crosses the placenta and stimulates fetal pancreatic insulin (anabolic growth factor)
      • → fetal macrosomia, shoulder dystocia, neonatal RDS (surfactant inhibition)
      • neonatal hypoglycemia after cord clamping.
    • Pre-Existing T1DM/T2DM Teratogenicity: Congenital heart defects (VSD, TGA) are the most common anomaly overall
    • caudal regression syndrome (sacral agenesis) is the most specific.
  • Screening, Stepwise Management & Postpartum Follow-Up: 🔥 x11
    • Screening Window: Universal screening at 24–28 w using a 50-g 1-hour GCT followed by confirmatory 100-g 3-hour OGTT.
    • 1st-Line Therapy for Newly Diagnosed GDM:
      • Medical nutrition therapy (diet control) + regular exercise + daily blood glucose monitoring
    • Add Insulin only if glycemic targets fail (fasting >95, 1h >140, 2h >120 mg/dL).
    • Postpartum Insulin Drop: Maternal insulin requirements plummet immediately after delivery of the placenta.
    • Mandatory Postpartum Screening after GDM: Perform a 75-g 2-hour OGTT at 6 w postpartum (4–12 w)
    • lifelong fasting glucose/HbA1c screening every 6–12 m.

2. Hyperemesis Gravidarum vs. Intrahepatic Cholestasis of Pregnancy 🔥 x8

Feature Hyperemesis Gravidarum 🔥 x4 Intrahepatic Cholestasis of Pregnancy (ICP) 🔥 x4
Trimester & Trigger 1st trimester (8–12 w); linked to peak serum β-hCG (twins, molar pregnancy) 3rd trimester (30–36 w); estrogen/progesterone impaired bile transport
Classic Clinical Picture Intractable vomiting causing >5% weight loss, dehydration, orthostasis, and tachycardia Intense generalized pruritus worst on palms and soles at night WITHOUT a primary skin rash
Diagnostic Lab Hallmark Marked ketonuria on urinalysis + hypochloremic, hypokalemic metabolic alkalosis Elevated fasting serum total bile acids (>10 μmol/L) + mild transaminitis
1st-Line Tx Admit: IV fluids, electrolyte replacement, IV thiamine, and antiemetics Ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) + early delivery at 36–37w

3. Urinary Tract Infections, Anemia & Thromboembolism in Pregnancy 🔥 x12

FeatureAsymptomatic Bacteriuria x2Acute Cystitis x1Acute Pyelonephritis x2
Clinical & Lab PresentationUrine culture ≥100,000 (105) CFU/mL without urinary symptomsDysuria, urgency, frequency, suprapubic pain; afebrileHigh spiking fever, rigors, nausea/vomiting, flank pain & CVA tenderness
Maternal / Fetal Risk20–30% progress to acute pyelonephritis & preterm birthAscending upper tract infectionSeptic shock, ARDS, preterm labor, and maternal fever-induced fetal tachycardia
1st-Line Tx in PregnancyOral Abx (Nitrofurantoin / Amoxicillin) + mandatory follow-up urine cultureOral Nitrofurantoin or Cephalexin (avoid fluoroquinolones & TMP-SMX)Hospital admission + IV fluids + IV Cephalosporin (Cefazolin / Ceftriaxone)
  • Anemia, VTE & Surgical Abdomen in Pregnancy: 🔥 x7
    • WHO Definition of Anemia in Pregnancy x2:
      • Hemoglobin <11.0 g/dL in the 1st and 3rd trimesters (<10.5 g/dL in the 2nd trimester due to plasma expansion).
    • Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT) in Pregnancy x2: 1st diagnostic test for unilateral swollen calf = Compression Duplex US
    • Treat with LMWH or Unfractionated Heparin (Warfarin is contraindicated (teratogenic)!).
    • Red Degeneration of Uterine Fibroid:
      • Pregnant woman in 2nd trimester with known fibroid developing acute focal uterine pain, tenderness
      • low-grade fever, and leukocytosis
      • → conservative management (rest + analgesia).
    • Acute Appendicitis in Pregnancy: Nausea, fever, leukocytosis
    • right-sided abdominal pain (appendix displaced upward by gravid uterus) → prompt surgical appendectomy.
    • Acute Severe Asthma in Pregnancy: Prioritize aggressive maternal oxygenation (SpO2 ≥95%), inhaled bronchodilators
    • systemic corticosteroids to prevent fetal hypoxia.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص تأكيد تشخيص سكري الحمل (سؤال Q1526):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: سيدة ذات خطورة عالية (تاريخ ولادة طفل ميت Stillbirth سابق) أظهر المسح المبدئي لسكر الدم بعد ساعة ارتفاعاً (145 mg/dL). الفحص التأكيدي التشخيصي المعتمد هو منحنى تحمل السكر الفموي Standard Oral Glucose Tolerance Test - OGTT (Option E) (فحص الـ 100g 3-hour أو 75g 2-hour OGTT).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم وضع Option C (Serial follow up with domiciliary blood sugar testing)، علماً بأن قياس السكر المنزلي للمتابعة لا يُغني إطلاقاً عن إجراء فحص الـ OGTT التشخيصي لتأكيد سكري الحمل رسمياً.

6. Preterm Labor, Cervical Insufficiency, PPROM & Chorioamnionitis

🔥 High Yield (25×) 12 Qs

1. Cervical Insufficiency vs. Preterm Labor 🔥 x6

Feature Cervical Insufficiency (Incompetence) x1 Preterm Labor (<37 Weeks) 🔥 x5
Typical Gestational Age 2nd trimester (16–24 w) 20 to <37 w gestation
Risk Factors Prior cervical trauma (conization, LEEP, forceful D&C) or recurrent 2nd-trimester loss Prior preterm delivery, multiple gestation (twins), short cervix (<25 mm), bacterial vaginosis
Clinical Presentation Painless cervical dilation and effacement with pelvic pressure and bulging membranes Regular painful uterine contractions causing progressive cervical dilation & effacement
Prevention & Management Cervical Cerclage (placed prophylactically at 12–14w or rescue cerclage) Prophylaxis: Vaginal Progesterone • Acute: IM Steroids + MgSO4 + tocolysis

2. PPROM vs. Intra-Amniotic Infection (Chorioamnionitis) 🔥 x6

Feature Uncomplicated PPROM (<37 Weeks) x3 Acute Chorioamnionitis x3
Clinical Presentation Sudden gush of clear fluid from vagina with pooling on speculum; afebrile, non-tender uterus Fever (≥38.0°C) + maternal tachycardia + fetal tachycardia + tender uterus + foul fluid
Bedside Confirmation Microscopic arborization (“Ferning” test) (#1 most specific) + blue Nitrazine pH test Clinical diagnosis + maternal leukocytosis and amniotic fluid culture
1st-Line Tx If <34 w: Admit for antenatal corticosteroids + latency Abx (Ampicillin/Erythromycin) IV Abx (Ampicillin + Gentamicin) AND induction of labor / delivery at ANY GA!

7. Fetal Surveillance, IUGR, Amniotic Fluid & Multiple Pregnancy

🔥 High Yield (28×) 15 Qs

1. Antepartum Fetal Surveillance: NST, BPP & Umbilical Artery Doppler 🔥 x9

FeatureNon-Stress Test (NST) x2Biophysical Profile (BPP) x3Umbilical Artery Doppler x3
Primary Clinical Indication1st-line screening test for decreased fetal movements or high-risk pregnancy (≥32w)Follow-up of a non-reactive NST or decreased fetal movement with high-risk comorbidityGold standard for IUGR (EFW <10th percentile, maternal HTN)
Normal / Reassuring ResultReactive: ≥2 FHR accelerations (≥15 bpm for ≥15 sec) within 20 minScore 8/10 or 10/10 across 5 parameters (NST + breathing, movement, tone, AFI)Low-resistance forward diastolic flow in umbilical artery
Abnormal Result & ActionNon-reactive → → Biophysical Profile (BPP)Score ≤4/10 predicts high risk of fetal demise within 1 week → urgent delivery!Absent or reversed end-diastolic flow indicates severe placental insufficiency → delivery
  • Elevated Maternal Serum Alpha-Fetoprotein (MSAFP at 16–20 Weeks): x1
    • Causes: Most common cause overall is underestimated GA (wrong dates)
    • Pathological causes include open NTDs, ventral wall defects, and multiple gestation.
    • Best Next Step: Perform a targeted obstetric US to verify GA and inspect the fetal spine/cranium.

2. Amniotic Fluid Disorders, Multiple Pregnancy & Amniotic Fluid Embolism 🔥 x6

Feature Oligohydramnios (AFI <5 cm) x2 Polyhydramnios (AFI >24 cm) x1
Diagnostic Criteria Amniotic Fluid Index <5 cm (or single deepest pocket <2 cm); uterus smaller than dates Amniotic Fluid Index >24 cm (or single deepest pocket >8 cm); uterus larger than dates
Classic Etiologies ROM, placental insufficiency, fetal renal agenesis or PUV; avoid Indomethacin Maternal uncontrolled diabetes mellitus (#1 identifiable cause) or fetal impaired swallowing
Management at Late Preterm/Term Daily fetal surveillance, maternal hydration, and delivery at 36–37 w Screen for GDM (OGTT), detailed fetal anomaly scan, and watch for cord prolapse / PPH
  • Amniotic Fluid Embolism (AFE — Anaphylactoid Syndrome of Pregnancy): x3
    • (1) Abrupt onset of
    • (2) Hypoxemia / respiratory failure (dyspnea, cyanosis)
    • (3) Cardiogenic/obstructive shock & cardiovascular collapse, and
    • (4) Fulminant DIC (profuse bleeding from uterus and IV sites).
    • Management: Immediate high-flow oxygenation/intubation, cardiopulmonary resuscitation, vasopressors
    • massive transfusion protocol.

8. Early Pregnancy Bleeding: Miscarriage, Ectopic & Molar Pregnancy

🔥 High Yield (44×) 37 Qs

1. Clinical Types of First-Trimester Miscarriage (Abortion) 🔥 x11

Type of Miscarriage Internal Cervical Os Clinical & Transvaginal US Findings 1st-Line Tx
Threatened Miscarriage 🔥 x4 Closed Painless/mild vaginal spotting + intrauterine fetus with present cardiac activity Reassurance, pelvic rest, and conservative follow-up
Inevitable Miscarriage x2 Open (Dilated) Heavy vaginal bleeding, severe lower abdominal cramps, gestational sac/products at dilated os Surgical uterine evacuation (suction curettage) or misoprostol expulsion
Missed / Anembryonic Miscarriage x3 Closed Brownish discharge, US shows fetal pole without cardiac activity or empty sac Expectant, medical (misoprostol), or surgical suction evacuation
Incomplete Septic Abortion x2 Open (Dilated) Unsterile instrumentation → high fever (≥38.5°C), foul purulent bleed, tender uterus IV fluids + broad-spectrum IV Abx + prompt surgical uterine evacuation

2. Ectopic Pregnancy vs. Complete Hydatidiform Mole 🔥 x25

Feature Tubal Ectopic Pregnancy 🔥 x20 Complete Hydatidiform Mole (GTD) 🔥 x5
Major Risk Factors Prior PID, prior ectopic, tubal surgery, or pregnancy with IUD in situ Extremes of maternal age (<20 or >35 years); prior molar pregnancy
Classic Clinical Picture 6–8 w amenorrhea, unilateral pain, vaginal spotting Dark bleed, size > • dates, severe hyperemesis, preeclampsia &lt
β-hCG & US Hallmarks Suboptimal 48h β-hCG rise (< • 53%) or empty uterus with β-hCG &gt Extremely high β-hCG + classic “snowstorm” vesicular appearance on US with absent fetal parts
1st-Line Tx 1st: β-hCG + TVUS • Ruptured: Salpingectomy • Stable: MTX Suction curettage evacuation + mandatory weekly serial serum β-hCG monitoring
  • Intrauterine Pregnancy with IUD in Situ: x2
    • Management Rule:
      • If an intrauterine pregnancy is confirmed and the IUD strings are visible at the cervix
      • remove the IUD immediately by gentle traction to reduce the risk of septic miscarriage and preterm birth.

9. Puerperium, Puerperal Sepsis, Lactation & Postpartum Disorders

🔥 High Yield (28×) 17 Qs

1. Postpartum Fever & Puerperal Sepsis Differential 🔥 x9

Condition Typical Postpartum Day & Risk Classic Clinical & Exam Clues 1st-Line Tx
Postpartum Endometritis 🔥 x4 Day 2–10 postpartum; #1 risk factor is Cesarean Section or prolonged ROM Fever (≥38.5°C), tender uterus / subinvolution, and foul-smelling lochia Broad-spectrum IV Clindamycin + Gentamicin until afebrile for 24–48 h
Septic Pelvic Thrombophlebitis x1 Day 3–7 post-CS or endometritis Spiking picket-fence fever despite 48–72h IV Abx Continue IV Abx + add therapeutic IV Heparin
Postpartum DVT / Pulmonary Embolism x2 First 6 w postpartum (hypercoagulable state + CS) DVT: painful swollen leg Therapeutic Heparin (LMWH / UFH) followed by warfarin (safe during breastfeeding!)
Perineal Necrotizing Fasciitis vs. Hematoma x2 Day 2–5 after episiotomy or vaginal delivery Fasciitis: Woody edema, dishwater fluid, crepitus • Hematoma: Tense purple mass Fasciitis: Urgent radical surgical debridement + IV Abx; Large hematoma: incision & evacuation

2. Lactational Breast Disorders: Engorgement vs. Mastitis vs. Abscess vs. Galactocele 🔥 x4

Condition Timing & Etiology Breast Exam Findings 1st-Line Tx
Breast Engorgement x1 Day 2–4 postpartum (onset of lactogenesis) Bilateral diffuse, warm, firm, diffusely tender breasts; low-grade transient fever Frequent breastfeeding/pumping, warm compresses, and analgesics
Lactational Mastitis x1 Week 2–6 postpartum (Staphylococcus aureus via cracked nipple) Unilateral focal wedge-shaped erythema, tenderness, myalgias, and high fever; no fluctuance Continue breastfeeding, lactation consultation for latch, + oral dicloxacillin/cephalexin
Lactational Breast Abscess x1 Untreated mastitis progressing to pus collection Unilateral painful, erythematous, FLUCTUANT breast mass with persistent spiking fever Needle aspiration or surgical incision & drainage + anti-staphylococcal Abx
Galactocele x1 Obstructed lactiferous duct in a lactating woman Subareolar mobile, cystic milk-filled mass WITHOUT fever or erythema Needle aspiration (diagnostic & therapeutic); no Abx needed

3. Postpartum Psychiatric Disorders: Baby Blues vs. Postpartum Depression x2

Feature Postpartum Blues (“Baby Blues”) x1 Postpartum Depression x1
Onset & Duration Starts Day 2–3, peaks Day 5, and resolves spontaneously within <2 w (10–14 days) Onset within 4–8 w postpartum and persists for ≥2 w
Clinical Symptoms Mild tearfulness & lability; preserved joy & sleep Depressed mood, anhedonia, guilt, insomnia & weight loss
1st-Line Tx Reassurance, family support, and observation Psychotherapy (CBT) + SSRI antidepressants (Sertraline)
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص الجلطة الرئوية بعد الولادة القيصرية (سؤال Q1528):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: سيدة بعد 3 أيام من ولادة قيصرية أصيبت فجأة بألم حاد جنبي في الصدر (Pleuritic chest pain) وبصاق مدمم (Hemoptysis) وتسارع التنفس ونقص الأكسجين (SpO2 92%) بدون حرارة (37.1°C)، مع احتكاك جنبي وصورة صدر تظهر عتامة طرفية مثلثة (Hampton Hump). التشخيص المعتمد بلا منازع هو الانصمام الرئوي Pulmonary Embolism (Option A).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: اختار النموذج القديم خطأ Post-operative pneumonia (Option E)، علماً بأن غياب الحرارة والبداية الصاعقة المفاجئة وظهور علامة الـ Hampton Hump المميزة للاحتشاء الرئوي تنفي ذات الرئة وتؤكد الـ Pulmonary Embolism.
