Topic 1 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

1. Amenorrhea, Hyperprolactinemia, PCOS & Infertility

🔥 High Yield (40×) 31 Qs

1. Primary Amenorrhea: Müllerian Agenesis vs. Imperforate Hymen vs. Turner Syndrome 🔥 x5

Feature Müllerian Agenesis (MRKH) x2 Imperforate Hymen x1 Gonadal Dysgenesis (Turner 45,X) x2
Karyotype 46,XX (normal female) 46,XX (normal female) 45,X (monosomy X)
Hormonal Profile Normal female estradiol, FSH, LH, and testosterone Normal female estradiol, FSH, and LH Markedly elevated FSH & LH, low estradiol
Breasts & Pubic Hair Normal breasts & normal pubic/axillary hair Normal breasts & normal pubic/axillary hair Absent breast development (infantile sexual traits)
Uterus & Vaginal Exam Absent/rudimentary uterus & blind vaginal pouch Blood-filled uterus (hematocolpos) + bulging bluish hymen Streak ovaries with prepubertal uterus; webbed neck
Key Clinical Clue & Step Painless primary amenorrhea (vs. 46,XY AIS = no pubic hair) Cyclic monthly pelvic pain → cruciate hymenotomy 1st lab test if breasts absent: Serum FSH level

2. Secondary Amenorrhea: Asherman vs. Sheehan vs. Premature Ovarian Insufficiency 🔥 x7

Feature Asherman Syndrome x3 Sheehan Syndrome x1 Premature Ovarian Insufficiency (POI) x2
Primary Etiology Vigorous postpartum or post-abortion curettage (D&C) Ischemic anterior pituitary necrosis after severe PPH shock Autoimmune or idiopathic follicular depletion <40 years
Pathophysiology Scar tissue / intrauterine adhesions obliterate endometrial basalis Panhypopituitarism (↓ Prolactin, FSH, LH, TSH, ACTH) Primary ovarian failure (↓ estradiol, ↓ inhibin B)
Classic Clinical Clues Secondary amenorrhea or hypomenorrhea; normal ovulation Failure to lactate, amenorrhea, loss of pubic/axillary hair Amenorrhea + hot flashes & vaginal dryness before age 40
Diagnostic Workup Hysteroscopy (gold standard) or HSG filling defects Low pituitary trophic hormones + empty sella on pituitary MRI Elevated Serum FSH (>40 mIU/mL) (also confirms menopause)
1st-Line Tx Hysteroscopic adhesiolysis + estrogen therapy Lifelong hormone replacement (cortisol, thyroxine, sex steroids) Combined estrogen-progestin hormone therapy until age 50
  • First Mandatory Rule in ANY Reproductive-Age Secondary Amenorrhea: x1
    • Step #1: Always perform a urine or serum Pregnancy Test (β-hCG) first before ordering any hormonal or imaging workup!

3. Hyperprolactinemia, Galactorrhea & Polycystic Ovary Syndrome (PCOS) 🔥 x11

  • Hyperprolactinemia & Prolactinoma: 🔥 x5
    • Etiology & Drug Triggers:
      • Pituitary lactotroph adenoma, primary hypothyroidism (↑ TRH)
      • dopamine-blocking drugs (Phenothiazines / Antipsychotics, metoclopramide
      • SSRIs).
    • Pathophysiology & Presentation: High prolactin inhibits hypothalamic GnRH pulsatility
    • → secondary amenorrhea + bilateral milky galactorrhea (± headache and bitemporal hemianopia).
    • Diagnostic Imaging & Treatment: After excluding pregnancy, medications
    • hypothyroidism, order Pituitary MRI / Brain CT
    • 1st-line Tx for prolactinoma is a dopamine agonist (Cabergoline or Bromocriptine).
  • Polycystic Ovary Syndrome (PCOS) & Hirsutism: 🔥 x6
    • (1) Chronic oligo-/anovulation (oligomenorrhea)
    • (2)
      • Clinical/biochemical hyperandrogenism (male-pattern hirsutism on upper lip/chin/chest
      • acne, elevated free testosterone &
      • LH:FSH >2:1), and
    • (3) Polycystic ovaries on US.
    • 1st-Line Therapy (Not Seeking Pregnancy): Weight loss + COCs (regulates menses & ↓ androgens).
    • 1st-Line Ovulation Induction (Seeking Pregnancy): Letrozole or Clomiphene citrate.

4. Couple Infertility Evaluation & Tubal Factor Management 🔥 x7

  • Stepwise Infertility Workup: 🔥 x4
    • Definition & Timing: Failure to conceive after 12 m of regular unprotected intercourse (<35y)
    • If married only 3 m → reassure and wait until 1 year is completed.
    • Confirming Ovulation:
      • Mid-luteal (Day 21) serum progesterone or
      • endometrial biopsy demonstrating a secretory endometrium.
    • Tubal Patency Assessment: Once semen analysis and ovulation are normal, order Hysterosalpingography (HSG).
    • Severe Male Factor (Azoospermia): Perform Testicular Biopsy to distinguish obstructive from non-obstructive azoospermia.
  • Tubal Blockage, Hydrosalpinx & Pelvic Tuberculosis: x3
    • Post-Abortal / Post-PID Salpingitis (Q1797): Most common cause of secondary infertility is blocked tubes (bilateral tubal occlusion) due to post-infectious scarring and peritubal adhesions. x2
    • Bilateral Hydrosalpinx Before IVF: Hydrosalpinx fluid is embryotoxic and lowers IVF success by 50%
    • → perform laparoscopic salpingectomy or proximal tubal disconnection prior to IVF!
    • Pelvic / Tubal Tuberculosis: Secondary amenorrhea, infertility, chronic pelvic pain
    • low-grade fever with bilateral adnexal masses (antibiotic-resistant).
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص الفحوصات الأولية لتشخيص تكيس المبايض PCOS (سؤال Q1508):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: في سيدة تعاني من تأخر الإنجاب وعدم انتظام الدورة مع زيادة الوزن وشعرانية (Hirsutism) وحب شباب، الفحوصات المخبرية الأولية الإلزامية لاستبعاد الأسباب الأخرى المسببة لاضطراب التبويض هي قياس TSH و Prolactin (Option B) لنفي قصور الغدة الدرقية وفرط برولاكتين الدم.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم اختيار Option D (All of the four options)، مع مراعاة أن السونار المهبلي التلفزيوني فحص تصويري تركيبي مكمل وليس فحصاً مخبرياً أولياً.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 2 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

2. Abnormal Uterine Bleeding, Menopause & Endometrial Cancer

🔥 High Yield (38×) 29 Qs

1. Abnormal Uterine Bleeding (AUB): Anovulatory vs. Ovulatory Patterns 🔥 x8

Feature Anovulatory Bleeding (AUB-O / DUB) 🔥 x6 Ovulatory Menorrhagia / Mittelschmerz x2
Classic Age Groups Adolescents (1–3y post-menarche) & perimenopausal women (45–52y) Reproductive-age women with regular 28-day cycles
Pathophysiology Absent ovulation/corpus luteum → unopposed estrogen endometrial proliferation Intact corpus luteum; mid-cycle LH surge triggers follicular rupture
Clinical Bleeding Pattern Unpredictable, irregular, painless heavy bleeding after amenorrhea Regular heavy menses, or mid-cycle unilateral ovulatory pain & spotting (Mittelschmerz)
1st-Line Tx Adolescent: Combined Oral Contraceptives (COCs) + iron; Age ≥45y: Endometrial Biopsy! Refractory menorrhagia (completed family, failed drugs): Endometrial Ablation

2. Postmenopausal Bleeding (PMB) & Endometrial Hyperplasia/Carcinoma 🔥 x15

  • Diagnostic Rule in Postmenopausal or Perimenopausal (≥45y) Bleeding: 🔥 x11
    • Golden Rule:
      • Any vaginal bleeding occurring ≥12 m after menopause is Endometrial Carcinoma
      • → urgent gynecological evaluation!
    • Histological Gold Standard: Endometrial Biopsy (Pipelle) or Fractional Dilatation & Curettage (D&C).
    • TVUS Cutoff: Endometrial thickness ≤4 mm rules out cancer (>99% NPV); thickness >4–5 mm mandates endometrial biopsy.
    • Tamoxifen Therapy Surveillance x2:
      • Tamoxifen is a partial estrogen agonist on the endometrium (causes polyps, hyperplasia, and cancer)
      • → evaluate bleeding with pelvic US and endometrial biopsy.
  • Endometrial Hyperplasia, Cervical Stenosis & D&C Perforation: 🔥 x4
    • Risk Factors for Endometrial Hyperplasia/Cancer (Unopposed Estrogen):
      • Obesity
        • , nulliparity, late menopause, PCOS / chronic anovulation, unopposed estrogen HRT
        • diabetes.
    • Cervical Stenosis with Hematometra: Worsening cyclic dysmenorrhea, scant dark spotting
    • a tender globular uterus after cervical surgery/cryotherapy.
    • Uncomplicated Uterine Perforation During D&C:
      • Perforation by a blunt curette/sound in a stable patient without bleeding
      • → stop procedure immediately, withhold further curettage, and observe vital signs.

3. Genitourinary Syndrome of Menopause (Atrophic Vaginitis) & HRT 🔥 x6

  • Atrophic Vaginitis (Genitourinary Syndrome of Menopause): 🔥 x4
    • Clinical Presentation: Postmenopausal woman presenting with vaginal dryness, itching/burning, dyspareunia
    • pale, thin, friable mucosa with loss of rugae.
    • First-Line Treatment: Low-dose topical Vaginal Estrogen cream.
  • Systemic Hormone Replacement Therapy (HRT) Rule: x2
    • Intact Uterus: Must combine Estrogen + Progestin to protect against endometrial carcinoma.
    • Post-Hysterectomy (No Uterus): Prescribe systemic Estrogen-only therapy for vasomotor symptoms.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص نزيف ما بعد انقطاع الطمث Postmenopausal Bleeding (سؤال Q1687 وسؤال Q1756):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: أي نزيف مهبلي بعد انقطاع الطمث (عمر 55 عاماً) يُعتبر سرطان بطانة الرحم (Endometrial Cancer) حتى يثبت العكس. الخطوة الأولية غير الجراحية لتقييم سمك بطانة الرحم هي Transvaginal Ultrasound - TVUS (Option A)، وإذا كان السمك >4 mm تكون الخطوة التشخيصية المعيارية الذهبية هي خزعة بطانة الرحم / الكحت المجزأ Endometrial Biopsy / Fractional Curettage (Option D).

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: في السؤالين Q1687 و Q1688 تم اختيار Option D (Fractional curettage) كإجراء نسيجي حاسم، بينما في السؤال Q1756 تم اختيار Option A (Transvaginal ultrasound) كفحص مسحي مبدئي.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 3 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

3. Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyoma), Adenomyosis & Endometriosis

🔥 High Yield (35×) 25 Qs

1. Uterine Leiomyoma (Fibroids) vs. Adenomyosis vs. Endometriosis 🔥 x18

Feature Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyoma) 🔥 x10 Adenomyosis x2 Endometriosis 🔥 x6
Pathology Benign monoclonal smooth muscle tumor Endometrial glands & stroma deep inside myometrium Ectopic endometrial glands & stroma outside uterus
Classic Patient Profile Reproductive-age woman (30–45y) Multiparous woman in her 40s (40–50y) Nulligravid young woman (20–35y) with infertility
Clinical Triad / Clues Heavy menstrual bleeding (menorrhagia) (#1) & pelvic pressure Progressive secondary dysmenorrhea + menorrhagia Secondary dysmenorrhea + deep dyspareunia + infertility
Bimanual Pelvic Exam Enlarged, firm, irregular, nodular (“bosselated”) uterus Uniformly enlarged, symmetrical, globular, tender (“boggy”) uterus Fixed retroverted uterus, uterosacral nodularity, adnexal chocolate cyst
Diagnostic Gold Standard Pelvic US; Hysteroscopy for submucosal Transvaginal US or Pelvic MRI Diagnostic Laparoscopy showing powder-burn implants

2. Stepwise Management of Uterine Fibroids & Leiomyosarcoma 🔥 x11

  • Tailored Management of Uterine Fibroids by Symptoms & Fertility Desire: 🔥 x8
    • Asymptomatic Small/Stable Fibroid: Expectant management with yearly follow-up (no surgery or biopsy required).
    • Symptomatic Submucosal Fibroid or Infertility: Hysteroscopic Myomectomy.
    • Large Symptomatic Intramural/Subserosal Fibroid Desiring Fertility:
      • Abdominal or Laparoscopic Myomectomy (performed in postmenstrual proliferative phase).
    • Symptomatic Fibroids After Completing Childbearing: Hysterectomy is definitive
    • Medical options for bleeding/anemia include Tranexamic acid, progestins, GnRH agonists, and iron.
  • Fibroid Degeneration vs. Uterine Leiomyosarcoma: x3
    • Malignant Transformation Rule: Benign leiomyomas practically NEVER transform into leiomyosarcoma (<0.1%).
    • Uterine Leiomyosarcoma Warning Sign: Rapidly enlarging uterine mass in a postmenopausal woman with bleeding and pain
    • → laparotomy with total hysterectomy!
    • Acute Fibroid Degeneration: Sudden focal uterine pain, tenderness, and low-grade fever → conservative analgesia.

3. Primary Dysmenorrhea, Endometrioma Rupture & Ureteric Injury 🔥 x4

  • Primary Dysmenorrhea & Endometriosis Surgery: x3
    • Primary Dysmenorrhea:
      • Adolescent with crampy lower abdominal pain at menses onset (excess prostaglandin F2α) and normal exam
      • → 1st-line is NSAIDs (Ibuprofen).
    • Laparoscopic Goal in Endometriosis Infertility: Excision/ablation of visible endometriotic implants and adhesiolysis.
    • Ruptured Ovarian Endometrioma:
      • Known endometriosis with an adnexal cyst developing
      • sudden severe peritoneal pain and free pelvic fluid.
  • Intraoperative Ureteric Injury During Hysterectomy: x1
    • Anatomic Risk & Action: Ureter passes under the uterine artery
    • If transected → immediately call the urologist for intraoperative repair!
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

تنبيه هام للامتحان بخصوص العلاج الطبي الأولي لغزارة الطمث مع الأورام الليفية (سؤال Q1575):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: سيدة 40 عاماً تعاني من غزارة الطمث وفقر الدم بوجود ورم ليفي رحمي غير مسبب لأعراض ضاغطة كبيرة. العلاج الطبي الأولي غير الهرموني للسيطرة على النزف وعلاج فقر الدم هو Tranexamic acid and Iron (Option A) أثناء الدورة الشهرية.

في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة: تم وضع Option B (Medroxyprogesterone, tranexamic acid, and iron) كعلاج مركب لإيقاف النزف الحاد ودعم الطور الأصفري.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 4 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

4. Contraception (COCs, IUDs, Implants, DMPA & Emergency)

🔥 High Yield (42×) 36 Qs

1. Combined Oral Contraceptives (COCs) vs. Progestin-Only Methods 🔥 x18

Feature Combined Oral Contraceptives (COCs) 🔥 x9 Subdermal Implant (Nexplanon) 🔥 x4 Injectable DMPA (Depo-Provera) 🔥 x5
Primary Mechanism Suppresses GnRH/LH/FSH → inhibits ovulation Etonogestrel rod inhibits ovulation & thickens mucus (3–5y) IM Medroxyprogesterone every 3 m inhibits ovulation
Non-Contraceptive Benefits Reduces Ovarian & Endometrial cancer; treats dysmenorrhea & PCOS Most effective reversible contraceptive (<0.05% failure) Reduces heavy bleeding; safe in smokers ≥35y & obesity
Characteristic Side Effects Low-estrogen causes breakthrough bleeding (BTB); VTE & HTN Unpredictable irregular spotting (#1 reason for removal) Prolonged anovulation / delayed fertility return (up to 18m)
Absolute Contraindications Age ≥35y + Smoking, Migraine with Aura, Prior DVT/PE, Breastfeeding, Breast cancer Active breast cancer Active breast cancer

2. Intrauterine Contraceptive Devices: Copper IUD vs. Levonorgestrel IUD 🔥 x18

Feature Copper T380A IUD (ParaGard) 🔥 x9 Levonorgestrel IUD (Mirena / LNG-IUD) x3
Primary Mechanism Copper ions trigger spermicidal sterile inflammation → prevents fertilization Thickens cervical mucus + induces endometrial atrophy
Approved Duration Up to 10 years of continuous contraception 5 – 8 years
Effect on Menstrual Bleeding Increases menstrual blood loss & cramps (avoid in menorrhagia) Reduces menstrual blood loss by 80–90%; harmless spotting in first 3–6m
Key Clinical Indications #1 choice in Breast Cancer (hormone-free) & #1 Emergency Contraceptive (≤5d) Heavy menstrual bleeding, adenomyosis, & post-bariatric surgery
  • IUD Contraindications, Displacement, Infection & Perforation: 🔥 x6
    • Absolute Contraindication to IUD Insertion:
      • Current or recent Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID), mucopurulent cervicitis, or unexplained vaginal bleeding.
    • Displaced / Low-Lying IUD in Cervix on US: Remove the displaced IUD and insert a new one.
    • Post-Insertion Pelvic Infection: Mild endometritis at 6 w = combined outpatient antibiotic therapy
    • Severe PID unimproved on Abx = remove IUD and give IV Abx.
    • Uterine Perforation During IUD Insertion:
      • Sudden severe lower abdominal pain, loss of resistance during sounding, absent strings
      • intraperitoneal IUD on US → laparoscopic removal.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص آلية النزيف الرحمي بعد تركيب اللولب IUD (سؤال Q1585):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer والـ Model Answer: غزارة الطمث والنزف غير المنتظم بعد تركيب اللولب النحاسي ينجم مجهرياً عن اضطراب التوازن الموضعي للبروستاجلاندين والثرومبوكسان Unbalanced ratio of prostaglandins and thromboxane (Option B) في بطانة الرحم مما يسبب توسع الأوعية الدموية وهشاشتها. (تنبيه: نص السؤال الأصلي به خطأ مطبعي في العمر بذكر 64 عاماً بدلاً من عمر الإنجاب، وفي مريضة حقيقية بعمر 64 عاماً يجب استبعاد سرطان بطانة الرحم حتماً).

2. تنبيه بخصوص حبوب منع الحمل المركبة وتاريخ سرطان الثدي العائلي (سؤال Q1615):
وجود تاريخ عائلي لسرطان الثدي لدى القريبات من الدرجة الأولى يستدعي المتابعة والفحص الدوري المنتظم للثدي (Regular breast screening, Option C)، بينما في الـ Model Answer في بنك الأسئلة تم اختيار التحويل إلى وسيلة غير هرمونية Shift to another non-hormonal method (Option D) كإجراء احترازي وقائي.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 5 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

5. Vaginitis, Cervicitis, Vulvar Lesions, Bartholin Gland & PID

🔥 High Yield (48×) 43 Qs

1. Infectious Vaginitis Comparison: Bacterial Vaginosis vs. Trichomoniasis vs. Candidiasis 🔥 x16

Feature Bacterial Vaginosis (BV) 🔥 x5 Trichomoniasis x3 Vulvovaginal Candidiasis 🔥 x8
Causative Pathogen Gardnerella vaginalis (loss of Lactobacilli) Trichomonas vaginalis (flagellated protozoan STI) Candida albicans (yeast; DM, Abx, pregnancy)
Discharge & Symptoms Thin, homogeneous, grayish-white discharge with “fishy” odor Copious, yellow-green frothy discharge + “strawberry cervix” Intense vulvar pruritus & thick white “cottage-cheese” discharge
Vaginal pH Elevated: pH > 4.5 Elevated: pH > 4.5 (5.0–6.0) Normal acidic pH (4.0 – 4.5)
Microscopy / Wet Mount Pathognomonic “Clue cells” + positive 10% KOH whiff test Motile flagellated trophozoites + abundant PMNs Budding yeast and pseudohyphae on 10% KOH mount
First-Line Treatment Oral or vaginal Metronidazole (or Clindamycin) Oral Metronidazole (treat BOTH patient & partner!) Topical azole or Oral Fluconazole; if recurrent (≥4/yr): Screen for Diabetes

2. Mucopurulent Cervicitis, Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID) & TOA 🔥 x13

  • Mucopurulent Cervicitis (Chlamydia & Gonorrhea): 🔥 x4
    • Clinical Clues: Purulent endocervical discharge, friable cervix, intermenstrual/postcoital bleeding, and dysuria
    • → Chlamydia trachomatis (#1) and Neisseria gonorrhoeae.
    • Culture Media: N. gonorrhoeae grows on Thayer-Martin chocolate agar
    • C. trachomatis grows on McCoy tissue culture cells.
    • Uncomplicated Gonococcal Cervicitis Treatment: Single-dose IM Ceftriaxone.
  • Acute Pelvic Inflammatory Disease (PID) & Tubo-Ovarian Abscess (TOA): 🔥 x9
    • Diagnostic Triad:
      • Acute lower abdominal pain
      • fever
      • purulent discharge with cervical motion tenderness and adnexal tenderness.
    • Tubo-Ovarian Abscess (TOA): Severe PID complicated by high spiking fever and a tender fluctuant adnexal mass on exam/US.
    • Indications for Hospital Admission & IV Abx:
      • High fever/toxicity, vomiting/inability to take oral drugs, pregnancy, or Tubo-Ovarian Abscess.

3. Bartholin Gland Cyst vs. Bartholin Gland Abscess 🔥 x9

Feature Asymptomatic Bartholin Duct Cyst x1 Acute Bartholin Gland Abscess 🔥 x8
Anatomic Location Posterolateral lower vestibule at 4 or 8 o’clock position Posterolateral labium majus / vestibule at 4 or 8 o’clock position
Clinical Presentation Small, painless, non-tender cystic swelling noticed incidentally Acute, excruciatingly painful, erythematous, warm, fluctuant mass ± fever
1st-Line Tx Small (<2–3 cm) & asymptomatic: Observation; large/recurrent: Marsupialization Incision and drainage with Word catheter placement OR Marsupialization

4. Pediatric Vaginal Foreign Body, Retained Tampon & Genital Ulcers 🔥 x5

  • Foul-Smelling Vaginal Discharge in Children vs. Young Adults: x3
    • Prepubertal Girl (3–6 Years Old) with Foul, Blood-Tinged Discharge x2:
      • Most common cause is an intravaginal foreign body (toilet paper or small toy).
    • Young Woman with Foul Discharge After Menses: Speculum exam reveals a retained forgotten tampon → remove immediately.
  • Genital Herpes, Contact Vulvitis & Postcoital UTI Prophylaxis: x2
    • Genital Herpes Simplex Virus (HSV):
      • Multiple painful grouped vesicles/ulcers on an erythematous base + tender inguinal nodes
      • → oral Acyclovir / Valacyclovir.
    • Allergic / Irritant Contact Vulvitis:
      • Acute vulvar itching and erythema with normal pH after using scented hygiene products.
    • Preventing Postcoital Recurrent UTIs: Advise voiding immediately after sexual intercourse.
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص الخلايا الدليلية Clue Cells والتهاب المهبل البكتيري (سؤال Q1733):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: وجود الـ Clue cells (خلايا طلائية مهبلية مغطاة بكثافة ببكتيريا تفقدهم حدودهم الواضحة) هو العلامة المشخصة الحصرية لـ التهاب المهبل البكتيري Bacterial Vaginosis (Option A) ببكتيريا Gardnerella vaginalis (كما تم تأكيده في Q1648 و Q1731 و Q1732). وفي الـ Model Answer في Q1733 تم اختيار Option D (Chlamydiasis) بالخطأ.

2. تنبيه بخصوص العلاج المتزامن للعدوى مع السيلان (سؤال Q1742):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: في مريضة مصابة بعدوى السيلان (Neisseria gonorrhoeae)، العدوى المتزامنة الأكثر شيوعاً التي تتطلب علاجاً تجريبياً مشتركاً إجبارياً هي Chlamydia trachomatis (Option B) (باستخدام Doxycycline أو Azithromycin). بينما الـ Model Answer اختار Trichomoniasis (Option E).

3. تنبيه بخصوص كيس بارثولين الصغير غير المؤلم (سؤال Q1802):
علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: كيس بارثولين صغير بحجم 2 سم غير مؤلم ولا ملتهب (Asymptomatic non-tender Bartholin cyst) في شابة، الإجراء الطبي المعتمد هو المراقبة والطمأنة Observation and reassurance (Option C). بينما الـ Model Answer اختار إجراء Marsupialization (Option A)، علماً بأن الجراحة مخصصة للأكياس الكبيرة المزعجة أو المتكررة أو الخراج الملتهب.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 6 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

6. Cervical Screening (Pap Smear, HPV, CIN), Cervical & Vulvar Cancer

🔥 High Yield (32×) 22 Qs

1. HPV Oncogenesis, Cervical Screening & Management of Abnormal Pap Smears 🔥 x16

  • Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Subtypes & Screening Rules: 🔥 x6
    • Low-Risk HPV (Types 6 & 11): Cause benign exophytic, cauliflower-like anogenital warts (Condylomata acuminata).
    • High-Risk Oncogenic HPV (Types 16 & 18): Cause >70% of cervical cancers
    • HPV Type 16 is the single most carcinogenic subtype.
    • Progression Timeline: Cervical Carcinoma in Situ (CIN 3) precedes invasive carcinoma by an average of 10 years.
    • Routine Cervical Screening: Start at age 21 with Liquid-based cervical cytology every 3 years
    • After total hysterectomy for benign fibroids, Pap smears are no longer indicated!
  • Stepwise Management of Abnormal Pap Smear & Postcoital Bleeding: 🔥 x10
    • ASC-US (HPV+), LSIL, or HSIL on Pap Smear: Refer for Colposcopy!
    • Abnormal Colposcopic Acetowhite Lesion / Mosaicism:
      • Perform a Colposcopy-directed cervical punch biopsy for histopathology.
    • Postcoital Bleeding with Normal-Looking Cervix: Refer for Colposcopy (after excluding Chlamydia).
    • Grossly Visible Ulcerated / Friable Cervical Mass:
      • Offensive bloody discharge and postcoital bleeding with a visible cervical mass
      • → perform direct punch/excisional biopsy of the cervical lesion!

2. Vulvar Lichen Sclerosus vs. Vulvar Squamous Cell Carcinoma 🔥 x6

Feature Vulvar Lichen Sclerosus x2 Vulvar Squamous Cell Carcinoma 🔥 x4
Patient Profile & Risk Postmenopausal women (55–80y); autoimmune dermal sclerosis Elderly women (65–80y); arises from chronic Lichen sclerosus or HPV
Classic Vulvar Appearance Porcelain-white, thin, wrinkled (“cigarette-paper”) skin with labial fusion Firm, raised, ulcerated, leukoplakic, or exophytic labia majora mass
Diagnostic Gold Standard Clinical diagnosis + punch biopsy to exclude dysplasia Mandatory vulvar punch biopsy of any suspicious lesion
Lymphatic Drainage & Therapy High-potency topical corticosteroid (Clobetasol propionate) Drains FIRST to Superficial Inguinal Lymph Nodes; radical excision
⚠️ Model Answer vs. AI Recommended Answer

1. تنبيه بخصوص نتيجة الـ HSIL في مسحة عنق الرحم (سؤال Q1555):

علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: سيدة 35 عاماً أظهرت مسحة عنق الرحم وجود آفة حرشفية داخل الظهارة عالية الدرجة (HSIL). الخطوة التشخيصية الإلزامية التالية هي التنظير المهبلي مع أخذ خزعة موجهة Colposcopy-directed biopsy (Option B) (كما تم تأكيده في السؤال المماثل Q1727). بينما اختار الـ Model Answer إجراء Cervical conization (Option D)، وهو إجراء استئصالي علاجي لا يُجرى إلا بعد تأكيد الخزعة أو عندما يكون التنظير غير كافٍ.

2. تنبيه بخصوص المسح الدوري لعنق الرحم بعمر 25 عاماً (سؤال Q1627):
علمياً ووفقاً للـ AI Recommended Answer: وفقاً للإرشادات الدولية الحديثة (ASCCP/USPSTF)، المسح الدوري للنساء من عمر 21 إلى 29 عاماً يتم عبر فحص مسحة عنق الرحم السائلة كل 3 سنوات Liquid-based cervical cytology every 3 years (Option D). بينما اختار الـ Model Answer إجراء Pap smear every year (Option A) وفقاً للنظم السنوية القديمة.

↑ Back to Top / جدول المحتويات
Topic 7 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

7. Adnexal Masses, Ovarian Cysts, Ovarian Torsion & Ovarian Cancer

🔥 High Yield (26×) 15 Qs

1. Acute Adnexal Emergencies: Ovarian Torsion vs. Ruptured Ovarian Cyst 🔥 x11

Feature Adnexal / Ovarian Torsion 🔥 x7 Ruptured Functional Ovarian Cyst 🔥 x4
Pathophysiology & Trigger Ovary/mass (≥5 cm) twists on infundibulopelvic ligament, occluding blood flow Follicular or corpus luteum cyst ruptures during exercise (tennis) or intercourse
Clinical Presentation Sudden, severe unilateral lower abdominal pain + nausea/vomiting & tender adnexal mass Sudden sharp unilateral pelvic pain with mild peritoneal irritation & negative β-hCG
First-Line Imaging Pelvic / Transvaginal US with Color Doppler (enlarged edematous ovary, ↓ flow) Transvaginal US showing small amount of free fluid in pouch of Douglas
1st-Line Tx Urgent diagnostic laparoscopy with detorsion & ovarian cystectomy Hemodynamically stable: Conservative management (analgesia, rest, & observation)

2. Epithelial Ovarian Carcinoma & Postmenopausal Adnexal Masses 🔥 x4

  • Epithelial Ovarian Cancer Presentation, Tumor Marker & Screening: x3
    • Classic Presentation:
      • Postmenopausal woman (55–75y) with vague abdominal bloating, early satiety, pelvic pain, ascites
      • a fixed solid/complex adnexal mass.
    • Primary Serum Tumor Marker: CA-125 is used to evaluate postmenopausal adnexal masses and monitor therapeutic response.
    • Asymptomatic Family History Screening Rule:
      • There is NO valid routine screening test for ovarian cancer in asymptomatic average-risk women!
  • Persistent Postmenopausal Ovarian Cyst: x1
    • Management:
      • A 5-cm ovarian cyst in a 75-yo postmenopausal woman that fails
      • to resolve after 1 year of follow-up warrants laparoscopic bilateral salpingo-oophorectomy.

Topic 8 of 8 • Obstetrics & Gynecology • Gynecology

8. Pelvic Organ Prolapse & Urinary Incontinence

🔥 High Yield (20×) 6 Qs

1. Pelvic Organ Prolapse & Stress Urinary Incontinence 🔥 x6

Feature Anterior Wall: Cystocele / Urethrocele x2 Posterior Wall: Rectocele x1 Apical: Uterovaginal Prolapse x3
Anatomic Defect & Organ Pubocervical fascia defect → bladder & urethra herniate into anterior vagina Rectovaginal fascia defect → rectum herniates into posterior vagina Cardinal & uterosacral ligament weakness → descent of uterus & cervix
Classic Clinical Symptoms Stress Urinary Incontinence (SUI): leakage on coughing/sneezing/lifting Constipation, incomplete bowel emptying, & need for digital vaginal splinting to defecate Pelvic heaviness, dragging sensation, & protrusion of cervix/vaginal bulge
1st-Line Conservative Care Pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises (Grade 1) & weight loss Pelvic floor muscle (Kegel) exercises & high-fiber diet Vaginal ring pessary (1st-line in elderly or poor surgical candidates)
Definitive Surgical Repair Anterior colporrhaphy & mid-urethral sling Posterior colporrhaphy Vaginal hysterectomy with apical suspension (remove neglected ulcerated pessary first!)

1. BLS/PALS Resuscitation, Choking, Drowning & Shock States

🔥 High Yield (8×) 8 Qs

1. BLS / PALS Resuscitation, Choking & Drowning Arrest 🔥 x7

Feature Late Pregnancy Choking x1 Choking Child Failing ETT x2 Drowning Asystole Arrest 🔥 x4
Scenario 39w pregnant woman choking on steak Unconscious, cyanotic 5-yo; ETT blocked by toy Submerged 4m → GCS 3, pulseless asystole
Contraindicated Abdominal Heimlich thrusts & blind sweep Blind finger sweep (pushes object deeper) Defibrillation (non-shockable) or stopping CPR
Immediate 1st Step Mid-sternal chest thrusts Needle cricothyroidotomy Continue CPR + IV Epinephrine
  • Out-of-Hospital BLS & Pediatric Bradycardia Rules: 🔥 x7
    • Collapsed Unresponsive Victim (Out-of-Hospital): x3 After checking responsiveness → Call for help / activate EMS before compressions.
    • Child Responding to Pain with Bradycardia & Poor Perfusion: 🔥 x4 1st step → maintain airway & assisted breathing (hypoxia is #1 cause).

2. Shock States Comparison (Neurogenic vs. Hypovolemic vs. Septic) 🔥 x8

Feature Neurogenic Shock x3 Hypovolemic Shock x3 Septic Shock x2
Primary Cause Spinal cord trauma (loss of sympathetic tone) Severe persistent diarrhea & vomiting (4d) Systemic infection + bilateral ARDS infiltrates
Heart Rate & BP Bradycardia (HR 46/min) + BP 80/48 Tachycardia (HR 128/min) + BP 88/52 Tachycardia + BP 80/45
Skin & Temp Warm, dry extremities Cold, clammy, dehydrated Fever 39.5°C + disturbed consciousness

Topic 2 of 3 • Miscellaneous • Emergency

2. Thermal Burns: Depth Classification, Rule of Nines & Parkland Resuscitation

🔥 High Yield (10×) 10 Qs

1. Thermal Burn Depth Classification 🔥 x10

Feature First-Degree (Superficial) x1 Second-Degree (Partial-Thickness) 🔥 x9 Third-Degree (Full-Thickness)
Layers Involved Epidermis only (sunburn) Epidermis + dermis (hot iron / scald) Entire dermis destroyed
Skin Appearance Dry erythema, no blisters Weeping clear fluid blisters, pink/red base Leathery, charred, waxy-white eschar
Pain / Sensation Exquisitely tender Severely painful & tender Painless / insensate
Management Observation (excluded from Parkland TBSA%) Dressings (counted in Parkland TBSA%) Parkland fluids, escharotomy, excision & grafting

2. Wallace Rule of Nines & Parkland Fluid Resuscitation 🔥 x17

  • Adult Rule of Nines (Count 2nd & 3rd Degree Only): 🔥 x4
    • Regions: Head/neck = 9% (face/anterior neck = 4.5%) • Anterior trunk = 18% • Posterior trunk = 18% • Each arm = 9% • Each leg = 18%.
    • Anterior Chest + Abdomen (18%) + Both Arms (18%): x1 Total = 36% TBSA (0.36).
    • Face/Anterior Neck + Anterior Trunk (18%) + Both Arms (18%): x3 Total = 40% (0.4) or 45% (0.45).
  • Parkland Formula (Ringer’s Lactate): 🔥 x13
    • 24-Hour Total: 4 mL × kg × %TBSA → give half (50%) in first 8 hours (= 2 mL × kg × %TBSA).
    • 80 kg with 50% Burn (First 8h): 🔥 x4 2 × 80 × 50 = 8,000 mL (8 L).
    • 70 kg with 40% Burn (First 8h): x2 2 × 70 × 40 = 5,600 mL.
    • 80 kg with 40% Burn (First 8h): x1 2 × 80 × 40 = 6,400 mL.
    • 70 kg with 14% Steam Burn (<5% Full-Thickness): x3 1–2 L Ringer’s lactate in 8 hours.
    • Best Clinical Monitor of Burn Fluid Adequacy: x3 Hourly urine output (target 0.5–1 mL/kg/hr).
⚠️ QBank Excel-Format Alert: In burn TBSA questions, Excel converted percentages (36%, 40%, 45%) into decimals (0.36, 0.4, 0.45) — select the matching decimal option.

3. Clinical Toxicology & Poisoning Antidotes

🔥 High Yield (7×) 7 Qs

1. Clinical Toxicology & Poisoning Antidotes 🔥 x20

Poison / Overdose Key Clinical & Lab Clues Critical Monitoring / Rule Definitive Antidote / 1st Step
Paracetamol (<4h) x2 Asymptomatic toddler 2h after liquid ingestion Draw serum level at 4 hours post-ingestion Plot on Rumack-Matthew nomogram
Paracetamol (≥8–12h) x3 Adult 12h after 34 tablets (17 g); centrilobular necrosis Do NOT delay treatment waiting for serum level Give N-Acetylcysteine (NAC) immediately
Organophosphates x2 AChE inhibition → bronchorrhea, miosis, bradycardia Discontinue/titrate atropine when secretions stop Atropine + Pralidoxime (2-PAM)
Salicylate (Aspirin) x1 Vomiting, lethargy, tachypnea & tachycardia Mixed respiratory alkalosis + metabolic acidosis (pH 7.5, PaCO2 20, HCO3− 20) Charcoal + IV sodium bicarbonate
Barbiturates / Opioids 🔥 x11 Unattended child in acute coma (GCS 6) Pinpoint (miotic) pupils + respiratory depression Secure airway; Naloxone / ICU support
Household Bleach x1 Pediatric caustic alkali ingestion Contraindicated: emesis, ipecac, lavage & charcoal Do NOT induce vomiting; give water or milk

1. Hand Hygiene, PPE, Isolation Precautions & Nosocomial Infection Control

🔥 High Yield (21×) 21 Qs

1. Hand Hygiene Rules: Soap & Water vs. Alcohol Hand Rub 🔥 x11

Feature Soap and Water Handwashing x3 Alcohol-Based Hand Rub 🔥 x4
Indications Visibly soiled hands (blood/fluids), C. difficile, or Norovirus Routine antisepsis when hands are not visibly soiled
Mechanism Friction washes away spores & non-enveloped viruses Denatures proteins; fails against spores & Norovirus
Core Rule #1 measure to stop C. difficile & Norovirus outbreaks #1 overall measure to prevent hospital-acquired infections (HAIs)
  • WHO Five Moments & Glove Discipline: 🔥 x4
    • WHO 5 Moments: x2 Before patient contact • Before aseptic task (IV cannula) • After fluid risk • After patient contact • After surroundings.
    • Blood Draw on Coughing Febrile Patient: x1 Perform hand hygiene and wear clean gloves.
    • Not Changing Gloves Between Patients (Even If Sanitized): x1 Causes cross-contamination between patients.
⚠️ QBank Key Attention: Both “Before performing an aseptic task” (Option C) and “After contact with a patient” (Option E) are valid WHO Hand Hygiene Moments; the Model Answer selects Option E in one version and Option C in its twin.

2. Isolation Precautions, PPE Sequence & Device Bundles 🔥 x9

Feature Contact Precautions x1 Airborne Precautions (Ward) x3 Outpatient Clinic TB Triage x1
Pathogens MRSA, VRE, C. difficile, Norovirus Tuberculosis (TB), Chickenpox (Varicella), Measles Suspected pulmonary TB coughing in waiting room
Room Rule Single room or cohort isolation Segregate in negative-pressure single room (closed door) Isolate patient immediately from waiting room
Mask / PPE Gown and gloves for all staff entering Staff wear fit-tested N95 respirator mask Place surgical mask on the patient (source control)
  • PPE Order, Needlestick Injury & ICU Device Infections: 🔥 x4
    • Correct PPE Donning Order: x1 Gown → Mask → Face shield/Goggles → Gloves.
    • 1st Step After Contaminated Needlestick Injury: x1 Wash wound with soap and water for 15 minutes (never use bleach).
    • Coughing ICU Visitor Refusing a Mask: x1 Offer a mask and explain its importance.
    • Preventing CAUTI, CLABSI & VAP Clusters: x3 Reinforce strict hand hygiene before & after patient/catheter contact.

2. Surgical Site Infection (SSI) Prevention, OR Sterility & Retained Sponge Protocol

🔥 High Yield (10×) 10 Qs

1. Surgical Site Infection (SSI) Prevention & Wound Closure 🔥 x7

Feature Elective Cesarean Section 🔥 x4 Dirty Perforated Ulcer Laparotomy x1
#1 Microbial Source Patient’s own endogenous skin/body flora Gross peritoneal spillage (1.5 L turbid fluid)
Antibiotic & Prep Prophylactic IV antibiotics BEFORE skin incision + theatre wear Therapeutic IV antibiotics + peritoneal lavage
Skin Closure Rule Subcuticular closure; never shave hair with razors Leave skin wound open for local wound care
⚠️ QBank Key Attention: When “Prophylactic antibiotics before skin incision” is listed, it is always the #1 choice; in the version where it is omitted from options, the Model Answer selects “Using separate surgical knives for skin and deeper tissues” (Option C).

2. Operating Room Sterility & Missing Surgical Towel Protocol x3

  • Intraoperative Sterility, Missing Towel & Gallstone Imaging: x3
    • Breach in Sterile Technique During Surgery: x1 Discard contaminated instruments immediately and replace them.
    • Sudden Spike in Outpatient Surgical Site Infections: x1 Audit instrument sterilization techniques.
    • Missing Towel at Closure with Negative Search & Negative X-Ray: x1 Notify OR head nurse, keep X-ray copy, close & file incident report.
    • Best Initial Imaging for Suspected Gallstones (RUQ Pain): x1 Abdominal ultrasound.

3. Core Bioethics Principles: Autonomy, Beneficence, Non-Maleficence & Double Effect

🔥 High Yield (16×) 16 Qs

1. Core Bioethics Principles 🔥 x16

Ethical Principle Core Definition High-Yield EMLE Scenario Mandatory Physician Action
Autonomy (Treatment) 🔥 x5 Competent patient’s self-determination Competent patient refusing surgery, chemo, or terminal care Respect patient’s refusal (family cannot override)
Autonomy (Blood) x3 Religious refusal of blood by alert adult Jehovah’s Witness refusing blood transfusion Respect refusal & give non-blood supportive care
Advance Directive x1 Prior signed refusal when incapacitated Shocked PPH patient with signed blood-refusal directive Follow the patient’s advance directive
Beneficence x3 Acting in patient’s best interest to save life Unconscious pregnant emergency (ectopic / arrest / PPH) Proceed with life-saving emergency surgery
Non-Maleficence x1 Duty to do no harm / prevent harm Disclosing data to prevent harm to third parties Guided by Non-Maleficence
Fidelity x1 Keeping promises & loyalty to patient Keeping promise to ICU patient not to tell family Uphold promise under Fidelity
Double Effect x1 Good intent permissible despite foreseen risk Terminal cancer morphine bolus causing respiratory arrest Permissible under Doctrine of Double Effect
  • Maternal-Fetal Refusal Dilemma: x1
    • Term Placenta Previa Refusing Cesarean Section: Convene the hospital ethics committee (do not force surgery).

4. Informed Consent, Confidentiality, Truth-Telling & Clinical Communication

🔥 High Yield (19×) 19 Qs

1. Informed Consent & Pediatric Parental Consent 🔥 x4

  • Adult & Pediatric Consent Rules: 🔥 x4
    • Explaining Procedure Risks & Benefits to Patient: x1 Fulfills Informed Consent.
    • New Study Showing Superior Technique Night Before Surgery: x1 Disclose new evidence and surgeon’s limited experience.
    • 14-Year-Old Boy Brought by Neighbor for Scalp Suturing: x1 One parent must give consent.
    • Stable 7-Year-Old Boy with Pneumonia Refusing Admission: x1 Discuss risks of untreated illness with the father.

2. Medical Confidentiality & Legal Exceptions 🔥 x9

Scenario Core Principle Correct Physician Action
Spouse / Family Asking for Results 🔥 x4 Confidentiality (keeping data private) Maintain confidentiality & counsel patient on disclosure
Student Viewing EHR / Social Party x2 Confidentiality breach Do not access non-assigned charts; do not acknowledge patient at party
Teenager Consulting for STI x1 Adolescent STI privacy Respect teenager’s confidentiality and reassure her
Husband with Contagious Reportable STI x1 Partner protection Encourage patient to inform his wife & offer to facilitate
Mandatory Disclosure of Secrecy x1 Judicial override Disclose professional secret if ordered by the court

3. Breaking Bad News & Clinical Communication 🔥 x6

Feature Newly Diagnosed Lung Cancer x1 Family Begging to Hide Colon Cancer x1
Who to Inform Explain diagnosis & prognosis to the patient himself Ask the patient how he wants information handled
  • Interviewing & Patient Counseling Techniques: 🔥 x4
    • Tense 16-Year-Old Girl Avoiding Eye Contact: x1 Start with a general open-ended question on how to help her.
    • Acutely Dyspneic Patient Leaning Forward: x1 Use focused closed-ended questions.
    • Diabetic Refusing Flu Shot (“Vaccine Causes Flu”): x1 Explain that incubating viral illness is mistaken for vaccine flu.
    • Explaining “Take Pill Once Daily With Water”: x1 Communication goal is giving instructions to patients.

5. Medical Law (Battery vs. Malpractice), Colleague Impairment & Conflict of Interest

🔥 High Yield (9×) 9 Qs

1. Medical Tort Law: Battery vs. Malpractice x3

Feature Medical Battery x1 Malpractice / Res Ipsa Loquitur x1 Off-Duty Crash Witness x1
Definition Touching / operating against explicit refusal Unsanitary OR causing post-op infection Resident after 30-h shift passes crash with bystanders
Legal Rule Forceps after refusal = Battery Medically Malpractice (B); Key marks Res ipsa loquitur (D) No pre-existing duty → no legal action taken
⚠️ QBank Key Attention: For post-operative infection from unsanitary OR conditions, the medically/legally verified concept is Malpractice (Option B), whereas the Model Answer marks Res ipsa loquitur (Option D).

2. Errors, Impaired Colleagues & Conflict of Interest 🔥 x6

  • Professional Integrity & Ethics Rules: 🔥 x6
    • Wrong Medication Dose Administered by Nurse: x1 Document error in chart & inform attending physician immediately.
    • Resident Asks Student to Falsify Vital Signs: x1 Refuse and report the resident to superiors.
    • Colleague Appearing Intoxicated at Work: x1 Report behavior to hospital administration immediately.
    • Pharma Gifts or Trial Enrollment Financial Incentives: x2 Creates an unethical Conflict of Interest.
    • Accepted Physician–Physician Professional Courtesy: x1 Treating colleagues without fees (never split referral/lab fees).

6. Healthcare Quality Improvement (TQM, Lean Six Sigma, RCA), EHR & Public Health

🔥 High Yield (17×) 17 Qs

1. Quality Improvement Frameworks (TQM, Lean Six Sigma, RCA) 🔥 x7

Methodology Core Mechanism High-Yield EMLE Scenario
Total Quality Management (TQM) x1 Continuous quality improvement culture More attentive staff & thorough diabetic clinic visits
Lean Six Sigma x1 Streamlines processes & eliminates defects Fixing lost lab results & lab-to-clinic delays
Root Cause Analysis (RCA) x1 Retrospective system analysis after adverse event Primary action after patient slips and falls in corridor
Workflow Flow Analysis x1 Identify scheduling bottlenecks first Long clinic wait times → analyze scheduling & patient flow
Targeted Outreach Analysis x1 Close remaining gap to national benchmark Vaccination rose 65% → 78% (goal 80%) → target remaining unimmunized
Double-Check System x1 Cross-device verification Measuring BP multiple times = double-check for accuracy

2. Hospital Safety, Readmissions, EHR & Public Health 🔥 x10

  • Medication Safety & Care Redesign: 🔥 x4
    • Violates Hospital Medication Safety Goals: x1 Container label showing only drug name and strength.
    • High 30-Day Heart Failure Readmission Rate: x1 Implement standardized discharge checklist & follow-up calls.
    • Frequent Non-Urgent ED Visits by Chronic Patients: x1 Implement primary care coordination program.
    • Patients Feeling Rushed During Clinic Visits: x1 Switch to team-based care with medical assistants taking initial history.
  • EHR Implementation, Rural Health & Mandatory Reporting: 🔥 x6
    • EHR Access Delays During Rollout: x1 Collect staff feedback to optimize EHR configuration.
    • Nurses Reporting Slow EHR & Documentation Errors: x1 Mandate staff training on the EHR system.
    • Patients Confused by New Clinic Portal: x1 Host training sessions for patients.
    • Rural Clinic Wait Times & Physician Shortage: x1 Implement telemedicine consultations.
    • Addressing Rural Diabetes Social Determinants: x1 Invest in a Community Health Worker (CHW) program.
    • Confirmed Active Pulmonary TB in Clinic: x1 Document in chart & report to local public health department.
    • Hospital Water Positive for Legionella: x1 Notify public health authorities & restrict water use.
